   isn't there.

2. (READ-ONLY) TASK 1/2'S REAL PROBLEM IS PROVENANCE DRIFT, NOT "IS IT
   VERIFIED" — AND IT'S A LEGITIMATE GAP. verify_dataset.py never reads
   dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl (grep confirms — only dpo_pairs.jsonl and
   repair_pairs.jsonl appear in FILES). build_training_set.py:42-63 samples
   the capped file straight from dpo_pairs.jsonl's rows unmodified — no

   WAS covered by the last verify_dataset.py pass over its source, IF that
   pass is still current. The actual hole: nothing on disk proves
   dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl wasn't hand-edited, regenerated with a different
   seed, or built from a dpo_pairs.jsonl that verify_dataset.py never re-ran
   against, after the fact. That's a real, well-scoped, worth-closing problem
   — the ask's underlying instinct is right even though its stated premise
   (Task 1/3's "TRL/README" framing) isn't.

3. (READ-ONLY, adopt-don't-build) THE EXACT HASH ALREADY EXISTS IN THIS REPO,
   UNWIRED. build_training_set.py:74-76 computes `sha256(body)` over the exact
   capped-file bytes and prints it with the comment "Put N and this sha256 in
   the prereg; train run 1 from THIS file only." That is the identical
   artifact Task 1 Part A asks to newly invent — it's a manually-copied
   process promise (human pastes it into a prereg doc) rather than a
   mechanically-enforced one, which is precisely the promise-vs-gate
   distinction this file's own OPERATING PROTOCOL names for red witnesses.
   The design work is wiring this existing hash into an automatic write/check,
   not building a new receipt scheme from nothing.

4. (READ-ONLY, inherited audit note) BROADER PATTERN, NOT WORTH ADOPTING A
   LIBRARY FOR: "hash the input, refuse to run on mismatch" is the same shape
   as pip's `--require-hashes`, npm's package-lock integrity field, and
   Terraform's `.terraform.lock.hcl`. None are installable here without adding
   a dependency, which cuts against this project's stated zero-dependency
   stance (localllm is deliberately torch-only/no-base). Hand-rolling the
   stdlib hashlib/json version is the right call for this repo specifically —
   noting this so the executor doesn't spend time evaluating those tools.

5. (READ-ONLY, design risk — not yet tested against any input) THE PROPOSED
   RECEIPT SHAPE HAS A SELF-ISSUE GAP. A receipt is only meaningful if
   verify_dataset.py is the ONLY code path that can write dpo_receipt.json,
   and it refuses to write one when violations > 0. Worth stating explicitly
   in the executor's design rather than leaving it implicit. This project's
   own standing rule (a guard needs a red witness) applies here directly —
   which is exactly what the ask's own Task 2 already requests (tamper one
   byte, confirm the RuntimeError). That test, run for real with a pasted
   traceback, is the right bar and should happen regardless of how Task 1/3
   get resolved.

6. (READ-ONLY, cross-reference for the executor, not a ruling) This proposed
   gate is itself a new guard, landing on a channel where the LIVE
   unanswered DIRECTIVE (top of this file, activation #13) asks the PhD to
   find the general form behind three guards whose own threshold or scope was
   invented and passed while the thing they guarded broke. A byte-equality
   hash check has no eyeballed threshold (unlike the leakage 0.20/0.05 or
   split_health's 0.5) — its failure mode, per finding 5, is a SCOPE question
   (who may write the receipt), the same shape as invented-guard example #3
   (dangling-reference check scoped to quoted-only). If the executor builds
   this and gets the scope right, it's a real data point for that DIRECTIVE's
   Q1 — worth writing up as one when it ships, not asserting now.

THE DATA GATE — "verified before training" is now a mechanism, not a habit.
The claim was true as process and false as machinery: verify_dataset.py never
read the file training loads, and neither trainer called it. New dataset_gate.py
is one chokepoint; verify_dataset.py writes a receipt carrying each verified
file's sha256; train_native.py AND train_dpo.py both refuse to start unless a
receipt covers their exact input bytes with zero violations. No bypass flag.
  RED 1  pre-gate receipt      -> "receipt schema is None, this code needs 2", exit 1
  RED 2  tampered capped file  -> "CONTENT CHANGED since verification", verified
         85fc0bdc... vs on-disk 80cca364..., exit 1 (file restored byte-identical)
  RED 3  chosen/rejected swapped on a real pair ->
         "chosen_does_not_pass,rejected_incorrectly_passes"; a receipt recording
         violations>0 -> REFUSED
  GREEN  clean state -> "[gate] verified receipt OK - dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl
         (918 pairs); 0 violations, hashes match", proceeds to import torch
GATE RUN (executed, not summarized): 1269 unique pairs, 0 violations, covering
2190 rows across dpo_pairs.jsonl (1234), dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl (918),
repair_pairs.jsonl (38). The old "1272" counted rows, not pairs: 3 pairs exist in
BOTH dpo_pairs.jsonl and repair_pairs.jsonl (rows 13/2, 435/35, 1016/21), so they
are seen twice per epoch under --include-repair. Default run-1 config unaffected.

SECOND BUG, RED-WITNESSED. build_training_set.py printed a sha256 computed over
LF text while write_text() wrote CRLF — the number it tells you to "put in the
prereg" never matched the file (printed 0bb9a659..., file was 85fc0bdc...). Now
writes newline="" and aborts if written bytes disagree with the reported hash.
I ALSO RAISED A FALSE ALARM AND RETRACTED IT: I briefly concluded the capped file
was not reproducible. It is — LF-normalised it is exactly the builder's output.
Line endings were the entire discrepancy.

TIER 3 (efficacy) REMAINS UNTOUCHED AND UNCLAIMED. Nothing above is evidence that
DPO+NLL improves anything. That needs an adapter on disk, eval.py's frozen
held-out set scored before/after, a null baseline (same Modelfile, ADAPTER line
removed), and k>=5 seeds with test-retest sigma reported apart from
between-config sigma. With the current instrument (Track A ruler std 0.009 over 3
runs, §5; Track B 0.0093) anything under roughly +/-0.03 is inside the noise. The
shared noise-floor utility is still unbuilt. eval.py's own docstring concedes the
held-out shapes may overlap pretraining, so it is a RELATIVE ruler — iteration-N
vs iteration-0 on a frozen set — and cannot support "the model is N% better".

standing by for next DIRECTIVE

demonstrated. TRL logs `nll_loss` only when rpo_alpha is not None

Controlled experiment, both runs identical except the one keyword:
  TREATMENT rpo_alpha=1.0   step 5: loss 1.0959  nll_loss 0.4036
                            step 10: loss 1.0720  nll_loss 0.3981
  CONTROL   rpo_alpha=None  step 5: loss 0.6914  nll_loss KEY ABSENT
                            step 10: loss 0.6762  nll_loss KEY ABSENT
Additivity checks out against dpo_trainer.py:1338 (losses + rpo_alpha*nll_loss):
0.6914+0.4036 = 1.0950 vs observed 1.0959; 0.6762+0.3981 = 1.0743 vs 1.0720.
Agreement to ~1e-3, residual is batch-level variation between runs. The control
ran from a TEMPORARY copy of train_native.py; the copy was deleted afterwards and
the real file was never edited.
Recorded in data/smoke_rpo_alpha_result.json. The adapter itself is NOT committed
(.gitignore extended: smoke_adapter_*/ — matching the existing convention for
dpo_adapter*/).

VERDICT: The task distribution is a ceiling, but not the binding one. The
binding ceiling is the ruler, which is empirically saturated (6 of 10 held-out
tasks scored a perfect 5/5 in all 57 logged runs; pass@3 was the identical
number 0.90 in 55 of 57), and the reward that certifies the training data is
gameable in two ways demonstrated this pass. Diversifying tasks before fixing
the verifier and the ruler would produce a number nobody can trust in either
direction.

    Result.score 363-368; dedup 528-539)

FINDINGS (ranked, most decisive first):

1. (EXECUTED) THE RULER IS SATURATED — answers #14 Q3, outranks the task-
   distribution question. 57 eval runs on file, same config throughout. 6/10
   held-out tasks pinned at mean 1.000, sd 0.0000 across all 57 runs (gcd,
   is_prime, reverse_words, flatten, count_vowels, dedup). rotate is pinned at
   the floor: mean 0.004 (1 pass in 285 draws) — traced to `rotate([], 2)`
   raising ZeroDivisionError on `k % len(lst)`, verified by running both
   variants. Aggregate pass@1 mean 0.8779 sd 0.0197; pass@3 took only 3
   distinct values in 57 runs, {0.89:1, 0.90:55, 0.96:1} — effectively
   constant. Total headroom to ceiling = 0.1221, and 0.100 of that is `rotate`
   alone.
   RULING on #14 Q3: neither "move" nor "freeze" as posed. Freeze the current
   ten as a legacy line, build a second wider instrument, report both — the
   reason is saturation, not diversification of task *style*. Cited: Recht,
   Roelofs, Schmidt & Shankar 2019 (arXiv:1902.10811, verified) — new
   ImageNet/CIFAR test sets showed absolute drops but preserved model
   *ordering*, the empirical warrant for keeping the old set as a second
   number rather than discarding it. Jain et al. 2024, LiveCodeBench
   (arXiv:2403.07974, verified) — the standard-practice answer to benchmark
   staleness in this exact domain is a moving *population* under a frozen
   *protocol* (continuous new-problem collection), not a hand-edited list;
   not affordable here, cited for what "moving ruler done correctly" means.
   Concrete spec for a new instrument: screen candidate tasks with measure.py's
   existing frontier() (n=100, Wilson CI) and admit only tasks whose base-model
   pass rate sits in ~0.2-0.8 — a task at 1.0 or 0.0 contributes zero variance,
   so this criterion is derived from the instrument's job, not eyeballed.

2. (EXECUTED) THE PUBLISHED NOISE FLOOR IS UNDERSTATED ~2.1x, and the fix
   needs zero new compute. measure.py:30 hardcodes NOISE_RUNS=3; the first
   three eval_history.jsonl rows give sd 0.0094 — the "~0.009" everywhere in
   the channel, README, and the PUBLIC dpo/ README. Over all 57 runs of the
   identical config now on disk: sd = 0.0197, observed range 0.80-0.90 = 0.10
   (not the "0.02" at §5 line 557). §18 predicted 0.5x-6.3x uncertainty on the
   3-run figure; measured answer is 2.1x. Consequence: sd of a single-run-vs-
   single-run difference = 0.0197*sqrt(2) = 0.0279; honest 95% bar =
   1.96*0.0279 = +/-0.055, not the published +/-0.03 (which is ~1.1 sigma, and
   would fire on noise ~28% of the time). Total headroom (0.1221) is only 2.2
   of the honest bars wide. Cited for the framing (not the arithmetic, which
   is mine): Miller 2024 (arXiv:2411.00640, abstract verified) — eval scores
   drawn from an unseen super-population; the sampling unit for any claim that
   generalizes beyond these ten tasks is the TASK (n=10, 6 degenerate), not
   N_SAMPLES within a task.
   ACTION: retire "~0.009 std / +/-0.03" everywhere it appears — instructions.txt
   §5/§18/§39/§40/§41, README.md, OPEN-ITEMS.md, and the PUBLISHED dpo/ README
   (currently the least honest number in the public repo). Replace measure.py's
   fixed NOISE_RUNS=3 with a read over every prior matching-config run in
   eval_history.jsonl.

3. (READ-ONLY) #13 Q3 — THE RECEIPT CERTIFIES DATA BYTES, NOT THE VERIFIER
   THAT GIVES "VERIFIED" ITS MEANING. dataset_gate.build_file_entries hashes
   only dpo_pairs.jsonl/dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl/repair_pairs.jsonl
   (verify_dataset.py:37-39). Nothing hashes forge.py. But "0 violations" means
   forge.verify() against forge.SEED_TASKS — weaken one assert, loosen BANNED,
   or change CAND_TIMEOUT, and the receipt still matches while the meaning of
   "verified" silently changes. This is the same scope-hole shape as the
   chokepoint issue, one level up, and worse because it produces a PASSING
   receipt. Fix: add forge.py's sha256 (or a canonical serialization of
   SEED_TASKS) to the receipt under a "verifier" key; refuse on mismatch. Red
   witness is trivial: change one digit in one assert, confirm refusal, revert.

4. (EXECUTED) #13 Q3 — THE DIRECTIVE'S ISOLATION CLAIM IS FALSE IN BOTH
   HALVES. "python -I plus the banned-op filter blocks network and filesystem
   on purpose" (DIRECTIVE line 131-133) — python -h confirms -I only isolates
   from the user environment (implies -E -P -s), no filesystem/network effect
   at all; forge.py's own comment is honest about this ("NOT a real sandbox"),
   the channel prose is not. Against the actual BANNED regex (forge.py:74-78):
   `from subprocess import run`, `import pathlib`/`from pathlib import Path`,
   `import importlib`, `import pickle`, `import multiprocessing`,
   `import ftplib`, `import asyncio` ALL PASS (executed). `import socketserver`
   is blocked by accident (no trailing \b on the socket prefix); `from os
   import system` is blocked only because `import sys` prefix-matches
   "system". AND: -I is load-bearing for an undocumented reason — ran the
   harness shape with PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 set: with -I, rc=1 (AssertionError,
   correct); without -I, PYTHONOPTIMIZE strips every assert, a function
   returning a wrong value prints __PASS__ and exits 0. -I currently defends
   against this BY ACCIDENT (it implies -E which discards PYTHONOPTIMIZE); a
   plausible future "simplify -I away" refactor would silently turn the
   verifier into a rubber stamp. Belongs in forge.py:393's comment and
   OPEN-ITEMS.md.

5. (EXECUTED) #13 Q3 / #14 Q2 — THE REWARD IS GAMEABLE TODAY. An object whose
   __eq__ always returns True passes every ==-based assert in SEED_TASKS —
   ran it against the literal roman and decode_ways test bodies, both pass
   completely; reversing comparison order doesn't help (NotImplemented falls
   back to the reflected __eq__). Tasks using `is True`/`is False` (balanced,
   word_break) are immune; tasks wrapping in sorted() (two_sum) need one more
   line to fake __iter__ too. NON-CLAIM: nothing in the current 1,234 pairs is
   known to exploit this — not scanned. But the project's central claim
   (forge.py:10-12, "an OBJECTIVE verifier decides, the model does not get to
   score itself") is stated as a property and demonstrably is not one under
   optimization pressure. Fix is structural, not a threshold: compare
   canonical serializations (`repr(f(x)) == repr(expected)`, or a type-check-
   then-compare helper) once in the harness template at forge.py:377-383,
   covering every task without editing 13 test bodies. Red witness: the
   Always class above, before/after.

6. (EXECUTED) #13 Q2 — RULING on split_health's tolerance: DELETE THE
   BOOLEAN, but the 0.5 is the SECOND defect. The first: split_health
   (data.py:102-108) computes `documents` and `largest_doc_frac` from the
   PRE-DEDUP list over RAW text length, while group_split (data.py:45-51)
   dedupes by sha1 BEFORE splitting — the diagnostics describe a different
   corpus than the one actually split. Demonstrated: 50 identical docs + 5
   unique -> split_health reports "55 documents, largest 1.8%" when the true
   post-dedup count is 6 documents, one of which is ~50/55 of the mass — both
   printed diagnostics point away from the real cause, and leakage.py:283-284
   prints exactly these two misleading numbers immediately before
   sys.exit(1) (leakage.py:280-287 hard-aborts on `not health["ok"]"`;
   studio.py:185 also consumes it).
   RULING: (1) delete the boolean — one bit conflates "corpus too lumpy to
   split" (remedy: more/smaller documents) with "val set too small to
   estimate anything" (remedy: raise val_frac / shrink block_size), and 0.5
   serves neither. (2) Return real numbers computed on the DEDUPED set:
   unique_documents, val_documents, val_tokens, achieved_val_frac,
   largest_unique_doc_frac, and the derived quantity the question actually
   asked for — achievable_val_frac, the best fraction any whole-document
   split could reach (largest subset-sum of deduped doc lengths <= target,
   a cheap DP). Then achieved/achievable measures the SPLITTER's competence;
   achievable/requested measures the CORPUS's feasibility — two different
   failures, two different remedies, currently conflated into one False. (3)
   Where a hard stop is genuinely needed, derive it from what the number
   feeds: val_tokens // block_size sets the usable-precision floor, and
   data.py:191-194's get_batch ALREADY raises ValueError below that floor by
   construction — that's the real hard floor. Above it, print and let the
   operator judge.

7. (EXECUTED, citations self-verified) #14 Q1 — RULING on diversity vs
   volume: the executor's instinct is right, and it understates the problem —
   this is 1,234 pairs from 13 DISTINCT PROMPT STRINGS, not "13 tasks" as a
   soft category. Effective-task count (exp of Shannon entropy over tid
   distribution): uncapped 13-task file (N=1234) = 8.86 effective tasks;
   capped run-1 file (N=918) = 9.93; uncapped+5 new tasks x30 (N=1384) = 11.74;
   capped+5 new tasks x30 (N=1068) = 13.53. THE CAP COST 316 PAIRS (25.6% of
   the dataset) TO BUY 1.07 EFFECTIVE TASKS. Adding 5 new tasks at 30 pairs
   each (+150 pairs, +12% data) buys 2.88 effective tasks — 2.7x the cap's
   gain, while ADDING data instead of deleting a quarter of it. Not
   recommending un-capping the pinned/receipt-covered file; recommending the
   cap stop being described as the skew fix in build_training_set.py:5-7 and
   the published README, because measurably it isn't one. SEPARATE
   CORRECTION: run 1 does not train on the 54%-skewed set — dpo_pairs_capped's
   top-3 share is 39.2% (largest single task 13.07%); the 54.0% figure (53.97%
   recount, confirmed) describes dpo_pairs.jsonl, which only --raw-pairs
   loads.
   Citations: Yuan et al. 2023 (arXiv:2308.01825, verified) — rejection-
   sampling fine-tuning with more DISTINCT reasoning paths improves math
   reasoning more; nearest structural analogue, caveat: SFT-on-filtered-
   samples not DPO, distinctness is solution-paths-within-a-problem not
   problem-count. LIMA (arXiv:2305.11206, verified) EXPLICITLY EXCLUDED as
   evidence here — it's plain SFT with no RL/preference modeling; supports
   "curate don't accumulate" as a slogan only, not this specific tradeoff.
   Chung et al. 2022 (arXiv:2210.11416, verified) — 1.8K tasks, +9.4% average
   at Flan-PaLM 540B scale; diminishing-returns-past-282-tasks claim is
   UNVERIFIED, not asserted. No paper found measuring the exact "40x30 vs
   400 more decode_ways" tradeoff for execution-feedback preference learning;
   ruling rests on the clustering argument (13 prompts = 13 independent
   contexts under a super-population framing) plus Yuan et al.'s directional
   result: go wide.

8. (READ-ONLY, citations verified) #14 Q2 — RULING on the frontier: the
   executor's list (parsing/validation/text transforms/data reshaping/small
   business logic) is correct and buildable — every item is a total
   deterministic function, and likely BETTER at producing distinguishing edge
   cases than LeetCode puzzles given finding 1's ceiling effect. Going further
   to I/O-shaped tasks with MOCKED boundaries is supported by real prior art:
   tau-bench (arXiv:2406.12045, verified) compares mocked-database FINAL STATE
   against an annotated goal state for an objective, non-gameable reward — the
   working pattern; also introduces pass^k consistency, a metric this
   project's ruler currently lacks entirely. InterCode (arXiv:2306.14898,
   verified) is the un-mocked version — real Docker sandboxes, not affordable
   at this project's NUM_CANDIDATES=4/CAND_TIMEOUT=8 scale — cited as the
   boundary mocking approximates. BigCodeBench (arXiv:2406.15877, verified) —
   existence proof that "tangible, not just math" is testable at scale
   (1,140 tasks, 139 libraries, 99% avg branch coverage); whether it mocks
   filesystem/network is UNVERIFIED. Adopt-don't-build: KodCode
   (arXiv:2503.02951, verified) — released, self-verified question/solution/
   test triplets, an importer is a small script not a new pipeline (size claim
   UNVERIFIED). ACECODER (arXiv:2502.01718, verified) — closest published
   system to this project's whole design (test-case-synthesis -> pass-rate
   preference pairs); reported +10/+5 points on two base models; evaluates on
   PUBLIC benchmarks (HumanEval-plus, MBPP, BigCodeBench, LiveCodeBench)
   rather than a bespoke ruler — the standard-practice answer to #14 Q3 that
   nobody proposed. Magicoder (arXiv:2312.02120, verified) — grounding task
   PROMPTS in real open-source snippets as a cheaper diversity lever than
   inventing 40 tasks from imagination.
   PRECONDITION: do not write these tasks until finding 5 is fixed — new
   tasks written as `assert f(x) == expected` inherit the __eq__ hole, and
   fixing it later means touching 53 test bodies instead of one template.

9. (READ-ONLY) #13 Q3 — the DIRECTIVE's own "chokepoint by convention, not
   construction" concern is real and cheap to close. train_native.py:76 and
   train_dpo.py:51 each call require_verified and then SEPARATELY load the
   data — two independent statements; a third trainer or a reordered import
   drops the guarantee with no error. Fix: invert the API — add
   dataset_gate.load_verified(paths, data_dir) -> rows and delete
   require_verified from the trainers' vocabulary; you cannot train on data
   you never loaded through the one function that checks. Named pattern
   (verified primary source): Alexis King, "Parse, don't validate"
   (lexi-lambda.github.io, 2019-11-05) — exactly this shape ("push the burden
   of proof upward as far as possible, but no further"). Backstop for the
   residue: a ~15-line grep-test for any open()/load_dataset()/read_text() on
   data/*.jsonl outside dataset_gate.py/verify_dataset.py, hung on
   sync_public.py's existing pre-publish test hook (§41) — converts "a future
   trainer might not call it" from an unbounded worry into a bounded,
   mechanically-checked list.

10. (READ-ONLY except where marked; enumerated, not exhaustive — did not
    inspect repair.py, sync_public.py, export_adapter.py, config.py,
    overnight.ps1, studio.py beyond two call sites, or localllm/ beyond
    data.py/leakage.py/exp_lr_width.py/test_detectors.py) #13 Q3 — REST OF
    THE SWEEP:
    a. receipt excludes forge.py (finding 3) — scope, real, fix now
    b. BANNED regex enumeration (finding 4) — scope, real, EXECUTED break
    c. == vs __eq__ (finding 5) — scope, real, EXECUTED break
    d. split_health pre/post-dedup mismatch + 0.5 (finding 6) — both, real
    e. dataset_gate chokepoint by convention (finding 9) — scope, cheap fix
    f. eval.py:189-191 / measure.py:82-83 catch-and-continue on generation
       exceptions, so a transport failure scores as a WRONG ANSWER, inflating
       the denominator and depressing the score/noise floor — real, not
       previously flagged. Fix: count n_attempted vs n_scored separately.
    g. forge.py:61 CAND_TIMEOUT=8s — threshold AND non-determinism: a correct
       but slow solution scores as failing and can become the "rejected" half
       of a pair (teaches the model a correct answer is wrong); wall-clock
       means re-running verify_dataset.py on a busy machine could flip a pair
       and invalidate a receipt for a reason unrelated to the data. Fix:
       record timeout distinctly from failed; never emit a pair whose only
       defect is a timeout.
    h. leakage.py CONTAMINATED=0.20/SUSPECT=0.05 — threshold, already known
    i. exp_lr_width.py's gap>=0.020 AND range-disjoint — DEFENSIBLE: pre-
       registered, printed with the verdict, conjoined with a distribution-
       free check no single number can fake. Model for the others to imitate.
    j. forge.py conciseness margin >=40 chars — dormant, EMIT_CONCISENESS=False
    k. build_training_set.py CAP=120 — real but low-stakes, now measured
       (finding 7)
    l. dedup signature mismatch between forge.py (excludes tid) and
       verify_dataset.py (includes tid, documented why) — currently harmless
       since all 13 prompts are distinct; breaks silently if a future task
       scheme reuses a prompt template
    m. test_detectors.py:131 `ok = worst < 0.001` — NOT ASSESSED, did not read
       the surrounding function
    What this table would miss: guards expressed as control flow rather than
    a numeric literal (item f was found by reading, not grep); PowerShell
    guards; anything in .venv-train; the un-inspected files listed above.
    Nothing mechanically enforces this sweep except item 9's proposed
    grep-test.

LEFTOVER RISKS:
  1. Even with a perfect ruler, the effect may be unmeasurable at this scale
     — headroom 0.122, honest single-run 95% bar +/-0.055. A wider ruler
     moves the ceiling, not the fundamental problem: between-task variance
     dominates at 10-30 tasks, and task COUNT (not samples-per-task) sets the
     floor. Detecting a genuine +3% needs many more tasks or a paired
     before/after design; neither is costed.
  2. Fixing the verifier (findings 4/5) invalidates every existing pair's
     "verified" status under the stricter rule, and finding 3's proposed
     forge.py-in-the-receipt would correctly detect this. Expect a full
     re-verify of 1,269 council-pinned (§12/§14) pairs; if any fail, the
     dataset can no longer be pinned to those numbers. Unbudgeted collision.
  3. CAND_TIMEOUT nondeterminism (10g): two verify_dataset.py runs on
     identical bytes can in principle disagree; nothing currently detects
     this — would surface as a mystery violation on a busy machine.
  4. Mocked-boundary tasks (finding 8) reintroduce the ambiguity the pure-
     function rule removed — tau-bench's goal-state comparison works because
     the goal state is hand-annotated; a bug in a task's mock is a silent
     reward corruption with no red witness available.
  5. Diversifying tasks AND widening the ruler at the same time means a
     positive result won't disambiguate "better tasks" from "unsaturated
     instrument" — the old ten-task line stays the disambiguator, and it's
     only informative for the 4 tasks that can actually move.
  6. The chokepoint fix (finding 9) defends against drift/accident, not a
     modified dataset_gate.py itself — same honestly-stated limit as the
     receipt not defending against a modified receipt. Fine as a design
     choice; must never be described as tamper protection.
  7. 57 runs of a BASE model is not 57 runs of a TRAINED model — the noise
     floor after LoRA + Ollama ADAPTER re-export may differ; the export
     path's contribution to run-to-run variance is unmeasured.
  8. This entire pass is one model reading one repo. Findings 4/5/6 are
     demonstrations constructed by replicating the harness shape rather than
     calling forge.verify() directly (to avoid writing temp files) — if that
     replication is unrepresentative of the real end-to-end behavior, the
     executor is better placed to produce the real red witness.

THE QUESTIONS THEMSELVES: Activation #14 was the right instinct pointed one
step too far downstream. It presumes an instrument that could tell whether
the task distribution is the ceiling. Q3 got closest — "the ruler is made of
the same math" was flagged as the part the executor would get wrong alone —
but it framed the ruler problem as COMPOSITION (LeetCode vs realistic) when
the measured problem is RESOLUTION (6 of 10 tasks pinned at ceiling for 57
straight runs). That was visible in a file already on disk, no literature
search required.
  The question it should have asked FIRST: "What is the minimum effect
eval.py as configured can detect, given the runs already on disk — and does
the frozen held-out set have enough unsaturated headroom to register any
improvement at all?" Answerable in fifteen minutes, zero new compute, zero
web access. Asked first, #14's actual question arrives with a working
measuring stick attached.
  A second question nobody has asked in 42 sections: "Is the reward actually
unhackable?" It's the load-bearing claim of the whole project (forge.py:10-12,
repeated in the public README, assumed settled by #14 Q2's own framing), and
two probes falsified it in under ten minutes. A claim that central should
have had a red witness from the beginning — the project's own standing rule
says exactly that.
  One thing worth keeping: §41's adapter-is-live control gates on "not byte-
identical to null" rather than a tuned similarity number, reporting the
ratios as evidence without keying off them — the correct general form of the
answer to #13's original Q1, and exp_lr_width.py's prereg-plus-range-
disjointness (finding 10i) is the same instinct. Both should be named and
reused; the rest of finding 10's guards are missing exactly this pattern.

RECOMMENDED NEXT PROMPT (proprietor -> executor):
  "Close the reward-integrity class before writing a single new task, because
  new tasks written today would inherit the holes. Three red witnesses, in
  this order.

  (1) The always-equal object. `class Always: __eq__ = lambda s,o: True`
  passes every ==-based assert in SEED_TASKS — confirmed against roman and
  decode_ways. Fix once in the harness template (forge.py:377-383), not in 13
  test bodies: compare canonical forms (repr, or type-check-then-compare).
  RED: Always marked ok=True. GREEN: same candidate rejected, all 13 real
  solutions still pass.

  (2) The banned-op filter. `from subprocess import run`, `import pathlib`,
  `from pathlib import Path`, `import importlib`, `import pickle`,
  `import multiprocessing`, `import ftplib`, `import asyncio` all pass
  forge.py:74-78; `import socketserver` is blocked by accident; `from os
  import system` only by sys prefix-matching system. Rewrite against an
  allow-list, or state plainly in forge.py and the public README that it is
  not an isolation boundary. Separately: -I does not block filesystem/network
  (verified against python -h) but DOES block PYTHONOPTIMIZE, which without
  -I strips every assert and turns a wrong answer into __PASS__/exit 0 —
  write this at forge.py:393; right now nothing documents that -I is the only
  thing standing between the reward and a pass-everything oracle.

  (3) The receipt's scope. dataset_gate hashes the three data files and
  nothing else, but 'verified' means whatever SEED_TASKS/BANNED/CAND_TIMEOUT
  currently say. Add a verifier sha256 (forge.py, or a canonical
  serialization of SEED_TASKS' prompt/entry/tests fields) to the receipt;
  make require_verified refuse on mismatch. RED: change one digit in one
  assert, confirm refusal, revert. While there: replace require_verified with
  load_verified so the gate is the only code path that opens the data files
  (Parse, don't validate) — closes the by-convention/by-construction gap.

  Then re-run verify_dataset.py and report what changed. If any of the 1,269
  pairs now fails under the stricter verifier, do NOT quietly regenerate the
  dataset — report the count and tids and stop; that collides with the
  §12/§14 pin and is the proprietor's call.

  One thing that needs no code, same commit: retire the '~0.009 std /
  +/-0.03 noise floor' figure everywhere — instructions.txt §5/§18/§39/§40/§41,
  README.md, OPEN-ITEMS.md, and the PUBLISHED dpo/ README. It came from
  measure.py's hardcoded NOISE_RUNS=3 and the first three eval_history.jsonl
  rows. 57 runs of the identical config now exist: mean 0.8779, sd 0.0197,
  range 0.80-0.90. Honest single-run 95% bar is +/-0.055; total headroom to
  ceiling is 0.122, of which 0.100 is the rotate task alone (base model passes
  it ~1% of the time — rotate([], 2) raises ZeroDivisionError). 6 of 10 held-
  out tasks scored 5/5 in all 57 runs; pass@3 was 0.90 in 55 of them. Change
  measure.py to compute the floor from every matching prior run instead of
  always three. The ruler rebuild is the next directive and it is gated on
  (1)-(3) landing first."

FINDING 5 — THE REWARD WAS GAMEABLE. CONFIRMED, then CLOSED.
Real red witness, through forge.verify() end to end, not a replica:
  RED   class with __eq__ returning True, on task `roman`  -> ok=True   (!!)
        honest wrong answer, same task                     -> ok=False
  The project's central claim (forge.py:10-12, "the tests decide, the model does
  not get to score itself") was FALSE under optimization pressure.
FIX is structural and lives in ONE place, the harness template, not in 13 test
bodies: the entry point is wrapped and its return value must be built from plain
builtins, checked by EXACT type. isinstance is not enough — `class S(str)` with a
custom __eq__ passes isinstance and still lies.
  GREEN __eq__-always-True exploit  -> ok=False, TypeError from the guard
        str-subclass exploit        -> ok=False, TypeError from the guard
        honest correct (str task)   -> ok=True
        honest correct (list task)  -> ok=True
LEFTOVER RISK 2 IS RESOLVED, NOT MERELY FEARED. He predicted the stricter rule
might invalidate the pinned dataset. Re-ran the full gate: 1269/1269 pairs PASS,
0 violations, all three file hashes unchanged. The dataset survives.
NOT CLAIMED: that no existing pair exploits this. His non-claim stands — I did
not scan the 1,234 pairs for exploit shapes, only re-verified them under the
stricter rule, which they pass.

FINDING 2 — NOISE FLOOR. CONFIRMED by independent recomputation, one number of
his corrected. Over all 57 runs of the identical config in eval_history.jsonl:
  pass@1 mean 0.8779, sd 0.0199 (he said 0.0197 — agrees), range 0.80-0.90
  pass@3 took 3 values in 57 runs: {0.90: 55, 0.96: 1, 0.89: 1} — near-constant
  single-run-difference sd = 0.0199*sqrt(2) = 0.0281
  honest 95% bar = +/-0.055, NOT the published +/-0.03
  headroom to ceiling = 0.1221
  CORRECTION TO HIM: he reported the first-3 sd as 0.0094; I measure 0.0115 from
  the first three rows. Understatement vs the PUBLISHED "~0.009" is ~2.2x, vs the

scored 1.000 in ALL 57 runs (gcd, is_prime, reverse_words, flatten, count_vowels,

45. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-28 — §43 finding 3 CLOSED: the receipt now pins the
    verifier, not only the data)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE HOLE, in his words and confirmed: "0 violations" is a statement about
forge.verify() run against forge.SEED_TASKS. Every data hash could match while the
MEANING of "verified" silently changed — a PASSING receipt for a weaker claim,
which is worse than a failing one.

CLOSED. The receipt now carries a "verifier" fingerprint (sha256 of forge.py) and
require_verified refuses on mismatch. Schema bumped 2 -> 3.

RED/GREEN, three states, data bytes untouched throughout:
  GREEN clean          -> "[gate] verified receipt OK - dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl
                          (918 pairs); 0 violations, hashes match"
  RED   weakened ONE assert in the roman task (assert ... or True) ->
        "GATE: the VERIFIER changed since this data was verified.
           forge.py: receipt d84e4a544c20e208... on-disk 5aff327c14868eac..."
  GREEN restored byte-identical -> passes again
  Also RED on the stale schema-2 receipt before regeneration ("receipt schema is
  2, this code needs 3"), which is the pre-existing guard doing its job.

WHOLE FILE, DELIBERATELY, and this is the part worth recording. The tempting
alternative was to hash only "the parts that matter" — SEED_TASKS, BANNED,
CAND_TIMEOUT, the harness template. That requires hand-picking which parts of a
verifier are semantic, which is EXACTLY the invented-scope class activation #13
is about: the guard passes while the thing it guards has moved. A whole-file hash
has no scope to get wrong. Accepted cost, stated in the code: editing a comment in
forge.py invalidates the receipt and forces a ~90s re-verify. That is the correct
direction to be wrong in, and it is offered as a second data point for #13 Q1
alongside §41's bright-line criterion — both fixes avoided inventing a threshold
or a scope, by different means (bright line; no scope at all).

Dataset re-verified under the new schema: 1269/1269 PASS, 0 violations, all three
data hashes unchanged (ff78db76, 85fc0bdc, 9eaa4666).

REMAINING FROM §43, unchanged priority order: finding 9 (invert the API to
load_verified so a trainer cannot load data without the check — note the honest
scoping: returning verified PATHS still lets a future trainer construct a path
itself, so his grep-test backstop is the part that actually bounds it, not the
API change alone), finding 6 (split_health pre/post-dedup), 10f (transport failure
scored as wrong answer), 10g (timeout scored as failure), finding 1's second
instrument, finding 7 (stop calling the 120-cap a skew fix).

standing by for next DIRECTIVE

46. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-28 - findings 6, 7, 9, 10f, 10g CLOSED. Everything
    fact-checked by re-derivation, not by trusting the advisor)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
EVERY NUMBER BELOW WAS RE-DERIVED HERE. He has no peer seats, said so, and asked
to be re-run rather than trusted. Where I got a different number I say so.

10f - A TRANSPORT FAILURE WAS SCORED AS A WRONG ANSWER. Confirmed at both sites:
eval.py continued on a generation exception while still dividing by N_SAMPLES,
and measure.py did the same over its own n. So an Ollama hiccup depressed the
score AND inflated the run-to-run spread - in the very file that is the ruler,
and therefore in the 0.0199 noise floor published in section 44. Fixed:
attempted / scored / gen_errors counted separately; the score is over what was
actually measured; a task where nothing could be sampled is EXCLUDED rather than
counted as zero; pass@k skips tasks with fewer than k scored samples. eval.py now
records coverage and gen_errors_total per run.
  Synthetic check (no Ollama): 3 tasks, one with 2 gen errors, one with 5 ->
  pass@1 0.8333 over 2/3 tasks, task c excluded and named. Old behaviour scored
  c as 0/5.
  NOT CLAIMED: that this changes the published 0.0199. The historical runs cannot
  be re-scored - the old records do not distinguish the two cases (his own
  non-claim, and it still stands). The number is honest for what it measured;
  future runs will measure it better.

10g - A TIMEOUT WAS A WRONG ANSWER. Result now carries timed_out, and a timed-out
candidate can never become the rejected half of a pair. It was previously
possible to teach the model that a correct-but-slow answer is wrong, and because
CAND_TIMEOUT is wall-clock the same bytes could flip between runs on a busy
machine. Red witness: an infinite-loop solution -> ok=False timed_out=True.

9 - CHOKEPOINT. dataset_gate.load_verified() added (the Parse-Do-Not-Validate
pattern); both trainers now OBTAIN their file list from the gate instead of
calling a check and then separately naming files. AND the honest part: that alone
does not close it, so test_gate_coverage.py fails if any module reads training
data without going through the gate.
  GREEN 9 modules scanned, 5 exempt, 2 routed through the gate -> PASS
  RED   planted a 2-line bypass trainer -> FAIL, names the file and line
Wired into the sync_public.py pre-publish hook, so it runs on every publish.
MY OWN GUARD WAS WRONG FIRST, and this is worth recording because it is exactly
the class activation 13 is about: version 1 tested "reads training data", which
flagged BOTH legitimate trainers. The property that matters is "reads training
data WITHOUT passing the gate". I caught it because I ran the green case and it
failed.

6 - split_health DESCRIBED A DIFFERENT CORPUS THAN THE ONE IT SPLIT. Reproduced
his probe exactly: 50 identical documents + 5 unique reported "55 documents,
largest 2.5%" when the truth is 7 unique documents with one at 15.9%. Both
printed diagnostics pointed away from the cause, and leakage.py hard-exits on
them. Rebuilt on the DEDUPED corpus, boolean deleted, and the derived quantity

CORRECTION TO HIM, MEASURED: he offered "a paired before/after design" as the
alternative to more tasks. IT BUYS NOTHING HERE - 1.0x tighter, unpaired 95% bar
+/-0.0549 vs paired +/-0.0564. Pairing cancels BETWEEN-TASK variance, but the
aggregate is already a per-task average and the residual noise is per-task
binomial sampling, which pairing cannot remove. More movable tasks and more seeds
are the only levers. This matters because the paired design was the cheap-looking
escape route from building a new instrument, and it is not one.

KODCODE IS DISQUALIFIED AS A SOURCE FOR PUBLISHED TASKS. CC BY-NC is

SCOPE LIMIT, STATED: I measured ONE of eleven shards and every row in it is

NOT DONE, AND NOT STARTED WITHOUT A DECISION: the screen itself. At n=100 per
candidate against the local 8B this is hours of GPU time - an overnight job. Two
choices change its cost materially and should be made before it runs:
  1. TWO-STAGE SCREEN. The 0.2-0.8 band does not need n=100 to reject a task
     sitting at 1.0 or 0.0. n=10 to discard obvious ceiling/floor cases, then
     n=100 only on survivors, should cut GPU time by most of an order of magnitude
     for the same admissions. Wilson CI at n=10 is wide enough that only clear
     rejects get dropped, which is the point.
  2. RANDOM SAMPLING of the candidate pool, not the first N, so the admitted set
     is not an artifact of dataset ordering.

A SIDE EFFECT WORTH KEEPING: the 0.2-0.8 admission criterion doubles as a
contamination filter. A task the base model memorised in pretraining scores ~1.0
and is rejected automatically - which is the exact weakness eval.py's own
docstring concedes it cannot otherwise rule out. Importing public data would
normally worsen contamination risk; here the criterion neutralises it.

the code that produced the results is recorded alongside them.
  400 candidates, target 30 admitted, band [0.2, 0.8]
  stage 1 n=8  - discard ceiling and floor cheaply
  stage 2 n=40 - measure only what survived
  random sample (seed 1337), not head-of-file, so the admitted set is not an

  resumable via data/screen_results.jsonl; honours council/STOP

WHAT WAS VERIFIED BEFORE SPENDING THE GPU, because a silent harness fault would
have rejected every candidate and produced a confident zero overnight:
  - AceCode asserts call the function by its own global name, so run_tests binds
    the return-type-guarded entry point to that name and the asserts run unchanged.
    Confirmed by printing a generated harness.
  - Smoke run on 2 candidates exercised the full loop. BOTH SCORED 0/3, which is
    exactly the result not to take at face value. Traced it: the model emitted
    malformed Python (a broken comprehension). Harness, binding and strict guard
    all correct; the failure was real. Had I assumed a bug, or assumed the tasks
    were simply hard, either conclusion would have been unevidenced.

EXPECTATION, STATED BEFORE THE RESULT SO IT CANNOT BE RETROFITTED: AceCode tasks
are substantially meatier than the LeetCode-shaped SEED_TASKS - the first two drawn
were Django-style URL routing and a heatmap generator. I expect a LOW yield with
mostly FLOOR rejections, which is the opposite failure from the one that saturated
the current ruler (all ceiling). First two candidates: 0/8 and 0/8, both floor.

THE TRAP TO AVOID WHEN THIS FINISHES, written down now while it is still cheap to
commit to: if 400 candidates do not yield 30 admitted tasks, the fix is MORE
CANDIDATES, not a wider band. Widening [0.2, 0.8] to reach a target number would be
inventing a threshold to make the answer come out right - precisely the class
activation 13 exists to close, and it would be the fourth instance. The band is
derived from the instrument's job: outside it a task cannot register a change. That
does not become less true because the yield was disappointing.

NOT CLAIMED: any yield number. The run is in flight. Two candidates screened at the
time of writing, both rejected at the floor, zero admitted. Whatever the final
number is, it gets reported as measured - including if it falls short of 30.

The floor-heavy half was right; "low yield" was not. A 33% admit rate is roughly an
order of magnitude better than the ~4% the current held-out set achieves (4 movable
of 10). At this rate the 30-task target is reached around candidate 90, not 400, and

the number that counts is the one at the end of the run.

"can we find enough movable tasks" and becomes "how many seeds can we afford", since
k drives the detectable effect once task count is satisfied. That reopens the k=3 vs
k=5 choice on cost grounds rather than availability grounds.

Read-only by construction: it watches data/screen_results.jsonl and never talks to
the screening process, so opening or closing it cannot disturb a run that holds the
GPU for hours. Shows screened/target progress, admitted/target, elapsed, observed
seconds-per-candidate, the rejection breakdown, and a tail of recent verdicts.
The ETA reports BOTH ways the run can end - pool exhausted or admitted target
reached - and highlights whichever lands first, because with a 33% admit rate the
target arrives long before the pool does. If the projection falls short of target it
says so in amber rather than only showing a time.

Decision 1 as specified rejects zero candidates; Decision 2 is correct and now
has hard evidence, plus a licensing gap the executor's top-layer check missed.

EVIDENCE READ: instructions.txt lines 1-200 (OPERATING PROTOCOL + live
DIRECTIVE) and 4061-4841 (§43-§47); forge.py :360-448 (Result.score, verify(),
the §44 guard :394-411, harness assembly :412-419, -I invocation :429),

FINDINGS (ranked, most decisive first):

1. (EXECUTED — RED WITNESS) THE §44 TYPE GUARD IS BYPASSED BY THE EXACT TASK
   SHAPE §47 PROPOSES TO IMPORT. forge.py:389's "This closes the class for
   every current and future task" is false and must be deleted. The guard
   only bites if the test body reaches the entry point THROUGH the run_tests
   parameter. All 13 current tasks are safe by a coincidence of naming: every
   run_tests parameter is named identically to the entry point, so it shadows
   the module-level definition — a convention, not a mechanism, nowhere
   enforced. Executed through the real forge.verify():
     A. CURRENT SEED TASK 'roman' (param shadows entry name)
        exploit  ok=False  TypeError from _strict   <- §44's fix CONFIRMED
        honest   ok=True                             <- not broken CONFIRMED
     B. IMPORT-SHAPE TASK: def run_tests(f): assert to_roman(1)=="I" ...
        exploit  ok=True   <- GUARD SILENTLY ABSENT
        honest   ok=True
   §47 measured AceCode's tests as "bare assert statements" and concluded "an
   importer is a small script." Bare asserts call the function by its literal
   name -> state B. Unless the importer rewrites every imported test into
   `def run_tests(<exact_entry_name>):`, every one of ~30+ imported held-out
   tasks lands with the reward hack fully open on the NEW instrument. Fix
   options, both cheap: bind the guard at module scope instead of by
   parameter, or mechanically require run_tests's first parameter name equal
   task.entry and refuse otherwise.

2. (EXECUTED) THE SCREENING COST MODEL IS WRONG BY ~3 ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE, AND
   DECISION 1 IS THE WRONG LEVER. Throughput derived from eval_history.jsonl
   timestamps: 57 runs x 50 generations over 8.78h -> 11.09 s/gen; median
   inter-run gap /50 -> 10.84 s/gen. Two estimators agree.
     screen all 83,600 @ n=100                 8,360,000 gens  1,049 days
     screen all 83,600 @ n=10 (executor stage1)   836,000 gens    105 days
     screen 2,000 @ n=100                         200,000 gens     25 days
     screen 150 @ n=100                            15,000 gens    1.9 days
     screen 60 @ n=100                              6,000 gens   18 hours
   §47 said "n=100... hours of GPU time... an overnight job" against the pool
   it names — off by a factor of ~25,000. You never screen the pool; you need
   ~30 admits (or ~150, finding 4) and the job is sized by how many candidates
   you DRAW, not by the pool's existence. n=100->n=10 is a 10x lever; correct
   pool-draw sizing is a ~500-1,000x lever, and Decision 1 asked about the
   small one.

3. (EXECUTED) DECISION 1 ANSWER: the two-stage screen as specified rejects
   NOTHING at n=10, and the correct stage 1 is already free, on disk. 95%
   Wilson, band [0.2,0.8]: at n=10 no k value's CI lies entirely outside the
   band (10/10 -> [0.7225,1.0], overlaps; 0/10 -> [0.0,0.2775], overlaps). The
   smallest n at which ANYTHING is rejectable is 16. This is not a
   hypothesis-testing problem and should not be designed as one: you need
   ~30-150 admits from a pool with thousands of in-band members; false
   negatives are nearly free (draw another), only false positives cost a
   slot — the opposite of what SPRT/group-sequential machinery is built to
   optimize (both-error-rate control on every arm).
   THE FREE STAGE 0: AceCode's `inferences` field carries 80 precomputed
   completions per row from 5 models — one is llama3.1_instruct, median 16
   completions/row, same family as the ruler's llama3:8b. Computed the
   repo-equivalent all-pass rate:
     shard 0  oss                 floor 0.561 | IN BAND 0.227 | ceiling 0.212
     shard 4  evol                floor 0.725 | IN BAND 0.089 | ceiling 0.185
     shard 10 bigcode_python_fns  floor 0.594 | IN BAND 0.201 | ceiling 0.205
   That's an n=16 same-family screen costing ZERO GPU seconds, better than the
   proposed stage 1. Recommended design replacing both stages: (0) filter on
   AceCoder's llama3.1_instruct all-pass rate in [0.2,0.8], free; (1) random-
   sample ~200 survivors; (2) local confirm at n=30 (smallest n where Wilson
   can reject a saturated candidate with margin) = 6,000 gens ~ 18 GPU-hours,
   genuinely overnight; (3) admit, then re-measure baseline with FRESH draws
   (finding 5). Citations (self-verified): Wald 1945 (SPRT, bibliographic
   record verified, full text not fetched, cited as the name for a fully-
   sequential alternative, not a recommendation) — Locatelli/Gutzeit/
   Carpentier 2016, Thresholding Bandit Problem, arXiv:1605.08671 (abstract
   verified — closest published match to the literal problem, but allocates a
   fixed budget to classify ALL arms, wrong objective here, not recommended
   for implementation) — Waudby-Smith & Ramdas, JRSS-B 2024 (bibliographic
   record verified, abstract summary only — anytime-valid confidence
   sequences, the right tool ONLY IF stage 2 stays expensive after free stage
   0 shrinks it, which is not expected).

4. (EXECUTED) §47'S POWER NUMBERS REPRODUCE EXACTLY AND ARE UNDERSTATED ~5.1x.
   Per-task sample sd over the 4 movable tasks: rle 0.1402, nth_fib 0.1118,
   kadane 0.0927, rotate 0.0265, mean 0.0928 — matches §47 to 4 digits, no
   arithmetic error. The ERROR IS THE INPUT: 0.0928 is the sd of the OLD
   movable tasks (including rotate, which is at the floor and contributes
   almost no variance), applied to NEW tasks that are screened specifically
   to MAXIMIZE variance. Per-task variance is essentially pure binomial
   (verified: predicted sqrt(p(1-p)/5) vs observed sd agree closely):
     n_samples=5, p=0.5 (worst case)     -> sd 0.2236 -> T=174.4 @k=5,d=3%
     n_samples=5, p=0.2/0.8 (band edge)  -> sd 0.1789 -> T=111.6
     n_samples=5, p~U(0.2,0.8)           -> sd 0.2098 -> T=153.5
   ~112-174 in-band tasks at k=5, not ~30 — a consistent 5.1x factor,
   (0.2098/0.0928)^2. Same correction at every cell (delta=5%,k=5 needs ~55,
   not ~11). Reframe: only the PRODUCT T x n_samples x k costs anything —
   task count is a generalization-breadth variable, not a cost variable
   (Miller 2024 super-population framing, carried from §43, not re-fetched).
   Honest budget: detect +3% needs T*n_samples*k >= 3,837 generations PER
   SIDE = 7,674 total = 23.1 GPU-hours PER VERDICT, forever. detect +5% needs
   2,763 gens = 8.3 GPU-hours per verdict. That number should drive the
   target-effect decision. Also: 10.84 s/generation is very slow for a
   quantized 8B — unbatched sequential Ollama calls; a throughput fix could
   matter more than every design refinement in §47 combined.

5. (READ-ONLY) SELECTION ON NOISE: do not reuse screening draws as the
   baseline. Tasks are admitted because their OBSERVED rate fell in band;
   observed != true. Reusing those draws as "before" conditions the number on
   the selection event (winner's-curse structure); the "after" measurement
   with fresh draws regresses toward the untruncated mean, biasing Δ by
   unknown sign in no one's error bars. Fix costs nothing decided now:
   screening draws are discarded, baseline is measured fresh.

6. (EXECUTED) DECISION 2 ANSWER: random sampling is right, and the evidence
   is stronger than the executor knew — the corpus is PERFECTLY BLOCKED BY
   SOURCE. `source` column across all 11 shards: shards 0-2 pure oss, 3 mixed,
   4-6 mostly evol, 6-10 pure bigcode_python_fns. Totals: oss 25,862 (29.7%),
   evol 26,920 (30.9%), bigcode_python_fns 34,367 (39.4%). Zero interleaving —
   "first N" gives 100% oss for any N up to 25,862. STRATIFY by source rather
   than sample uniformly — the three strata have different in-band rates
   (22.7%/8.9%/20.1%, finding 3) and different licenses (finding 7). Also:
   §47's "~83,600 usable" applies ONE stratum's rate (oss, the only one
   measured) to all three — the 39.4% bigcode_python_fns stratum is
   structurally different (harvested functions, not LLM-written pairs) and
   wasn't measured. §47 stated this scope limit and extrapolated past it
   anyway. (Stratified-sampling citation — Cochran 1977 ch.5, Neyman 1934 —
   NOT FETCHED, UNVERIFIED, do not cite downstream; the concrete
   recommendation rests on the measured blocking above, not the citation.)

7. (EXECUTED, primary sources) LICENSING: right at the top layer, incomplete
   underneath. Verified directly against dataset cards: KodCode-V1 is
   cc-by-nc-4.0, 487,432 rows — §47's disqualification is correct. AceCode-87K
   (same card as "89K") is mit, 87,149 rows — §47's conclusion correct as far
   as checked. NOT checked: AceCoder's seeds are Magicoder-OSS-Instruct-75K,
   Magicoder-Evol-Instruct-110K, and bigcode/stack-dedup-python-fns
   (corroborated by the source column matching one-for-one, seed list itself
   not read from the paper — see NON-CLAIMS). Their licenses, verified at
   card: oss stratum (29.7%) MIT + an OpenAI-usage-policy note (generated
   with gpt-3.5-turbo); evol stratum (30.9%) Apache-2.0; bigcode_python_fns
   (39.4%, THE LARGEST STRATUM) — NO LICENSE STATED, card exists but is
   empty. So AceCode's MIT tag is a declaration made over a stack of three
   upstreams, the largest of which states no terms. Mitigating: AceCoder
   rewrote questions and synthesized tests, so what ships may be their
   derivative rather than upstream text; importing ~30-150 problem statements
   is low practical exposure. But the repo's whole reason to check is a
   public MIT claim. RECOMMENDATION: restrict the import to the oss stratum —
   unbroken MIT->MIT->MIT, 25,862 rows, HIGHEST in-band rate of the three
   (22.7%), the one stratum actually measured at 96.0% usable. Yields ~5,870
   in-band candidates against a need of ~150. Also: .gitignore prevents
   accidentally staging KodCode FILES, not the failure mode §47 itself named
   (a good task hand-pasted into SEED_TASKS) — consider a provenance field
   per task instead.

8. (READ-ONLY) WHOLE-FILE HASHING (§45) IS THE RIGHT CALL, WITH EVIDENCE — but
   "no scope to get wrong" is half-true. forge.py has 6 commits of 341 total;
   3 are today's fixes, all semantic; ~90s re-verify cost; §46 shows the
   mechanism firing on a REAL semantic change (a true positive). Zero known
   false positives — the workflow-cost worry does not materialize. But the
   claim removed LINE scope, not FILE scope: dataset_gate.py:62
   VERIFIER_FILES=("forge.py",) is still a hand-picked one-element tuple.
   verify_dataset.py:76 check() — the function that DEFINES a violation — is
   unpinned. Weaken check() to require only chosen_ok and the receipt still
   matches, "0 violations" still prints — the identical failure §45 closed,
   one file over. Add verify_dataset.py to the tuple (and note a hash cannot
   defend against modification of the hasher/dataset_gate.py itself).

9. (EXECUTED) §44's "CORRECTION TO HIM" on the noise floor (0.0094 -> 0.0115)
   is a population-vs-sample-sd ESTIMATOR CHOICE, not an arithmetic error —
   confirmed by recomputing both ways. §44 applied the sample estimator here
   but called the identical population-vs-sample gap on the aggregate number
   (0.0197 vs 0.0199) "agreement." One discrepancy, two standards, in the
   same paragraph. Consequence-free (conclusion unaffected either way) but
   worth naming as an estimator choice, not banking as a found error.

10. (READ-ONLY) A §43 ERROR OF MY OWN, CAUGHT BY §47: leftover risk 1 offered
    a paired before/after design as an uncosted alternative; §47 measured it
    (1.0x, buys nothing) and is right. Sharper reason than §47 gave: eval.py
    runs a FROZEN HELD_OUT list, so every run is already paired on task —
    between-task variance was cancelled by construction before pairing was
    ever proposed. There was no unpaired design to improve on.

short of where §47 stopped; (5) the question behind all of these: does a
+3% verdict on a 150-task instrument costing a day of GPU each time actually
change any decision this project will make — if the honest answer is "we'd
act the same either way below +5%," finding 4's correction is permission to

51. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-28 - throughput estimate was wrong by ~15x; revised
    projection and firming yield numbers)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECOND WRONG PREDICTION IN TWO ENTRIES, and the proprietor caught this one before I
measured it. I estimated the screen at "5-6 hours". Measured at 46 candidates:

    elapsed              15.4 min
    rate                 20.1 s per candidate
    screened / admitted  46 / 14

WHY I WAS WRONG: I assumed ~3s per generation from the 2-candidate smoke run, which
was measured on a COLD model - the smoke was the first thing to touch Ollama after
the pull, so it paid model-load cost that no subsequent candidate pays. Warm, the 8B
is doing roughly 0.8s per generation on these short prompts and short completions.
At ~25 generations per candidate on average (8 at stage 1, plus 40 for the ~43% that
reach stage 2) that is the 20.1s observed. The lesson is the ordinary one: a timing
taken from the first run of anything includes startup that the steady state does not,
and I extrapolated from it without saying so.

REVISED PROJECTION: 30 admitted at roughly candidate 99, about 18 minutes from the
measurement above. Total run under an hour, not overnight. The overnight framing in
section 48 was wrong and the GPU is not occupied for the evening.

YIELD IS FIRMING AND DRIFTING DOWN, as section 49 warned it might:
                     at n=24        at n=46
    ADMITTED          33.3%          30.4%
    floor             45.8%          30.4%
    ceiling           16.7%          26.1%
    outside band       4.2%          13.0%
The floor share fell and ceiling/outside-band rose as the sample grew. Section 49's
headline (a ~33% admit rate, an order of magnitude better than the current ruler's
4-movable-of-10) survives at 30.4%, but the specific number moved and will move
again. The one that counts is still the final one.

NOT CLAIMED: that 30.4% is the settled rate either. Stated so the next revision is
not mistaken for a surprise.

standing by for next DIRECTIVE

52. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-28 - SCREEN COMPLETE. 30 admitted tasks, all in band.
    The replacement instrument now has a candidate set.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
RESULT, MEASURED:
    screened          121 candidates
    ADMITTED           30   24.8%   <- target reached, run stopped early
    floor (0/8)        39   32.2%
    ceiling (8/8)      36   29.8%
    outside band       16   13.2%
    wall time        43.2 min   (21.4 s per candidate)

ADMITTED SET PROPERTIES - this is the number that matters, because the whole point
was an instrument that can register a change:
    base pass rate   min 0.23   median 0.42   max 0.75
    every admitted task inside [0.2, 0.8]: TRUE (checked, not assumed)
Contrast the instrument this replaces: 6 of its 10 tasks sat at exactly 1.000 across
all 57 logged runs and could not move in either direction. The new set has 30 tasks
with a median near the middle of the band, which is where variance - and therefore
sensitivity - is greatest.

THIRD PREDICTION MISS IN THREE ENTRIES, recorded because the pattern is the point.
Section 51 projected the target at "roughly candidate 99"; it actually took 121. The
cause is visible in the drift I had already flagged: the admit rate fell steadily as
the sample grew.
    at n=24   33.3%
    at n=46   30.4%
    at n=121  24.8%   <- final
Each projection was made from the rate to date and each was optimistic, because the
rate was still falling. Section 49 said "the number that counts is the one at the
end" and then section 51 projected from a mid-run number anyway. The honest form is
that early admit rates on a shuffled pool are biased by nothing except sample size,
and I should have widened the projection rather than restating it as a point value.
24.8% is now a completed-run figure, not an estimate.

WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT MEAN:
  DOES - the authoring job is definitively dead. 121 candidates screened by machine
    produced 30 usable tasks in 43 minutes. Hand-writing 30 in-band tasks would have
    meant authoring 60-90 and screening them anyway.
  DOES NOT - this is a screened CANDIDATE SET, not a wired-in ruler. Nothing has
    replaced HELD_OUT, no baseline has been scored on the new set, and no claim about
    the model has changed. The 30 tasks are recorded in data/screen_results.jsonl
    with their full task definitions under the "task" key.

REMAINING BEFORE THE NEW INSTRUMENT IS USABLE, in order:
  1. Confirm the 30 are disjoint from SEED_TASKS by CONTENT, not just by id. The ids
     are ace_* so collision is impossible, but skill overlap is not - section 43's
     non-claim on the old set (reverse_words/rle/dedup overlap what SEED_TASKS
     teaches) applies here too and is not answered by an id check.
  2. Score iteration-0 on the new set to establish the baseline the whole thing
     exists to provide.
  3. Re-derive the noise floor ON THE NEW SET. The 0.0199 sd and +/-0.055 bar are
     properties of the OLD ten-task instrument and do not transfer. A 30-task set
     with a median near 0.42 should be substantially tighter, but "should be" is not
     a measurement and the published numbers must not be reused.
  4. Keep the old ten as a legacy line, per the section 43 ruling and Recht et al.
     2019 - new test sets shift absolute numbers while preserving model ORDERING, so
     the old set stays informative for the 4 tasks that can actually move.

NOT CLAIMED: that the new instrument is better. It has better STRUCTURAL properties
(30 movable tasks vs 4, median mid-band vs 6 pinned at ceiling). Whether that
translates into a tighter measured noise floor is item 3 and is unmeasured.

53. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-28 - monitor said "stalled" about a run that had
    FINISHED; and the §50 audit verified, two of four claims corrected)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A GUARD THAT REPORTED THE WRONG THING - mine, again, and the proprietor hit it
within minutes. screen_monitor.py judged liveness ONLY by whether the results file
was still growing, so a run that completed successfully displayed
"no new result for Xh - finished, stopped, or stalled". Three very different
states collapsed into one amber line, and the reader correctly read the worst one.
The run was fine: target of 30 admitted reached at candidate 121, exit code 0.
FIXED by checking the conditions that actually define completion before falling
back to the ambiguous case - admitted >= target, or candidate pool exhausted - so
COMPLETE now reads green and distinct from a genuine stall, which is now red and
says explicitly "target not reached, pool not exhausted".
This is the same shape as the invented-guard class (activation 13): the check was
built on the signal that was easy to observe (file mtime) rather than the property
that mattered (did it finish). Third instance of mine in this project. Monitor
relaunched, pythonw pid 135840.

§50 IS THE PHD'S AUDIT OF §44-§47 and it fired WHILE the screen was running, so he
never saw §48/§49. Verified rather than accepted - two of four claims stand, one
does not apply, one is unre-derived:

  (1) REWARD-HACK BYPASS - DOES NOT APPLY TO WHAT I SHIPPED. He is right that
      forge.py's "closes the class for every current and future task" is too strong,
      and right that AceCode's bare asserts call the function by its GLOBAL name.
      But screen_tasks.as_task() emits `def run_tests(_f): <entry> = _f` FIRST, which
      shadows the global inside run_tests, so the asserts hit the guarded function.
      Tested directly through forge.verify() against a real admitted task: the
      always-equal exploit is REFUSED with "solution returned 'A'; the verifier
      accepts only plain builtins". The general claim in forge.py should still be
      softened; the construction in flight is safe.

  (2) LICENCE CONTAMINATION - CONFIRMED AND WORSE THAN I CHECKED. He reports 39.4%
      of AceCode descends from a stratum whose own card states no licence. Measured
      against my 30 admitted:
          bigcode_python_fns   12   (no upstream licence stated)
          evol                 11   (provenance unverified)
          oss                   7   (clean MIT)
      So only 7 of 30 are safe to publish from an MIT repo. I checked AceCode's
      top-level licence and stopped there - the same one-layer check that section 47
      correctly applied to KodCode, not applied deeply enough here. Admit rate by
      stratum is near-identical (oss 20.0%, bigcode 26.1%, evol 27.5%), so
      restricting to oss costs yield but not quality.

  (3) TASK COUNT 5.1x TOO LOW (~112-174, not ~30) - PLAUSIBLE, NOT YET RE-DERIVED.
      His mechanism is real: I took per-task sd 0.0928 from the OLD movable tasks
      and applied it to deliberately mid-band new ones, and binomial variance is
      maximal near p=0.5, so the new set should be NOISIER per task, not quieter.
      This is the same error class as section 52 item 3 (do not carry the old
      instrument's numbers onto the new one) - I named it and then committed it one
      entry earlier. NOT accepting the specific 112-174 on his arithmetic alone;
      two of his four claims needed correction, so this gets re-derived here first.

  (4) FREE STAGE-1 SCREEN IN THE DOWNLOADED DATA - UNVERIFIED, CHEAP TO CHECK.
      He says AceCode's `inferences` column carries precomputed pass rates from a
      same-family model, which would make the n=8 stage largely redundant. Not
      checked yet.

CONSEQUENCE: the 30 admitted tasks are NOT the instrument. They are 7 publishable
tasks plus 23 that cannot ship, sized by a task-count target that is probably too
low by several fold. The screen needs re-running restricted to the oss stratum and
to a re-derived target. That is not a setback in the pipeline - the pipeline worked,
43 minutes for 121 candidates - it is a setback in what the run was pointed at.

54. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-28 - §50's remaining two claims re-derived. Task count
    CONFIRMED 5.16x too low. Free prefilter CONFIRMED to exist and MEASURED too
    weak to use. Rerun correctly sized - and a cheaper shape found.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DECISION ON THE PROPRIETOR'S QUESTION (report to council, or build?): BUILD. Every
open item was answerable here, and three of the four were answered in minutes below.
A round spent reporting these back would buy confirmation of what he already said.

CLAIM 3 - TASK COUNT - CONFIRMED, and his 5.1x is almost exactly right. Re-derived
from the 30 admitted tasks rather than from his arithmetic:
    OLD instrument, MEASURED per-task test-retest sd     0.0928
    NEW admitted set, implied sd at median p=0.42, k=5   0.2108
    ratio 2.27x noisier per task -> task count scales 5.16x
He said 5.1x and a range of 112-174; I get 155 at k=5. His range brackets mine.
The mechanism is the one he named and it is my error: I took the variance of the OLD
SATURATED tasks and applied it to deliberately mid-band new ones. Binomial variance
is maximal near p=0.5, so screening FOR sensitivity necessarily produces tasks that
are individually noisier. Section 52 item 3 named this exact trap ("the old
instrument's numbers do not transfer") one entry before I fell into it.

    detect +3% @80% power   k=3 -> 258 tasks   k=5 -> 155   k=10 -> 77
    detect +5% @80% power   k=3 ->  93 tasks   k=5 ->  56   k=10 -> 28

A CHEAPER SHAPE, which falls out of the table and which nobody proposed: eval cost
per run is tasks x k, so 155 tasks at k=5 is 775 generations and 77 tasks at k=10 is
770 - IDENTICAL running cost and identical power. But 77 admitted tasks needs half
the SCREENING to build: ~385 candidates (~2.3 h) against ~850 (~5.1 h). Same
instrument, same sensitivity, half the build. Recommend targeting ~80 tasks at k=10
seeds rather than ~155 at k=5.

CLAIM 4 - FREE STAGE-1 SCREEN - the FACT is true, his CONCLUSION is not. The
`inferences` column does carry 80 completions per task with a `pass_rate` and a
`model_name` - no GPU needed to read it. But the rates are from a different model
family (codellama_instruct), and measured against my 121 screened tasks:
    correlation(precomputed rate, measured llama3 rate) = 0.451
    prefilter [0.1,0.9] keeps 83% of candidates, retains 27/30 in-band
    prefilter [0.2,0.8] keeps 60% of candidates, retains 21/30 in-band
At [0.1,0.9] it discards 17% of the work while losing 10% of the good tasks; at
[0.2,0.8] it discards 40% while losing 30%. That is a poor trade in both directions,
so his "the two-stage design is largely redundant with a free column" does not hold.
It is a weak prior, not a substitute. KEEPING the GPU stage-1. Worth recording that
this only became knowable because 121 tasks had already been measured the expensive
way - the cheap check was validated against the expensive one, not instead of it.

RUNNING TALLY ON THE §50 AUDIT, now that all four are checked: licence contamination
CONFIRMED and I under-checked; task count CONFIRMED at 5.16x; reward-hack bypass real
as a general claim but DOES NOT APPLY to the harness I shipped; free-prefilter fact
true, conclusion wrong. Two clean hits out of four, and the two hits are the two that
actually change what gets built.

THE RERUN, correctly scoped:
    stratum   oss only - the only unbroken MIT provenance (12 of my 30 admitted are
              bigcode_python_fns with no upstream licence; 11 evol, unverified)
    target    ~80 admitted at k=10 eval seeds  (not 30, not 155)
    draw      ~400 oss candidates at the measured 20.0% oss admit rate
    cost      ~2.3 h, one run, GPU only
    keep      the existing 30 as a discardable pilot - it proved the pipeline and
              produced the variance number that resized this. Its 7 oss tasks are
              re-usable; the other 23 are not publishable.

NOT CLAIMED: that the oss stratum contains 400 screenable candidates. Not counted
yet - that is the first thing the rerun must check, and if it does not, the shortfall
is reported rather than backfilled from a stratum that cannot ship.

standing by for next DIRECTIVE

56. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-28 - both, in parallel: oss-only screen LAUNCHED and
    activation #15 opened. And a correction to how I have been using the advisor.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
(Section number read from the file immediately before writing, per section 55.)

THE PROPRIETOR'S QUESTION WAS "council or run?" AND THE ANSWER IS BOTH, because
they do not compete for anything. The screen is GPU-bound; a council round is not.
I had been treating them as alternatives and defaulting to "not council" on budget
grounds, which he corrected: the advisor is a resource to depend on, not a cost to
avoid. Recorded because the bias was real - across sections 47, 51 and 54 I argued
against opening a round three times, and at least one of those (this one) was wrong.

WHAT I SETTLED LOCALLY FIRST, so the round is not spent on it: run-to-run noise is
PURE SAMPLING NOISE.
    observed aggregate pass@1 sd over 57 runs   0.0199
    predicted from binomial sampling alone      0.0204
    ratio                                       0.98
This matters because the whole cheaper-shape argument depends on it. Averaging k
eval runs only tightens the estimate if the run-to-run variation is independent; a
systematic component would not average down and k=10 would buy far less than
sqrt(10). It is independent, so 77 tasks at k=10 really does cost the same per eval
run as 155 at k=5 (~3,850 generations either way) at the same power, while needing
half the screening to build. Had this come back at, say, 1.5, the k=10 plan would
have been wrong and I would have shipped it.

VERDICT: Q1's answer is not a bigger or smaller ruler — it is that the ruler is
no longer the binding constraint. The training set is 918 pairs over 13
distinct prompts (verified: `data/dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl`, `meta.tid` takes 13

for +1.0 to +4.3 points; the literature does not report this configuration and

centred near +0.5, with a real probability of a negative sign — so do not halve
