R4 - THE HELD-OUT BANK IS SPENT. Measured, not inferred.

Base model (llama3-forged-null, which section 61 confirmed is configurationally
identical to the stock base) on all 10 held-out tasks, 20 samples each, same
actor path / ACTOR_SYSTEM / verifier / temperature policy as eval.py:

     k    pass@k        gcd            20/20     rotate     0/20
     1    0.8700        is_prime       20/20     rle       18/20
     3    0.8999        reverse_words  20/20     nth_fib   17/20
     5    0.9000        flatten        20/20     kadane    19/20
    10    0.9000        count_vowels   20/20
    20    0.9000        dedup          20/20

pass@20 == pass@5 == pass@3 == 0.9000 EXACTLY. Nine of ten tasks are solvable;
rotate is solvable at no sampling budget up to 20; six tasks are at 20/20.

THE CONSEQUENCE, stated as arithmetic. The ceiling on this bank is 0.90. The base
already sits at 0.87 pass@1. So THE ENTIRE MEASURABLE RANGE IS 3 PERCENTAGE
POINTS, against section 62's corrected MDE of ~2.6pp. One detectable step in the
whole instrument. Section 61's held-out null (-0.8pp) was never evidence about
training: this ruler could not have registered more than +3pp if run 1 had been
perfect.

This closes section 62 leftover-risk 5 and gives Yue et al. (arXiv:2504.13837) a
concrete local number. Their claim is that verifier-grounded training re-ranks
what the base already contains. Here the base contains solutions to 9/10 tasks
within 20 samples, so training can only convert latent capability into pass@1,
and there are three points of it to convert. Whether that generalises is their
question, not mine; what is measured here is that MY bank is spent.

R3 IS THEREFORE NOT A JUDGEMENT CALL, IT IS A PRECONDITION. Section 63 reversed
section 58's screen shutdown on section 62's variance argument. R4 replaces the
argument with a measurement. The ruler must be replaced before any run whose
conclusion depends on a held-out number. rotate should not survive the
replacement: 0/20 is not a hard task, it is a dead channel.

R1 - THE TRAINING SEAM IS CLOSED. RED WITNESS, then GREEN, both kept.

  RED, the same assertions run against the pre-fix implementations:
    FAIL test_training_prompt_carries_the_system_turn (roles=['user'])
    FAIL test_training_system_message_is_the_sampling_system_message
         (no system turn at all)
    FAIL test_target_is_fenced_not_bare_source
         (target='def foo(x):\n    return'...)
    3 failed against the old code

  GREEN tests/test_training_seam.py -> 5 passed, 0 failed
        tests/test_screen_sizing.py -> 0 failed, no regression

I wrote the test AFTER the fix, so it started green and proved nothing. I then
ran its assertions against the old implementations to confirm they actually
bite. Stating that because a test written after a fix is decoration until it has
been shown to fail.

WHAT CHANGED, and why the fence half is additive rather than a replacement:
  - train_native.build_training_prompt() now renders system=ACTOR_SYSTEM + user,
    the same two turns forge.Actor.generate posts when sampling and eval.py
    posts when scoring.
  - forge.Result gains `raw`, defaulting to "" so every existing
    Result(code=...) call site is unaffected. run_task threads the verbatim
    completion into it.
  - forge writes chosen_raw / rejected_raw ALONGSIDE chosen / rejected.
    chosen/rejected MUST stay executable source because clean_dataset.py:26,
    verify_dataset.py:82 and both dedup hashes run forge.verify on them.
    Replacing them with fenced text would have broken all three gates. Additive
    on purpose.
  - train_native.training_target() prefers the verbatim completion and falls
    back to re-fencing the stored source for the 918 legacy pairs. THE FALLBACK
    IS A RECONSTRUCTION, NOT THE ORIGINAL BYTES - close, because ACTOR_SYSTEM
    forbids prose, but not identical. Pairs generated after this change carry
    the real thing.

  SCOPE: the test pins the two mismatches actually found. A third divergence
  introduced later is NOT caught automatically. Enumerated, not swept. The test
  uses a recording stand-in for the tokenizer, so it runs on plain python with
  no transformers, no download, no GPU.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO. It does not re-run anything. Every number banked in
sections 60, 61 and 63 was produced under the broken seam and none of them are
retro-corrected by this commit - they remain the record of what that
configuration did. The next run measures the fixed configuration, and R2 holds
prompt count at 13 precisely so that the seam is the only thing that moved.

FIRST LAUNCH OF THE RULER-REPLACEMENT SCREEN FAILED IMMEDIATELY.

  python screen_tasks.py --stratum oss --candidates 500 --target 78

ROOT CAUSE, measured rather than guessed:
  default `python`              3.14.6   pyarrow ABSENT
  .venv-train/Scripts/python    3.11.9   pyarrow 25.0.0, requests present

screen_tasks.py reads AceCode's parquet shards, so it needs pyarrow, which lives
only in .venv-train. The repo's default interpreter is 3.14 because that is the
system Python; the training stack cannot use it, which is why .venv-train exists
at 3.11 in the first place. Nothing in screen_tasks.py said so, and the failure
surfaced a hundred lines in, from inside candidates(), AFTER argparse had
accepted every flag and printed nothing.

THE CLASS, and it was already solved elsewhere in this repo. export_adapter.py
carries _reexec_in_venv_if_needed() for exactly this reason - its own docstring
says "rather than fail on an import three steps in, hand off to the right
interpreter immediately. Same lesson as sync_public.py's test gate." So this is
the THIRD script to need the guard and the first two already had it. I did not
invent a new mechanism; I copied the one that was there (build from what's
there), keyed on pyarrow instead of torch/gguf/safetensors.

  RED   python screen_tasks.py --stratum oss --candidates 500 --target 78
        -> ModuleNotFoundError from inside candidates(), 0 rows written
  GREEN python screen_tasks.py --help
        -> re-execs into .venv-train and prints usage, exit 0
        tests/test_screen_sizing.py -> 0 failed, no regression

  SCOPE: the guard keys on pyarrow specifically. A future import that exists in
  neither interpreter still fails the old way, and a fourth script needing the
  same treatment is not fixed automatically. Enumerated, not swept. What would
  actually close the class is one shared entry-point helper the scripts import
  rather than three copies of the same function - noted as an open item, not
  done, because two copies existed before I arrived and a third does not justify
  a refactor of all three mid-run.

RELAUNCHED under .venv-train, same arguments, resuming against the 24 rows
already on disk (the screen skips already-screened ids, so the earlier partial
oss draw is reused rather than redrawn). Running now; its result gets its own
section.

WHY THIS IS A SECTION AT ALL. Proprietor ruling 2026-07-29: one run, one
numbered section, after every run rather than at milestones - and section 64 was
named as the violation that prompted it, having folded R4 and R1 together. A
failed launch is a run: it produced evidence (the interpreter split), it changed
the repo (the guard), and a future session that hits ModuleNotFoundError here
should find this rather than rediscover it.

NOT CHECKED: whether the other scripts in this repo that touch parquet, torch or
gguf all carry the guard. I checked export_adapter.py (has it) and screen_tasks.py

66. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - section 62's double-BOS non-claim CLOSED, and
    the answer is NEGATIVE: TRL 0.12.2 does not double-add for this model. The
    seam fix in section 63 is not compounded.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 62 left this open: "I did not verify whether TRL 0.12.2's DPOTrainer
double-adds a BOS token on top of apply_chat_template's output, which is a known
class of bug in that vintage. That would require running the tokenizer.
Unchecked, and it would compound finding 2."

It would compound it because apply_chat_template already emits
<|begin_of_text|>. A second BOS would give every training sequence a prefix no
sampled sequence ever carried - the same defect class section 63 just closed,
one layer down. Ran the tokenizer. CPU only, no GPU, no weights, no writes.

THE HAZARD IS REAL AT THE TOKENIZER LEVEL:

  bos_token='<|begin_of_text|>' id=128000
  build_training_prompt() output starts with bos text : True
  tok(prompt)                            -> first ids [128000, 128000, 128006]  BOS count 2
  tok(prompt, add_special_tokens=False)  -> first ids [128000, 128006,   9125]  BOS count 1

So if anything tokenizes our prompt with library defaults, it DOES double-add.
The question was only ever whether TRL does.

IT DOES NOT, for this model. Read from the installed source, trl 0.12.2:

  dpo_trainer.py:580-582   prompt/chosen/rejected are each tokenized with
                           add_special_tokens=False
  dpo_trainer.py:585       if add_special_tokens:
                               prompt_input_ids = [tokenizer.bos_token_id] + prompt_input_ids
  dpo_trainer.py:473       "add_special_tokens": self.is_encoder_decoder

and for our base:

  unsloth/llama-3-8b-Instruct-bnb-4bit
    architectures      ['LlamaForCausalLM']
    is_encoder_decoder  False

So TRL binds add_special_tokens=False, the manual BOS-prepend branch never runs,
and the explicit add_special_tokens=False on all three fields prevents the
tokenizer from adding one either. Exactly one BOS reaches the model, supplied by
apply_chat_template, which is what the sampler also sent.

VERDICT: NEGATIVE FINDING. No defect. Section 63's seam fix stands unqualified -
the training sequence now matches the sampled sequence at the BOS/system-turn
level, and nothing downstream re-breaks it.

WHY A NEGATIVE FINDING GETS ITS OWN SECTION. Because it was a live open item that
would have invalidated the next run's interpretation, and "we checked and it was
fine" is only worth anything if the check is on the record with its evidence. An
unchecked hazard and a checked-clean hazard look identical in a summary and are
not the same thing. Also the one-run-one-section ruling does not have a
"good news" exemption.

SCOPE, and it is narrow. This is true for THIS model (decoder-only) and THIS TRL
version (0.12.2). A future encoder-decoder base would set is_encoder_decoder=True
and the branch WOULD fire on top of our template's BOS - so the safe reading is
not "TRL never double-adds", it is "TRL double-adds exactly when
is_encoder_decoder is True, and ours is False". A TRL upgrade re-opens this; the
binding at line 473 is the thing to re-read, not the conclusion.

NOT CHECKED: whether the EOS handling has an analogous issue (TRL appends
eos_token to completions; our chosen/rejected now end with a ``` fence rather
than <|eot_id|>, and whether that matters is unmeasured). Whether
max_prompt_length truncation interacts with the now-longer system-turn prompt -
section 62's A2 measured the OLD prompt at ~112 tokens against a 512 budget, and
the system turn adds ~47, so the headroom is still large but the measurement is
now stale by construction.

Screen run 2 continues in parallel: 33 screened, 8 admitted at the time of
writing. Its result gets its own section.

standing by

67. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - section 65's open item bit within the hour. The
    venv-guard class is now actually closed: one implementation, three callers.
    build_ruler.py added and nominating.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 65 SAID: "What would actually close the class is one shared entry-point
helper the scripts import rather than three copies of the same function - noted
as an open item, not done, because two copies existed before I arrived and a
third does not justify a refactor of all three mid-run."

That reasoning was wrong and it cost the next hour.

RED, and it is the copy failing exactly where a copy fails. build_ruler.py
imported screen_tasks' guard to avoid writing a fourth one:

  python build_ruler.py nominate
  usage: screen_tasks.py [-h] [--candidates ...] [--stratum STRATUM]
  screen_tasks.py: error: unrecognized arguments: nominate

The guard re-executes `me = Path(__file__).resolve()`, and inside screen_tasks.py
that is screen_tasks.py. So importing it made build_ruler re-launch a DIFFERENT
script with build_ruler's arguments. The function was correct in its own file and
wrong the instant it was reused, because it had a caller baked into it. That is
the tell that duplication here is not a style preference: a helper that only
knows its own filename cannot be shared, so every new script must copy it, so the
copies keep accumulating.

GREEN. venv_guard.py, one implementation, caller passed as a parameter:

    venv_guard.ensure(__file__, "pyarrow")

  screen_tasks     --help  OK (exit 0)
  export_adapter   --help  OK (exit 0)
  build_ruler      --help  OK (exit 0)
  grep for guard implementations across the repo -> exactly one:
      ./venv_guard.py:38:def ensure(caller, *modules, install_hint="")
  tests/test_screen_sizing.py  0 failed
  tests/test_training_seam.py  0 failed

export_adapter.py was migrated too rather than left as the last copy. It was
working, and leaving it would have preserved the exact condition that produced
this bug. It uses find_spec rather than import, so proving torch exists no longer
loads it.

BUILD_RULER.PY ADDED, and NOMINATE RUN (CPU only, no GPU, screen still running):

  admitted in band : 46
    -> ruler side  : 17
    -> train side  : 29   (complement, reserved)
  1 of 17 nominees sits >0.25 from band centre 0.5

TWO THINGS IT IS DELIBERATELY CAREFUL ABOUT:

  POOL DISJOINTNESS. The screened band is the natural source for BOTH the ruler
  and future training prompts. If a task can land in both, the ruler stops being
  held out. Split is sha256(tid)-based, so it is deterministic, reproducible, and
  independent of draw order and pool size - a later training draw takes the
  complement with no coordination. Verified over 2,000 synthetic tids: 50.1%
  ruler, stable across calls.

  SELECTION ON NOISE (section 62 leftover-risk 6). The screen admits on a stage-2
  estimate at n=40, se ~= 0.077 at p=0.5, so some admitted tasks have a TRUE rate
  outside the band and would behave as partial floor/ceiling tasks in the ruler -
  re-importing the exact bias the ruler exists to escape. Admission here is
  therefore a NOMINATION, not a decision. `build_ruler.py confirm --n 60`
  re-measures every nominee and keeps only those still in band. Regression to the
  mean is expected; the dropped set is reported rather than hidden, because its
  average movement IS the selection error made visible.

CAVEAT ON THE CURRENT SPLIT: 17/46 is 37%, not the intended 50%. At n=46 that is
~1.8 sd from expectation, so it is small-sample variation rather than a broken
hash - the function measures 50.1% over 2,000 ids. The ruler side should even out
as the screen fills toward 78 admitted. Re-run `nominate` when the screen
finishes; these numbers are preliminary by construction.

NOT DONE: `confirm` has not run - it needs the GPU and the screen is using it.
The ruler is nominated, not confirmed, and nothing may be measured against it
yet.

standing by

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE RUN WAS KILLED, NOT COMPLETED. Reported by the harness as `killed` with no
exit code, at ~04:12 after ~57 minutes.

WHAT I CHECKED, and it rules things out rather than in:
  council/STOP        absent (so not the project's own stop mechanism)
  traceback in log    none
  OOM / CUDA error    none
  surviving process   none
  GPU at the time     5,794 MiB, 2% utilisation - idle, not thrashing

I DO NOT KNOW WHY IT DIED. It ran 57 minutes; the background jobs that completed
normally today were 14 minutes and under. A lifetime limit on backgrounded
commands is the obvious hypothesis and I have NOT tested it. Recording it as
unknown rather than guessing, because "probably a timeout" would become a fact
by repetition and the next person would stop looking.

THE LOG LIED ABOUT PROGRESS, and this is the part worth carrying forward. The
task's captured stdout ends at:

  [83/476] ace_oss_31842   stage1 0/8 - at floor   admitted=27 (25.5m)

while data/screen_results.jsonl held 210 rows written a minute earlier. Python
buffers stdout when it is redirected to a file; the JSONL is flushed per row. So
the log ran ~127 candidates behind reality and anyone reading it would have
concluded the run was less than half as far along as it was.

This matters beyond one run: every progress judgement I made from that file
during the run was wrong, and the monitor I attached to it was reading the same
stale stream. The fix on relaunch is `python -u`. The general lesson is that a
buffered log is not a measurement of progress - the artifact the job WRITES is.
Same shape as the rest of today: I trusted a signal that was aimed slightly to
the side of the thing I wanted to know.

DATA INTEGRITY - VERIFIED, not assumed:
  screened            210
  admitted             49   (23.3%)
  duplicate tids        0
  all ace_oss_*      True
  final row parses   True

Nothing was lost. screen_tasks.py appends per row and skips already-screened ids
on restart, so the kill cost wall time only. That resumability was designed in
before today and it is the reason this is an inconvenience rather than an
incident.

RESUMED with `-u` this time, same arguments, from 210/49. 29 more admits needed;
at the measured 23.3% admit rate that is ~124 candidates, ~37 minutes. If it is
killed again at the same elapsed mark, that confirms the lifetime hypothesis and
the answer is to detach it from the harness the way overnight.ps1 already does
for long jobs.

ADMIT RATE HAS SETTLED at 23.3% over 210 candidates, against the 20.0% measured
on the pilot's n=35 and the 29.2% that the first 24 rows suggested. The pilot's
Wilson interval was [10.0%, 35.9%]; 23.3% sits inside it, and 210 candidates is
now a far better estimate than either earlier figure. Section 62 finding 8's
supply argument holds comfortably: 500 drawn from 25,862 eligible rows, using
1.9% of the stratum, with no scarcity anywhere near.

Completed on the third launch, detached from the harness via Start-Process after

  screened            343
  admitted             78   (22.7%)
  duplicate tids        0

  [screen] done. 78 admitted -> data/screen_results.jsonl

WHY THE THIRD LAUNCH WORKED. Not a fix to the screen - a change of custody. The
first two runs were harness background tasks and were killed at 57 minutes and
1.7 minutes respectively, which rules out a lifetime limit; no git hooks exist,
which rules out the commit-correlation theory. Cause still UNKNOWN. Rather than
keep testing hypotheses against a 35-minute job, the run was detached with
Start-Process the way overnight.ps1 already handles long jobs in this project.
That pattern existed before today and should have been the first choice.

THE COMPLETION BANNER IS ITSELF A CHECK ON SECTION 65. It printed:

  [screen] detect 3.0pp: needs 78 tasks at k=10, or 156 at k=5 (3,889 gens/arm)
  [screen] detect 5.0pp: needs 28 tasks at k=10, or 56 at k=5 (1,400 gens/arm)
  [screen] all of the above assume run-to-run noise is pure sampling. The new
           instrument's noise floor is UNMEASURED - measure it before trusting
           any large-k row.

That is computed from the single SIZING block, not retyped, and it carries its
own caveat. The superseded "~30 admitted tasks / +3% at k=5" text that stood in
three places this morning is gone from the running output.

ADMIT RATE, final: 22.7% over 343 candidates. The pilot measured 20.0% at n=35
with Wilson [10.0%, 35.9%]; the first 24 rows of this run suggested 29.2%. Both
were inside the interval and both were worse estimates than this one. Section 62
finding 8's supply argument holds with room to spare: 343 candidates drawn
against 25,862 eligible oss rows, 1.3% of the stratum consumed.

WHAT THIS IS AND IS NOT. It is a POOL of 78 tasks whose base-model pass rate was
measured inside [0.2, 0.8] - every one can move in both directions, which is
exactly what the old 10-task bank could not do (section 64: pass@20 == pass@3 ==
0.9000, two live channels). It is NOT a ruler yet. Two things stand between:

  1. The band membership is a stage-2 estimate at n=40, se ~= 0.077 at p=0.5. Some
     of these 78 have a true rate outside the band and will regress out on
     re-measurement. Freezing them into a ruler imports the selection error the
     ruler exists to escape (section 62 leftover-risk 6).
  2. The pool must be split so ruler tasks and future training prompts never
     overlap, or the ruler is not held out.

build_ruler.py does both. Nothing may be measured against these 78 until
`confirm` has run, and the count after confirmation will be lower than 78 by
construction.

NOT CHECKED: whether the 78 overlap the pilot's admitted 30 (screen_tasks.py
reserves forge.SEED_TASKS and eval.HELD_OUT tids by construction, but the pilot's
admitted set is not in either list and I have not diffed it).

70. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - RULER CONFIRMED. 31 of 35 nominees survived
    re-measurement. Selection-on-noise is now MEASURED, not assumed - and the
    number it produced validates the mechanism that predicted it.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
build_ruler.py confirm --n 60, run detached, 35 nominees re-measured against the
same base model that screened them.

  kept 31 / dropped 4   (11.4% regressed out of band)

THE SELECTION-ON-NOISE NUMBER, which this channel has reasoned about since
section 50 finding 5 and never measured:

  movement, screen(n=40) -> confirm(n=60), all 35 nominees
    mean |move|   0.0755      median 0.0667      max 0.2667
  dropped-only mean |move|  0.1313

AND IT IS CONSISTENT WITH PURE SAMPLING. Predicted se at n=40, p=0.5 is 0.0791;
at n=60, 0.0645; the sd of a difference of two such independent estimates is
0.1021. For a normal, E|X| = sd*sqrt(2/pi) = 0.0815. Observed mean |move| is
0.0755. So the drift between the two measurements is what independent
re-sampling predicts and nothing more - there is no systematic term hiding in
the screen's stage-2 estimate. That is a real check on the screening
instrument, not just on the tasks.

ALL FOUR DROPS WENT UP AND OUT THE TOP:
    ace_oss_20465   0.72 -> 0.85
    ace_oss_23467   0.80 -> 0.87
    ace_oss_37667   0.70 -> 0.88
    ace_oss_5663    0.75 -> 0.90
None regressed out the bottom. That is the expected SHAPE of selection on noise -
a task whose true rate is above 0.8 only enters the band when its n=40 estimate
lands low, and re-measurement moves it back toward truth - but the asymmetry
(4 top, 0 bottom) is on four events and I am not reading anything into the
direction. What matters is that the mechanism fired and was caught before these
tasks became a ruler.

VARIANCE ASSUMPTION HOLDS. mean p(1-p) over the 31 confirmed = 0.2190, against
the 0.2230 the SIZING block derives its generation counts from (section 54, from
the pilot's admitted set). The sizing model's central assumption survives contact
with an independently screened, independently confirmed task set. That was not
guaranteed.

THE NEW RULER vs THE OLD BANK. Predicted, NOT measured - stated that way
deliberately, because section 62 C4 caught me presenting an n=5 variance estimate
as fact and this is worse, it is a theoretical projection under an independence
assumption that section 62 A1b already showed is violated on the held-in bank:

  NEW (predicted, T=31, mean p(1-p)=0.2190, N_SAMPLES=5, independence assumed)
    per-task sd 0.2093 -> run-level sd 0.0376
      k=5    se_diff 0.0238   MDE@80% ~6.7pp     775 gens/arm
      k=10   se_diff 0.0168   MDE@80% ~4.7pp   1,550 gens/arm
      k=20   se_diff 0.0119   MDE@80% ~3.3pp   3,100 gens/arm
      k=25   se_diff 0.0106   MDE@80% ~3.0pp   3,875 gens/arm

  OLD (measured, sections 61/64)
      se_diff 0.0080, MDE ~2.6pp - and MAXIMUM POSSIBLE EFFECT 3.0pp, because
      7 of 10 tasks sat at ceiling, 1 at floor, pass@20 == pass@3 == 0.9000.

The old bank looks better on MDE and that comparison is a trap. Its precision was
bought by freezing 80% of itself; a stuck needle reads steady. The new ruler is
noisier per run and has 31 live channels instead of 2, so the effects it can
register are not capped at three points. Section 62's own words: "a low noise
floor bought by freezing 80% of the bank is not sensitivity - it is a stuck
needle."

THE HONEST COST: at k=5 the new ruler resolves ~6.7pp, which is WORSE than what
the next run needs. Reaching 3pp needs k=25, i.e. 3,875 generations per arm,
which matches the SIZING block's 3,889 for 3pp almost exactly - as it must, since
both come from the same formula. So the instrument is buildable and the price is
about an hour of generation per arm. That price was always there; the old bank
hid it by being unable to move.

STATUS: CONFIRMED BAND, NOT A FROZEN RULER. Freezing means pinning these 31 tids
and never screening against them again. Not done yet, deliberately - freezing is
irreversible in the sense that matters (a task that has been looked at cannot be
un-looked-at) and it should happen in the same act as declaring the training
split, so the disjointness is recorded once.

NOT CHECKED: the predicted run-level sd assumes per-task independence within a
run. Section 62 A1b measured that assumption failing on the held-in bank -
observed sd was 2.9x BELOW the independence prediction because per-task
deviations partly cancel. If the same holds here the real MDE is better than the
table above; if it fails the other way it is worse. UNMEASURED. Measuring it
costs 10 runs of the null arm and should happen before any k is chosen.

71. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - noise floor measured, and the measurement found a
    defect bigger than the question it was asked. forge.verify() inherited its
    interpreter from the caller, so GROUND TRUTH depended on which script ran.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 70's instruction was "measure it before any k is chosen", 10 null-arm
replicates. Ran, detached via Start-Process per section 69, `python -u` per
section 68. 31 tasks x 5 samples x 10 runs = 1,550 generations, 18.5 min, 0
generation errors. Evidence: data/ruler_noise.jsonl,
council/ruler_noise_analysis_prepin.txt.

THE ANSWER TO THE ASK, first, because it is the smaller half of this section:

  observed run-level sd   0.0222   95% CI [0.0153, 0.0405]  (chi-square, df=9)
  section 70's prediction 0.0376   ratio 0.59x

AND THE CI CONTAINS 0.0376. Ten replicates site the number and do NOT establish
the ratio. The shortfall is a DIRECTION, not a fact, and it is labelled that way
rather than banking 0.59x. Section 70 prescribed 10 runs; 10 runs buys 9 degrees
of freedom, which was enough to answer "roughly where" and not enough to answer
"is it really under". That is a limit of the plan, discovered by executing it.

Decomposition, which is why this was built rather than reporting one ratio:
  0.0376 assumed -> 0.0339 using these runs' own marginals
                 -> 0.0304 minus the greedy draw, CONSTANT on 31/31 tasks; the
                    predicted size of that step was sqrt(4/5)=0.894x and the
                    measured step was 1/1.118=0.894x
                 -> 0.0320 using observed per-task variances
                 -> 0.0222 observed; residual is negative covariance, mean
                    pairwise correlation -0.0147
The greedy step landing on its prediction matters: tests/test_ruler_noise.py
asserts that same 0.894x on synthetic data with a frozen greedy draw, so the
analyzer was checked against known answers before being pointed at an unknown
one. Seven scenarios pass. One FAILED first, and it was my error not the code's:
a balanced +/- latent gives genuine negative covariance and NO shortfall, because
it inflates the marginals by exactly what the covariance removes. Negative
covariance alone does not imply an observed sd below the binomial prediction;
only negative covariance at UNINFLATED marginals does. That was backwards for one
round and both cases now ship as tests.

NOW THE PART THAT MATTERS. The same run reported 9 of 31 tasks OUTSIDE [0.2,0.8]
under the eval instrument and 3 DEAD CHANNELS pinned at 1.000 across all 10 runs.
That is the disease the new ruler exists to escape (section 64: 7 of 10 at
ceiling), reappearing in a set confirmed in band six hours earlier.

Chased in this order, and two of three hypotheses were WRONG:

  H1 the eval instrument polarizes rates. CONFIRMED, and it is an arithmetic
     identity rather than an effect: eval rate == 0.2*greedy + 0.8*temp0.8 to
     0.000000 across all 31 tasks. Greedy is deterministic, pinned at 1.0 on
     22/31 and 0.0 on 9/31, so 20% of every task's score is a constant that
     pushes 22 tasks up and 9 down. Real, and NOT sufficient - it cannot move a
     task confirmed at 0.433 to 1.000.
  H2 selection-on-noise at the CONFIRM stage - the same class section 70 found
     one level down, kept tasks being those whose n=60 estimate landed in band.
     TESTED AND REJECTED. It predicts the shift correlates POSITIVELY with the
     confirmed rate; measured corr(confirmed-0.5, shift) = -0.1341, the sign of
     ordinary regression to the mean. A tidy story the data declined.
  H3 confirm measured different task objects. build_ruler.confirm rebuilds tasks
     from candidates(--draw 500 --seed 1337) while ruler_noise reads the payload
     screen_tasks stored per admitted row. REJECTED by direct comparison: 0 of
     31 payloads differ, 0 missing from the draw.

What remained was the temp-0.8 half disagreeing with confirm about the SAME
quantity: +0.0710 mean paired shift, t=+2.08, max |dev| 0.6667, same code path,
same options. So the code path got read instead of theorised about, and
forge.Actor.generate is stateless - identical POST, identical {"temperature",
"num_ctx": 2048}. Which left the verifier.

    forge.verify() ran  [sys.executable, "-I", path]

GROUND TRUTH WAS INHERITED FROM WHOEVER CALLED IT. screen_tasks.py and
build_ruler.py call venv_guard.ensure(__file__, "pyarrow"); system Python has no
pyarrow (checked: find_spec -> None), so they ALWAYS re-exec under .venv-train,
Python 3.11.9. eval.py, ruler_noise.py and forge.py have no guard and run under
system Python 3.14.6. The screen that admitted this ruler and the eval that
scores it were verifying against different Pythons, and no artifact said so.

RED WITNESS. 310 completions banked ONCE and replayed through verify() under both
launchers - identical code bytes, identical task objects, generation noise removed
entirely:

  before the pin   launcher 3.14 -> 172/310 = 0.5548
                   launcher 3.11 -> 154/310 = 0.4968     18 disagreements
  after the pin    launcher 3.14 -> 154/310
                   launcher 3.11 -> 154/310               0 disagreements

All 18 ran ONE WAY (3.14 passes, 3.11 fails), on 6 of 31 tasks. Cause is PEP
649/749 deferred annotation evaluation. Minimal probe:

  def f(x: List[int]) -> Tuple[int, int]: return (1, 2)
  3.11 -> NameError: name 'List' is not defined      3.14 -> DEFINED_OK

The AceCode oss prompts carry annotated signatures, so the model echoes
List/Tuple/Dict/Union without importing them. The 13 SEED_TASKS are bare
functions (gcd, is_prime, two_sum) and never trip it, which is exactly why this
stayed invisible until the ruler was built from imported tasks. It has been
latent for as long as both interpreters have been in use here.

MEASURED CONSEQUENCE: 5 tasks sitting at 0.60-0.80 under 3.11 become dead
channels at 1.000 under 3.14; tasks outside the band go 2/31 -> 7/31. So the
"9 of 31 out of band" first read as a property of the ruler was mostly a property
of the verifier.

FIXED structurally, and the choice is stated rather than defaulted. VERIFY_PY is
resolved in ONE place - dataset_gate.py, the only module both interpreters already
import, stdlib-only by its own docstring - and forge.py imports it rather than
re-deriving it, because a second copy of "which python verifies" is the same shape
as the copied venv guard that broke build_ruler within an hour (section 65/67).
Default is .venv-train NOT because it saves the ruler (that is the
reasoning-that-saves-work tell) but because unimported annotations do not run on
any Python before 3.14, so accepting them rewards code broken nearly everywhere,
and because .venv-train is what the training stack runs on. SRLM_VERIFY_PY
re-pins it deliberately.

SWEPT THE CONSUMER. dataset_gate's own comment already named this failure -
"every data hash still matches while the *meaning* of 'verified' silently
changes" - so the receipt's `verifier` entry now carries the interpreter beside
the forge.py hash, SCHEMA 3 -> 4, and the gate's existing equality check covers it
for free. Witnessed: the gate refused the stale receipt with "GATE: receipt schema
is 3, this code needs 4."

CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED - the pin does not invalidate the training data:
  "PASS: 1269/1269 pairs valid", 0 violations across dpo_pairs.jsonl (1234 rows),
  dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl (918), repair_pairs.jsonl (38), 24.9s.
The stricter reading costs nothing on the dataset side, measured under the pin
rather than inherited from the pre-pin run.

THE 10 REPLICATES ABOVE ARE VOID as a description of the pinned instrument. They
were scored by the permissive verifier, on a set it had turned into 3 dead
channels. They stay in the artifact as evidence and are NOT poolable: ruler_noise
measure/analyze refuse to mix rows across verifier versions, and eval.evaluate now
records the fingerprint so a row without it is identifiable as pre-pin.
Re-measured in section 72.

WHAT WENT WRONG HERE, recorded because section 70's method note asked for exactly
this: this section opened with a tidy selection-bias story (H2) that fit the first
four numbers and was false. What killed it was one correlation computable before
the story was written. Section 70 said "check how many times you measured it";
the addition is "check the SIGN your explanation predicts, before believing it."

standing by

72. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - re-measured under the pinned verifier. The
    diagnosis is confirmed by the thing that should confirm it: the epoch
    discrepancy collapsed from +0.0710 to +0.0024. Ruler FROZEN.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Same command, same 31 tasks, same detached launch, verifier now pinned to
python 3.11.9. 1,550 generations, 20.7 min, 0 generation errors. Evidence:
data/ruler_noise.jsonl (rows 11-20), council/ruler_noise_analysis_pinned.txt.

THE PIN IS CONFIRMED BY A PREDICTION, not by the number improving. The temp-0.8
half of the eval instrument and build_ruler.confirm measure the SAME quantity by
the same code path, so with the verifier fixed they must agree. They do:

                                   pre-pin        pinned
  temp-0.8 half minus confirmed    +0.0710        +0.0024
  max |per-task deviation|          0.6667         0.2500
  dead channels (pinned 0 or 1)      3              0
  tasks outside [0.2,0.8]            9/31           3/31

A +0.0710 systematic gap became +0.0024. That is the cleanest evidence available
that the interpreter was the cause and not a co-symptom: nothing else changed
between the two runs.

THE NOISE FLOOR, for real this time:

  observed run-level sd   0.0283   95% CI [0.0195, 0.0516]  (chi-square, df=9)
  section 70's prediction 0.0376   ratio 0.75x
  per-run pass@1: 0.5419 0.4710 0.4903 0.4839 0.5548 0.5355 0.5226 0.5226
                  0.5032 0.5419

THE CI STILL CONTAINS 0.0376, so the same caveat stands and is not softened: the
independence prediction is not established as too pessimistic. Direction
favourable, ratio unproven. Decomposition:
  0.0376 -> 0.0363 own marginals -> 0.0324 greedy constant (31/31 again)
         -> 0.0320 observed marginals -> 0.0283 observed
  residual negative covariance, mean pairwise correlation -0.0072 (was -0.0147)

WHAT IT DOES TO SECTION 70'S TABLE, at the point estimate and at the CI's
pessimistic end, because quoting only the first would be picking the number that
flatters the instrument:

     k    se_diff   MDE@80%   gens/arm    MDE at sd=0.0516
     5     0.0179     5.0pp        775          9.1pp
    10     0.0126     3.5pp      1,550          6.5pp
    20     0.0089     2.5pp      3,100          4.6pp
    25     0.0080     2.2pp      3,875          4.1pp

So 3pp needs k=20 (3,100 gens/arm, ~40 min/arm) on the point estimate, and is NOT
reachable at any listed k if the true sd sits at the top of the interval. Section
70 priced 3pp at k=25/3,875; the pinned measurement makes that slightly cheaper
and leaves the uncertainty on the sd, not on the formula. NO k IS CHOSEN HERE -
the sd's own interval is too wide to spend 3,100 generations against, and
narrowing it is cheap (2.1 min per extra replicate; ~40 replicates would halve the
interval). That is the next measurement, not the next guess.

RULER FROZEN - and this is the act section 70 deferred, done as section 70
specified: pinning and declaring the training split in ONE operation so
disjointness is recorded once. `build_ruler.py freeze` ->
data/ruler_frozen.json.

  ruler                31 tasks
  ruler set sha256     74560a4c514587c1ab9094a15cbce960d0838759bc4b481aae2b01f637e8413d
  training pool        43 tids (the split_side complement)
  excluded from both    4 dropped nominees
  [ok] every ruler tid has a stored task payload
  [ok] ruler and training pool are disjoint
  [ok] every ruler tid hashes to the ruler side
  [ok] every training tid hashes to the train side
  [ok] no tid collides with forge.SEED_TASKS or eval.HELD_OUT

IT PINS THE TASKS, NOT THE TIDS. A tid is a pointer; if the payload can change,
nothing is frozen. Every prompt, entry point and test body is hashed individually
plus a set-level hash. Section 71's whole finding was that "verified" can change
meaning while every name stays the same, so the frozen artifact also records the
eval-instrument rate per task beside the confirmed rate.

THE FOUR DROPPED NOMINEES GO NOWHERE, deliberately. They sit on the ruler side of
split_side() but failed confirmation. Moving them to training is the obvious
tidy-up and it would break the one property the sha256 split exists for - that a
later training draw takes the complement with NO coordination and no exception
list. An exception list is a second source of truth about sides.

THREE FROZEN TASKS SIT OUTSIDE THE BAND under the eval instrument and are frozen
anyway, flagged in the artifact rather than quietly dropped:
  ace_oss_16070   confirmed 0.783 -> eval 0.960   (greedy 1.00, temp0.8 0.95)
  ace_oss_19459   confirmed 0.783 -> eval 0.820   (greedy 1.00, temp0.8 0.78)
  ace_oss_24748   confirmed 0.283 -> eval 0.120   (greedy 0.00, temp0.8 0.15)
Dropping them would select on the very measurement being reported, which is the
regress section 71 H2 was testing for; each drop-and-remeasure round biases the
next. So the ruler is 28 clearly-live channels plus 3 weak ones, stated, rather
than 31 clean ones claimed. ace_oss_16070 at 0.960 is nearly a dead channel and
should be treated as carrying almost no signal.

ALSO CLOSED: the noise-floor status was carried in TWO places in screen_tasks.py
(the SIZING comment and the completion banner) - one superseded claim, two chances
to go stale, the same class section 67 closed for the sizing figures. It is now
defined once in NOISE_FLOOR and the banner COMPUTES its caveat, including whether
the CI still contains the assumed value. tests/test_screen_sizing.py gained both
retired strings and a check that the measured sd literal appears exactly once.

STILL OPEN, and the first two are load-bearing for any efficacy claim:
  1. se_diff = sqrt(2)*run_sd assumes BOTH ARMS have the same run-level sd. Only
     the NULL arm was measured. A trained arm could be noisier; nothing here
     bounds that. Measuring it needs a trained artifact, so it cannot precede
     training - which means the first efficacy run must report its own arm sd
     rather than borrowing this one.
  2. The sd's 95% CI spans [0.0195, 0.0516], a factor of 2.6. Every MDE above
     inherits that. ~40 replicates would halve it, at 2.1 min each.
  3. Whether the 78 admitted overlap the pilot's admitted 30 - still not diffed
     (carried from section 69).
  4. The greedy anchor costs 20% of every score as a deterministic constant and
     polarizes the rate distribution. It buys a 0.894x variance reduction and it
     is what pushes 16070 to 0.960. Whether eval.py should sample all 5 at
     temp 0.8 is a real design question this section does not settle; changing it
     would re-baseline every eval_history row, so it is named, not done.
  5. Cross-version verifier divergence beyond the annotation case is
     UNENUMERATED. tests/test_verifier_pin.py is a fixed probe for the one
     mechanism found plus a structural check that the interpreter is no longer
     inherited. It is a flashlight, not a fence.

standing by

73. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - RECORD CORRECTION. Section 72's noise floor was
    wrong in the OPTIMISTIC direction. At 40 replicates independence holds
    exactly: 1.01x. Section 70's sizing table was right as published.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 72 closed by saying the sd's interval was too wide to spend 3,100
generations against and that narrowing it was the next measurement, not the next
guess. Ran it: 30 more null-arm replicates under the pinned verifier, 40 total,
62.5 min, 0 generation errors. Evidence: data/ruler_noise.jsonl,
council/ruler_noise_analysis_n40.txt.

THE CORRECTION, stated before anything else:

                          n=10 (section 72)      n=40 (this section)
  run-level sd            0.0283                 0.0381
  95% CI                  [0.0195, 0.0516]       [0.0312, 0.0489]
  ratio to prediction     0.75x                  1.01x
  residual covariance     -0.0072 cancellation   +0.0102 reinforcement

BOTH the shortfall and the SIGN of its residual were small-sample noise. Section
72 reported a 25% precision bonus and a cancellation effect; neither exists. The
independence prediction of 0.0376 that section 70 derived is right to within 1%,
and the mean pairwise correlation is very slightly POSITIVE, which is the opposite
of what section 62 A1b measured on the old bank and what section 72 half-believed.

WHAT THIS DOES TO THE DECISION - it restores section 70's table exactly:

     k    se_diff   MDE@80%   gens/arm     section 70 published
     5     0.0241     6.7pp        775          6.7pp
    10     0.0170     4.8pp      1,550          4.7pp
    20     0.0120     3.4pp      3,100          3.3pp
    25     0.0108     3.0pp      3,875          3.0pp

Section 70 priced 3pp at k=25 / 3,875 generations per arm and that price stands.
Section 72's "3pp needs k=20 (3,100)" is WITHDRAWN. The instrument is not cheaper
than it was costed; it is exactly as expensive, and the SIZING rows in
screen_tasks.py are correct as published and must not be tightened.

WHY THIS WAS CAUGHT RATHER THAN BANKED. Section 72 refused to bank 0.75x, on the
single ground that the 95% CI still CONTAINED 0.0376, and said so in those words
both in the channel and in the completion banner. That refusal is the only reason
this is a correction to an unspent number instead of a wasted training run costed
off a 25% error. Section 70's method note was "check how many times you measured
it - n=1 is a rumour". The addition: n=10 is a rumour too when the quantity is a
VARIANCE. Ten replicates give 9 degrees of freedom and a CI spanning a factor of
2.6; a variance estimate needs an order more samples than a mean does for the same
confidence, and the interval is the thing to read, never the point estimate.

THE PIN IS FURTHER CONFIRMED, and this is the strongest form of it yet. The
temp-0.8 half of the eval instrument and build_ruler.confirm measure the same
quantity by the same code path, so with the verifier pinned they must agree:

  temp-0.8 half minus confirmed    n=10: +0.0024     n=40: +0.0002
  max |per-task deviation|         n=10:  0.2500     n=40:  0.2063
  pre-pin, for contrast                   +0.0710

+0.0002 over 31 tasks at 160 draws each. The interpreter was the whole of that
+0.0710, and nothing else systematic remains in the seam.

RULER STATE, unchanged in composition, better characterised:
  dead channels (pinned 0 or 1) across 40 runs   0/31
  tasks outside [0.2, 0.8] under the eval        4/31
    ace_oss_16070   confirmed 0.783 -> eval 0.880  (greedy 1.00, temp0.8 0.85)
    ace_oss_19459   confirmed 0.783 -> eval 0.855  (greedy 1.00, temp0.8 0.82)
    ace_oss_24748   confirmed 0.283 -> eval 0.145  (greedy 0.00, temp0.8 0.18)
    ace_oss_2454    confirmed 0.217 -> eval 0.180  (greedy 0.00, temp0.8 0.23)
ace_oss_2454 is new to this list at 40 replicates; section 72's list of 3 was
itself an n=10 artifact and is superseded by this one. Every one of the four is
pushed out by the greedy anchor (all four have greedy pinned at 1.00 or 0.00),
which makes the open question about dropping the greedy draw more pointed, not
less: it is the sole mechanism putting tasks outside the band on this ruler.
The frozen artifact records the n=10 rates and should be re-issued from n=40 -
NOT DONE, and it needs --force because freezing twice silently is exactly what
the guard exists to prevent. Named, not done.

STILL OPEN, revised:
  1. se_diff = sqrt(2)*run_sd still assumes BOTH arms share a run-level sd, and
     still only the NULL arm is measured. Unchanged by this section and now the
     single largest unmeasured quantity in the sizing.
  2. The CI is [0.0312, 0.0489], a factor of 1.57 rather than 2.6. Tighter, and
     it still contains the prediction. It no longer blocks a k choice: at the
     pessimistic end k=25 gives 3.9pp, so 3pp is not guaranteed at any listed k
     and k=25 remains the honest floor of the range section 70 named.
  3. data/ruler_frozen.json carries n=10 eval-instrument rates (above).
  4. The greedy anchor is now the only mechanism pushing tasks out of band.
  5. Cross-version verifier divergence beyond the annotation case: still a
     flashlight, not a fence.

74. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-30 - bounding section 71's damage: is the OLD held-out
    bank interpreter-sensitive? Measured, and it is NOT. eval_history's pre-pin
    rows stay comparable.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 71 pinned the verifier and showed the permissive reading changed 18 of 310
completions on the NEW ruler. That immediately raises a question section 71 did not
answer: eval_history.jsonl holds 57+ runs of the OLD ten-task bank, all scored by
unguarded eval.py under python 3.14. If the old bank is interpreter-sensitive too,
every historical held-out number is incomparable with anything measured from now
on, and the project's whole baseline series is broken.

MEASURED, same method as section 71 - 100 completions from the 10 HELD_OUT tasks
banked ONCE, then replayed through verify() with the verifier forced to each
interpreter in turn (SRLM_VERIFY_PY, emulating the pre-pin inherit):

  verifier 3.14.6   87/100
  verifier 3.11.9   87/100
  per-task counts   IDENTICAL on all 10 tasks

Zero disagreements. So the pre-pin eval_history rows for the old bank are NOT
affected and remain comparable. The mechanism predicted this and it is worth
saying why it is a prediction rather than a relief: the divergence needs
unimported typing generics in the completion, the model emits those because the
AceCode oss prompts CONTAIN annotated signatures, and the old bank's prompts are
bare English about bare functions (gcd, is_prime, rotate). Same reason the 13
SEED_TASKS were unaffected (section 71, 1269/1269 pairs still valid).

SCOPE, because this is one measurement and not a proof: 100 completions, ONE model
(llama3:8b-instruct-q4_K_M), ONE epoch. It does not re-verify the 57 historical
runs - those generations were never stored, so they cannot be re-scored, ever. What
is established is that the old bank's TASK SHAPES do not trip the known mechanism.
A different mechanism, or a different model's output style, is not covered.

INCIDENTAL CORROBORATION of section 64: the same replay shows 7 of 10 tasks at
10/10 and `rotate` at 0/10, i.e. the spent bank measured again by accident. 87/100
overall against the 0.87 pass@1 section 64 reported. The old instrument is exactly
as saturated as it was declared to be.
