================================================================================
CORRECTIONS TO THE ROUND  (audit duty; separate from the research findings)
================================================================================

C1. THE STATED REASON FOR THE INSTRUMENT CHANGE IS MEASURABLY WRONG. EXECUTED.
    The DIRECTIVE says the greedy anchor "is the SOLE mechanism putting tasks
    outside the [0.2,0.8] band". The observation it rests on is true (all four
    out-of-band tasks have greedy pinned). The causal inference is false, and the
    counterfactual is FREE because eval.py already stores `greedy` and `sampled`
    apart. Re-scoring the banked 40 replicates from the `sampled` field only:

      instrument                     mean     run sd   outside [0.2,0.8]
      1 greedy + 4 @0.8  (current)   0.5150   0.0381   4/31
      4 @0.8, greedy dropped         0.4905   0.0476   3/31

    Only ace_oss_2454 returns to band (0.180 -> 0.229). ace_oss_16070 (0.850),
    ace_oss_19459 (0.819) and ace_oss_24748 (0.181) are out of band on the
    temp-0.8 draws ALONE. The greedy anchor accounts for ONE of four, not four of
    four. Drop it for the estimand reason (Q1 below), not for the band reason.

C2. THE INVITED ATTACK, DELIVERED — section 73's residual-covariance SIGN. EXECUTED.
    Section 73 reports "mean pairwise correlation +0.0102 (reinforcement)" and builds a
    claim on its SIGN: "the opposite of what section 62 A1b measured and what
    section 72 half-believed." No interval is given. Bootstrap over the 40
    replicates (2000 resamples, stdlib random, seed 7):

      mean pairwise correlation  +0.0075   95% CI [-0.0080, +0.0280]

    THE INTERVAL CONTAINS ZERO. Section 73 corrected section 72 for banking a point
    estimate and then asserted a sign on a point estimate. My point value
    (+0.0075, average of pairwise correlations) also differs from the reported
    +0.0102 (implied from Var(run mean)); two definitions, both unlabelled. The
    honest statement is STRONGER than the one made: at n=40 the residual
    covariance is INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ZERO, which supports independence better
    than a claimed positive sign does.

C3. "NOTHING ELSE SYSTEMATIC REMAINS IN THE SEAM" OVER-REACHES. EXECUTED.
    Section 73's +0.0002 (temp-0.8 half minus confirmed) is a point estimate. Paired
    over the 31 tasks: sd 0.0625, 95% CI [-0.0227, +0.0231]. Residual seam bias
    is bounded at +/-2.3pp — which is 77% of the 3.0pp MDE being bought. The
    supportable claim is "any residual seam bias is under 2.3pp", not "nothing
    systematic remains". The +0.0710 -> +0.0002 collapse remains excellent
    evidence for the interpreter diagnosis; it is not an absence proof.

C4. SECTION 71's H2 WAS NOT REJECTED, IT WAS UNTESTED FOR POWER. EXECUTED.
    "TESTED AND REJECTED ... measured corr(confirmed-0.5, shift) = -0.1341" has
    no interval. I could not reproduce -0.1341 from any banked artifact — it
    appears only in channel prose; ruler_noise_analysis_prepin.txt carries only
    the -0.0147 mean pairwise correlation. My reproduction with the natural
    definition (pre-pin rows, eval-instrument rate minus confirmed) gives
    r = -0.0687, Fisher 95% CI [-0.413, +0.293]. At n=31 that interval admits
    substantial POSITIVE correlation. H2 is NOT SUPPORTED; it was not rejected.
    (This matters little now — the verifier turned out to be the cause — but the
    same sentence shape will be reused, so the shape is what is being flagged.)

C5. SECTION 74's "ZERO DISAGREEMENTS" NEEDS ITS UPPER BOUND. EXECUTED.
    0/100 gives a 95% one-sided upper bound of 2.95% on the disagreement rate
    (rule of three, 1 - 0.05^(1/100)). At 10 tasks x 5 samples that is up to 1.48
    completions per eval run, i.e. a pass@1 shift of up to 2.95pp on the old bank
    cannot be excluded. Section 64's maximum possible effect on that bank was 3.0pp. So
    the measurement does NOT license "the pre-pin eval_history rows remain
    comparable" for any effect under ~3pp — it licenses "the old bank is not
    GROSSLY interpreter-sensitive". Same finding, honest size.

C6. CLAIM A IS CONDITIONAL ON A DIRECTORY EXISTING, AND THE TEST SUITE CANNOT
    WITNESS THE CONDITION FAILING. EXECUTED, with a red witness.
    dataset_gate.verify_py() (dataset_gate.py:91-98) falls through to
    `return sys.executable` when .venv-train\Scripts\python.exe is absent.
    Probe (dataset_gate imported, _VENV_PY repointed at a nonexistent path):

      live verify_py()                -> ...\.venv-train\Scripts\python.exe
      with .venv-train absent         -> C:\Python314\python.exe
      sys.executable                  -> C:\Python314\python.exe
      IDENTICAL TO LAUNCHER: True     <-- the pre-pin defect, restored silently

    And three of the five tests in tests/test_verifier_pin.py `return` early on
    `if not VENV_PY.exists()`, which the file's own runner counts as PASS:

      (skipped: .venv-train absent)
      PASS test_both_launchers_reach_the_same_verdict
      PASS test_env_override_is_honoured
      PASS test_eval_records_the_verifier
      (skipped: .venv-train absent)
      PASS test_the_probe_can_actually_discriminate
      (skipped: .venv-train absent, nothing to pin away from)
      PASS test_verifier_is_not_the_launching_interpreter
      0 failed  <-- suite green with the pin unenforceable

    All 5 also PASS on the live tree (EXECUTED, as do tests/test_ruler_noise.py
    7/7 and tests/test_screen_sizing.py 4/4). So the green suite is real but is
    not evidence the pin holds. Claim A as written — "can no longer depend on
    which script called it" — is true only while that path exists. The precise
    claim is: "cannot depend on the launcher WHEN .venv-train is present, and
    reverts to launcher-dependence, silently, when it is not."
    This is a fence with a gate in it, and the gate is what the flashlight
    metaphor was already conceding about the other axis.

C7. eval_history.jsonl HAS 68 ROWS, NOT 57. EXECUTED. Of those 68: 0 carry the
    `verifier` key and 0 carry the greedy/sampled split; 67 are n_tasks=10, 1 has
    no n_tasks; 58 have task_set=None (pre-dating the field). Consistent with
    section 71's flagging rule, and it means NO historical row can be re-analysed
    greedy-free. The count in the record is low by 11.

C8. k=25 GIVES 3.02pp, NOT 3.0pp. The honest k for MDE <= 3.0pp at sd 0.0381 is
    26 (4,030 gens/arm). Trivial in size; quoted as "3pp at k=25" in section 70,
    section 73 and the DIRECTIVE, so it is now three copies of a rounded number.

C9. PRIOR ART — the hand-rolled incomplete gamma was NOT gratuitous, and the
    bootstrap replaces it anyway. EXECUTED. scipy is absent from BOTH
    interpreters (.venv-train 3.11.9 has numpy, not scipy; system 3.14.6 has
    numpy + requests, not scipy), so there was no chi2.ppf to import and
    ruler_noise._gammap/chi2_ppf was a real gap being filled. Its output agrees
    with my independent implementation to 4 dp on this dataset (sd 0.038067,
    CI [0.0312, 0.0489] — reproduced exactly, along with the whole k-table).
    BUT: a nonparametric bootstrap over the 40 replicates is stdlib-only, ~15
    lines, needs no special functions, and supplies intervals for the THREE
    quantities that are currently bare point estimates (C2, C3, and the obs/pred
    ratio). Recommendation: adopt the bootstrap, delete _gammap/chi2_ppf and most
    of decompose's bookkeeping. That is a net deletion that also closes C2/C3.

C10. THE STALE n=10 IN ruler_frozen.json — UNTIDY WITH ONE REAL EDGE, AND IT IS
    THE THIRD INSTANCE OF ONE CLASS. EXECUTED (grep over *.py).
    `eval_instrument_rate` is written by build_ruler.cmd_freeze:281 and read by
    NOTHING — untidy only. But `eval_instrument_out_of_band` (line 315) lists 3
    tids when the truth at n=40 is 4, and that field is exactly the flag a future
    reader uses to decide which tasks carry signal. A reader trusting the frozen
    artifact treats ace_oss_2454 as healthy. Small, real.
    THE CLASS: section 67 closed "sizing figures in two places"; section 72 closed
    "noise-floor status in two places"; this is the same shape a third time — a
    MEASURED value stored in a place that is never re-derived. Per this file's own
    second-time rule, close the class rather than re-freezing: an immutable
    artifact should carry only immutable things (payload hashes, the set sha256,
    the split). Measured rates belong in the analysis file, which is regenerated
    every run. `freeze --force` re-issues the same defect with fresher numbers.

================================================================================
Q1 — INSTRUMENT VALIDITY
================================================================================

THE CITATION, AND IT IS DECISIVE. Chen et al. 2021, "Evaluating Large Language
Models Trained on Code" (arXiv:2107.03374) — full text read via ar5iv, not the
abstract. Verbatim: "we generate n>=k samples per task (in this paper, we use
n=200 and k<=100)". On temperature: "the optimal temperature for pass@1 is
T*=0.2 and the optimal temperature for pass@100 is T*=0.8"; "higher temperatures
are optimal for larger k, because the resulting set of samples has higher
diversity"; for Codex-S, "We use T*=0 for computing pass@1 and T*=1 for
computing pass@100"; "top p=0.95 for all sampling evaluation in this work".

So THREE things, and they answer the ask separately:

  (a) TEMPERATURE IS TUNED PER k, AND HELD CONSTANT WITHIN A GIVEN n. Chen et al.
      do use greedy (T=0) for pass@1 — but then ALL n draws are greedy, so n
      collapses to 1 and the combinatorial estimator is not in play. They never
      mix temperatures inside one n. Mixing is therefore NOT documented practice.

  (b) IT IS NOT DOCUMENTED AS AN ERROR EITHER. I searched for work analysing a
      greedy+sampled mixture fed to the unbiased estimator and found none. The
      nearest on-point paper is Hariri et al. 2025, "Don't Pass@k: A Bayesian
      Framework for Large Language Model Evaluation" (arXiv:2510.04265), which
      attacks pass@k for instability at small n and proposes Dirichlet posteriors
      instead — and reports that "under a uniform prior, the Bayesian posterior
      mean is order-equivalent to average accuracy (Pass@1)". It does not address
      the mixture. PLAIN STATEMENT, as requested: NOBODY ADDRESSES THE MIXTURE.
      Label this an absence-of-search-evidence, not a proven absence.

  (c) THE TEMPERATURE THIS PROJECT USES FOR pass@1 IS THE ONE Chen et al. FOUND
      OPTIMAL FOR pass@100. eval.py:37 TEMP=0.8 with KS=(1,3). Not a defect for a
      RELATIVE ruler, and do not "fix" it: T=0.2 would collapse rates toward the
      argmax and re-create the dead-channel problem the band selection exists to
      avoid. The point worth recording is that the [0.2,0.8] BAND AND THE
      TEMPERATURE ARE COUPLED — the band is a property of the instrument at
      T=0.8, not of the tasks, and no convention pins both.

IS "pass@3" AS THIS FILE REPORTS IT THE QUANTITY IT NAMES? NO. And the failure is
at k=3 only; separate the two k's, which the DIRECTIVE does not:

  - AT k=1 THERE IS NO ESTIMATOR PROBLEM. pass_at_k(n,c,1) = 1 - (n-c)/n = c/n
    identically, so run-level pass@1 IS mean accuracy. The DIRECTIVE's "identity
    to 0.000000" is likewise algebraic, not empirical: with n=5, one greedy and
    four sampled and zero gen errors, c/5 == 0.2*greedy + 0.8*mean(sampled) by
    construction. EXECUTED: 0 violations over 1,240 task-runs, and `n` is 5 on
    every one. So the k=1 issue is purely WHICH ESTIMAND — a fixed 20/80 blend of
    two different decoding policies — not a broken estimator.

  - AT k=3 THE ESTIMATOR GENUINELY MISATTRIBUTES. It estimates pass@3 of a pool
    containing one deterministic item and four random ones. P(a random 3-subset
    of 5 includes the greedy slot) = C(4,2)/C(5,3) = 0.6, so a task whose greedy
    draw passes has reported pass@3 >= 0.6 REGARDLESS of its sampled behaviour.
    Reported pass@3 is a 0.6/0.4 blend of "pass@2 of the sampled policy, floored
    by the greedy indicator" and "pass@3 of the sampled policy". No single
    sampling distribution has that as its pass@3.

  - AND pass@3 IS SATURATED ANYWAY, WHICH IS THE BIGGER FINDING. EXECUTED over
    the 40 replicates: mean reported pass@3 = 0.834, and 20 of 31 tasks fall
    outside [0.2,0.8] at k=3 versus 4 of 31 at k=1. That is mostly NOT the
    greedy anchor's fault — it is intrinsic: a pass@1 band of [0.2,0.8] maps to
    [1-0.8^3, 1-0.2^3] = [0.488, 0.992] at k=3. A ruler band-selected at pass@1
    cannot also be in band at pass@3. RECOMMENDATION: drop 3 from KS, or keep it
    explicitly labelled a diagnostic. pass@1 is the only metric on this ruler
    with resolution, and it is already the one every sizing figure uses. Hariri
    et al.'s order-equivalence result above is independent support for making
    mean accuracy the headline.

WHAT IS LOST BY DROPPING THE GREEDY DRAW: NOTHING THE LITERATURE CREDITS.
The 0.894x is not a variance-reduction technique's benefit. sqrt(4/5)=0.894 is
the arithmetic of replacing one of five random draws with a constant. Adding a
constant to an estimator always lowers its variance and always moves its
estimand; that is bias, not Monte Carlo variance reduction (a control variate
lowers variance AT FIXED ESTIMAND — this does the opposite). There is no citation
to lose because there is no technique being used.

Worse, the "reduction" evaporates in the comparison it is for. Under the NULL arm
the greedy draw is a constant (EXECUTED: 0 of 31 tasks had a non-constant greedy
draw across 40 runs), so it contributes ~zero variance. Between ARMS it is not
constant — training changes the argmax path. Each greedy flip moves the reported
rate by 0.2/31 = 0.65pp with ZERO associated sampling noise, so five greedy flips
manufacture a 3.2pp "effect" — the full MDE — with no change in the sampled
distribution at all. The null-measured sd cannot see that term.

THE CHEAPEST VALID INSTRUMENT, AND IT COSTS FEWER GENERATIONS THAN THE CURRENT
PLAN. EXECUTED, all from banked data:

  option                                 new gens needed for MDE 3.0pp
  (A) keep as-is                          null done; trained 31*5*26 = 4,030
      -- but the estimand is a 20/80 policy blend and pass@3 is saturated
  (B) re-generate all 5 at T=0.8          null 4,960 + trained 4,960 = 9,920
      -- requires re-measuring the null arm from scratch
  (C) CHANGE THE REPORTED METRIC, NOT THE GENERATION. Score pass@1 from the
      four `sampled` draws only; report the greedy draw as a separate labelled
      diagnostic line.                    null 0 + trained 31*5*40 = 6,200

  (C) wins by 37% over (B) because THE GREEDY-FREE NULL BASELINE IS ALREADY
  MEASURED. The same 40 replicates give it for free: mean 0.4905, run-level sd
  0.0476, and with both arms at 40 replicates
  se_diff = 0.0476*sqrt(2/40) = 0.01064, MDE@80% = 2.98pp. Nothing needs
  re-generating on the null side, ever.
  Note the level shift: 0.5150 -> 0.4905, a 2.45pp re-baseline. That is why this
  must be decided BEFORE the trained arm runs, not after — which is exactly why
  the DIRECTIVE was right to ask rather than decide.
  And C7 means the feared "re-baselines every eval_history row" cost is zero:
  all 68 rows are already unusable (no verifier key, no greedy/sampled split,
  spent 10-task bank).

  (B) is the tidier long-run instrument (5 real samples, n=5, sd ~0.0426
  EXTRAPOLATED by binomial scaling from the measured n=4 value, k=32,
  4,960/arm). It is the right end state. It is not worth 4,960 wasted null-arm
  generations to reach today.

================================================================================
Q2 — SIZING, PER-ARM VARIANCE, AND THE TERM THE FORMULA OMITS
================================================================================

DOES THE LITERATURE REPORT PER-ARM VARIANCE ON pass@k? I looked and did not find
a paper that reports run-level pass@k variance separately for a base and a tuned
model as a designed measurement. The field's problem is one level cruder: pass@k
is conventionally reported with NO interval at all — which is why Miller 2024,
"Adding Error Bars to Evals: A Statistical Approach to Language Model
Evaluations" (arXiv:2411.00640) had to be written. So homoscedasticity is not
ASSUMED in this literature; it is never CONFRONTED. Label: absence of search
evidence over ~6 targeted queries, not a survey.

IS THERE A DOCUMENTED DIRECTION? YES, AND IT FAVOURS YOU — ON ONE TERM ONLY.
  - Kirk et al., "Understanding the Effects of RLHF on LLM Generalisation and
    Diversity" (arXiv:2310.06452, ICLR 2024): "RLHF significantly reduces output
    diversity compared to SFT across a variety of measures."
  - Yue et al. 2025, "Does Reinforcement Learning Really Incentivize Reasoning
    Capacity in LLMs Beyond the Base Model?" (arXiv:2504.13837): pass@1 rises
    after RLVR but "the base models achieve a higher pass@k score when k is
    large", with "coverage and perplexity analyses" attributing this to a
    narrowed sampling distribution. This is the closest thing to your exact
    setting (execution-verifiable reward) in print.
  - Omura et al. 2024, "Entropy Controllable Direct Preference Optimization"
    (arXiv:2411.07595): DPO's reverse-KL term "encourages mode-seeking fitting to
    the reference policy"; H-DPO adds entropy control and shows "superior results
    in pass@k evaluations". Confirms the mechanism is recognised for DPO
    specifically, not just RLHF.
  So per-sample entropy after DPO is expected to FALL, per-task rates move toward
  0/1, and the trained arm's GENERATION-noise sd is more likely below 0.0476 than
  above it. On that term, sqrt(2)*run_sd is conservative. Yue et al. also carries
  a warning for the ruler: if you keep any pass@k with k>1, DPO can move it DOWN
  while pass@1 goes up.

BUT THE HOMOSCEDASTICITY ASSUMPTION IS NOT THE LARGEST UNMEASURED TERM, AND Q2
ASKS ABOUT THE WRONG ONE. se_diff = sqrt(2)*run_sd contains generation noise and
nothing else. The null arm is the SAME CHECKPOINT re-sampled, so it has zero
training variance by construction. The trained arm has a second component the
formula has no slot for: the training run itself — LoRA init, data order, seed.
  - Dodge et al. 2020, "Fine-Tuning Pretrained Language Models: Weight
    Initializations, Data Orders, and Early Stopping" (arXiv:2002.06305): "even
    with the same hyperparameter values, distinct random seeds can lead to
    substantially different results", with init and data order contributing
    "comparably to the variance of out-of-sample performance."
  - Bouthillier et al. 2021, "Accounting for Variance in Machine Learning
    Benchmarks" (MLSys 2021, arXiv:2103.03098): identifies data sampling,
    augmentation, init and hyperparameters as variance sources and finds that
    "adding more sources of variation to an imperfect estimator approaches better
    the ideal estimator at a 51 times reduction in compute cost."
  With ONE trained checkpoint, a clean 3pp detection supports "this checkpoint
  differs from the base on these 31 tasks" and NOT "DPO on verifier-grounded
  pairs helps". No amount of k fixes that. Spending 6,200 generations to sharpen
  the generation term while the training term is unestimated is sharpening the
  wrong term, and Bouthillier is the citation that says so in cost language.

THE CHEAPEST DESIGN THAT NEVER NEEDS THE TRAINED ARM'S sd IN ADVANCE. Three
pieces, and the third is the one that answers the question as asked:

  1. NEVER POOL. Compute se_diff = sqrt(s_null^2/k_null + s_trained^2/k_trained)
     from each arm's OWN replicates and use Welch's df. This is free and removes
     the assumption entirely at analysis time. Nothing needs to be known in
     advance to compute it AFTERWARDS.
  2. PAIR BY TASK. Miller 2024's recommendation 4, verbatim from the paper:
     "When two models are being compared, conducting statistical inference on the
     question-level paired differences, rather than the population-level summary
     statistics", because "paired differences represent a 'free' reduction in
     estimator variance when comparing two models". His recommendation 3 —
     "Reducing variance by resampling answers" — is what your k replicates
     already are. Be precise about which of two SEs you report: the per-task
     paired-difference SE over 31 tasks answers "would this hold on a fresh draw
     of tasks" (it carries the tau^2/T term your own screen_tasks docstring flags
     and section 59 finding 2 named); the replicate-based Welch SE answers "did
     the mean rate on THESE 31 tasks change". Different questions. Report both,
     label both, prereg which one the claim is about.
  3. DO NOT PRE-SIZE — USE AN INTERNAL PILOT WITH VARIANCE-ONLY RE-ESTIMATION.
     The design literature for "the variance is unknown at the moment sample size
     must be chosen" is internal pilot / blinded sample size re-estimation
     (Wittes & Brittain 1990 originated it; Gould & Shih 1992 blinded it; Kieser
     & Friede developed procedures that do not inflate the type I error rate;
     Friede & Kieser 2006 is the review). Applied here: run the trained arm in
     blocks of 10 replicates, and after each block re-estimate ONLY the observed
     within-arm sds and recompute the k needed. Because the variance re-estimate
     is a function of within-arm spread and NOT of the between-arm difference,
     the look is effectively blinded to the effect and the type-I cost is
     negligible. THE TRAP, stated because it is easy to fall into: this licenses
     re-sizing on the VARIANCE, not stopping on the EFFECT. If you peek at the
     difference and stop when its CI excludes zero, you have run an unadjusted
     group-sequential trial and inflated alpha. Re-size freely; do not peek.
     STATUS: this thread is VERIFIED-SECONDARY only — I confirmed the papers and
     the result via a Medical College of Wisconsin annotated bibliography and
     Wiley listings, and did NOT fetch the primary texts. Treat the method as
     sound and the exact inflation figures as unread.

  Cheapest concrete instance: the null arm is ALREADY at k=40 greedy-free. Run
  the trained arm to 40 replicates in four blocks of 10, computing Welch se_diff
  from both arms' own sds after each block. Zero prior knowledge required, zero
  homoscedasticity assumption, and the null side costs nothing.

AND SPEND THE TRAINED ARM'S BUDGET ACROSS SEEDS, NOT ACROSS MORE REPLICATES OF
ONE SEED. 2 seeds x 20 replicates, or 3 x 13, is the SAME 6,200 generations as
1 x 40 — and the trained arm's replicate sd then includes training-seed variance,
so the Welch se_diff automatically prices the term the current formula omits.
This is Bouthillier et al.'s finding applied literally: randomise over more
variance sources at fixed budget rather than more draws from one. With 2 seeds
the seed component has df=1 and is a direction, not a fact (your own section 73
method note applies: n=2 is a rumour when the quantity is a variance) — but a
rumour about a term currently valued at zero is a strict improvement.

================================================================================
Q3 — DOES R2 STILL HOLD AT 13?
================================================================================

NO — AND NOT FOR THE REASON THE DIRECTIVE PROPOSES. The DIRECTIVE asks whether
the instrument swap "already spent the one-change-at-a-time budget R2 was
protecting." It did not spend it. It VOIDED THE ESTIMAND.

R2's isolation argument required a comparison of two numbers produced by the same
instrument: (old ruler, 13 prompts, buggy seam) vs (old ruler, 13 prompts, fixed
seam). Both terms are now unavailable, for two independent reasons:
  - the first term's instrument is spent (section 64: 7 of 10 at ceiling; section
    74 confirmed it again by accident at 87/100 with 7 tasks at 10/10); and
  - a buggy-seam baseline on the FROZEN 31-task ruler does not exist and cannot
    be created, because section 71 makes every pre-pin number on that set void.
One-change-at-a-time is a rule about holding the MEASURING DEVICE constant across
two measurements. With no valid "before", holding the prompt count at 13 holds it
constant against nothing. R2 is not violated; it is inapplicable.

WHAT THE RE-RUN WOULD ACTUALLY BE A MEASUREMENT OF: the first null-vs-trained
efficacy baseline of the whole pipeline on the new instrument — "does QLoRA DPO
on N verifier-grounded pairs move greedy-free pass@1 on the frozen 31-task ruler,
against the null arm measured at 40 replicates under the pinned verifier." The
seam fix and the verifier pin are now part of the PIPELINE DEFINITION, not
variables under test. Label the run a BASELINE, not an attributed experiment.
Subsequent runs can then be one-change-at-a-time against it, which is the first
time that rule will actually be purchasable.

AND 13 IS NOW THE WRONG SOURCE, ON AN ARGUMENT THAT IS NOT ABOUT COUNT. I am not
re-arguing the deferred 200. This is a different axis:
  Section 71 established that the 13 SEED_TASKS are bare functions (gcd, is_prime,
  two_sum) and the ruler is 31 AceCode `oss` tasks whose prompts CONTAIN
  annotated signatures — and that the two families differ in exactly the property
  that broke the verifier. Training on 13 bare-function prompts and measuring on
  31 annotated-signature tasks is a train/eval distribution shift that this
  project has already MEASURED to be behaviourally significant. The 43-tid
  training pool is drawn from the same distribution as the ruler and is disjoint
  by construction (build_ruler.split_side hashes the tid, RULER_SHARE 0.5, and
  cmd_freeze's five fatal checks were witnessed passing in section 72).
  So: draw the training prompts from the 43-tid pool, not from SEED_TASKS. That
  is a SOURCE change, not a COUNT change, and it does not re-open the deferred
  question. If you want to honour R2's spirit while you are at it, draw exactly
  13 from the 43 — count unchanged, distribution matched, and the deferred
  prompt-count question stays deferred and cleanly separable.

A CONFOUND IN THAT POOL THAT NOBODY HAS LOOKED AT, and it is created by your own
fix. Under the pinned 3.11 verifier, a completion that omits `from typing import
List` FAILS. The oss prompts invite exactly that omission. So pairs mined from the
43-tid pool may have `rejected` rejected for a MISSING IMPORT rather than a wrong
algorithm — in which case DPO learns import hygiene, and the ruler (same task
family, same verifier) will duly reward it with a real pass@1 rise that has
nothing to do with code repair. That would be a true positive on the instrument
and a false positive on the thesis. CHEAP CHECK, before training: on the mined
pairs, count how many `rejected` completions fail with a NameError naming a typing
generic. If that fraction is large, either fix it in the prompt (state the imports
are required) or report the effect split by failure class.

================================================================================
RANKED CRITIQUE  (most decisive first)
================================================================================
1. se_diff = sqrt(2)*run_sd HAS NO SLOT FOR TRAINING-SEED VARIANCE, and that is a
   bigger hole than the homoscedasticity assumption Q2 asks about. One trained
   checkpoint cannot support a claim about DPO, at any k. Fix costs zero extra
   generations: split the trained arm's 40 replicates across 2-3 seeds
   (Bouthillier et al. 2021; Dodge et al. 2020).
2. THE STATED REASON FOR CHANGING THE INSTRUMENT IS FALSE (C1). Greedy accounts
   for 1 of 4 out-of-band tasks, not 4 of 4 — measurable for free from data you
   already have. Change the instrument for the estimand reason.
3. THE FREE PATH IS BETTER THAN BOTH OPTIONS ON THE TABLE (Q1 option C). Score
   pass@1 from `sampled` only. 6,200 new generations vs 9,920, because the
   greedy-free null baseline is already banked at n=40 (mean 0.4905, sd 0.0476).
   And drop 3 from KS: pass@3 is saturated at 20/31 out of band, intrinsically.
4. CLAIM A IS CONDITIONAL AND THE SUITE CANNOT SEE THE CONDITION FAIL (C6). Red
   witness supplied: verify_py() returns sys.executable when .venv-train is
   absent, and 3 of 5 tests skip-as-PASS in exactly that state.
5. TWO "NOTHING REMAINS" CLAIMS HAVE INTERVALS COMPARABLE TO THE EFFECT BEING
   BOUGHT (C3: seam bounded +/-2.3pp against a 3.0pp MDE; C5: old-bank
   interpreter sensitivity bounded 2.95pp against a 3.0pp maximum effect).
6. THE INVITED ATTACK LANDS TWICE (C2 sign of the residual covariance, CI
   contains 0; C4 H2 "rejected" on r=-0.13 at n=31, CI [-0.41,+0.29] and the
   -0.1341 not reproducible from any artifact).
7. R2 IS INAPPLICABLE, NOT SPENT (Q3), and the training SOURCE — not its count —
   is the change that matters, plus an unexamined import-hygiene confound.
8. CLOSE THE CLASS RATHER THAN RE-FREEZING (C10, third instance) and REPLACE THE
   HAND-ROLLED chi-square WITH A BOOTSTRAP (C9) — a net deletion that also
   supplies the three missing intervals.

================================================================================
LEFTOVER RISKS  (unresolved even if everything above lands)
================================================================================
- TRAINING-SEED VARIANCE STAYS A RUMOUR. 2-3 seeds gives df=1 or 2. By your own
  section 73 rule that is not a measurement of a variance. It is a direction, and
  the design that prices it at zero is worse.
- tau^2/T IS STILL UNPRICED AND NO PREREG EXISTS. T=31 estimates the change on
  THESE 31 tasks. Nothing here supports "the model got better at code
  generation," and section 59 finding 2 has now survived four activations
  unaddressed.
- EFFECTIVE T IS BELOW 31. ace_oss_16070 sits at 0.880 (0.850 greedy-free) and
  carries almost no signal; 19459 and 24748 are weak. Every MDE divides by 31.
  Effective T is nearer 28. Do not drop them — dropping selects on the reported
  measurement, exactly as section 72 argued — but do not price them as full
  channels.
- CROSS-VERSION DIVERGENCE BEYOND ANNOTATIONS IS STILL UNENUMERATED, and after
  C6 the flashlight also fails to fire if the venv moves. Nothing bounds the
  class; the honest bound is "one mechanism found, one probed."
- OLLAMA-SIDE NONDETERMINISM IS IN NO VARIANCE TERM. Whether the server seeds its
  sampler per request, and whether kv-cache reuse or batch composition perturbs
  logits, is unmeasured. The greedy draw being constant on 31/31 across 40 runs is
  good evidence this is small AT T=0; it says nothing about T=0.8.
- THE IMPORT-HYGIENE CONFOUND IN THE TRAINING POOL (Q3). Unexamined, and it is
  the failure mode where the instrument and the thesis disagree while both
  numbers look right.
- THE VERIFIER FINGERPRINT RECORDS A VERSION, NOT AN ENVIRONMENT. Your own scope
  note says so. Add that interpreter_fingerprint() silently records
  "python unknown" if the probe fails, and the gate's equality check would then
  compare "unknown" to "unknown" and pass.
- 68 HISTORICAL RUNS ARE PERMANENTLY UNRECOVERABLE. Not fixable, correctly
  recorded, and worth restating so nobody tries.

================================================================================
THE QUESTIONS THEMSELVES
================================================================================
Q1 WAS THE RIGHT ASK, and asking rather than deciding was correct — a 2.45pp
level shift on a 3.0pp MDE is not a judgement call to make alone. It was posed
one notch too narrowly: it framed the greedy anchor as an estimator problem when
at k=1 it is purely an estimand problem, and it did not ask about pass@3 at all,
which is where the estimator actually breaks and where saturation (20/31 out of
band) makes the reported column unusable.

Q3 WAS THE RIGHT ASK and was framed with unusual honesty ("a variable ALREADY
changed, by your own ruling"). The available answer was better than the offered
options: the budget was not spent, the estimand was voided.

Q2 WAS THE WRONG SHAPE. It asked about heteroscedasticity BETWEEN ARMS when the
term the formula omits entirely — training-seed variance — is larger, is not a
heteroscedasticity problem, and cannot be fixed by any choice of k. Asking whether
the trained arm is noisier presupposes that generation noise is the whole of the
trained arm's variance, which is the assumption that needed challenging.

THE QUESTION THE DIRECTIVE SHOULD HAVE POSED AND DIDN'T:
  "With ONE training run, what am I entitled to conclude from ANY result on this
   ruler, and what is the cheapest way to get a second training seed into the
   variance estimate?"
  Answer, so it is not a rhetorical device: you are entitled to "this checkpoint
  differs from the base on these 31 tasks"; the cheapest way is to spend the
  trained arm's existing generation budget across 2-3 seeds instead of 1, which
  costs zero additional generations.

A SECOND ONE, smaller but sharper because your own fix created it:
  "Does the pinned 3.11 verifier make my training pool teach import hygiene
   instead of code repair?" — the cheap check is in Q3 above.

THE CHANNEL HAS TWO ANSWERS TO ACTIVATION #17, sections 75 and 76, both labelled

|COUNCIL)" | sort | uniq -d` is empty). Both blocks are intact. The channel has no
write lock, so "the next number is N" is a read-then-write race whenever two seats
are armed; §76's recommendation - number from the append, or lock - is adopted for

CORRECTION 1 (§75 F1, §76 C2/C4). SECTION 73'S HEADLINE WAS MEASURED AGAINST THE
WRONG NULL, and my own analyze output printed the right one three lines below the
one I banked.

  observed run-level sd (5-draw blend)                    0.0381
  null assuming all 5 draws vary  (what §73 banked)       0.0376  -> 1.01x
  null with the greedy draw CONSTANT (the actual scheme)  0.0326  -> 1.17x

The greedy draw is constant on 31/31 tasks, so a null that lets all five draws
vary is not this instrument's null. "INDEPENDENCE HOLDS EXACTLY" is WITHDRAWN. The
1.01x was two real effects cancelling: the constant greedy draw pulling variance
DOWN by 0.894x, and something pulling it UP by ~1.17x. Against the correct null the
observed sd is 17% HIGH, consistent in sign with the +0.0102 mean pairwise
correlation §73 already reported and then talked past. §75 gives the ratio's CI as
[0.957, 1.500], which contains 1.0 - so "excess variance" is a DIRECTION, not an
established fact, exactly as the shortfall was. The practical consequence for
§70's table is nil, because the table was built on 0.0376 and 0.0381 is what was
observed; the consequence for the EXPLANATION is total.

CORRECTION 2 (§76 C1). "ALL FOUR OUT-OF-BAND TASKS ARE PUSHED OUT BY THE GREEDY
ANCHOR" IS FALSE. It is one of four. Re-derived from the 40 pinned replicates,
comparing each task's eval rate against its greedy-free rate:

  ace_oss_16070   eval 0.880  greedy-free 0.850   still OUT without greedy
  ace_oss_19459   eval 0.855  greedy-free 0.819   still OUT without greedy
  ace_oss_24748   eval 0.145  greedy-free 0.181   still OUT without greedy
  ace_oss_2454    eval 0.180  greedy-free 0.225   IN band without greedy  <- 1 of 4

I reasoned from "each has greedy pinned at 1.00 or 0.00" to "greedy put them
there". The premise is true and the inference does not follow: three of them are
outside the band on their temp-0.8 rates alone. This mattered because it was the
stated justification for changing the instrument, so the strongest argument I gave
for the change was measurably wrong. The case for dropping greedy has to rest on
estimator validity (§76 Q1), not on band membership.

CORRECTION 3 (§76 C6). MY OWN PIN COULD SILENTLY UN-PIN ITSELF, AND MY OWN TEST
SUITE COULD NOT SEE IT. verify_py() ended `return sys.executable`. With
.venv-train absent the verifier becomes the launching interpreter again - the
section 71 defect, restored - while every artifact goes on recording a
fingerprint as if a pin were in force. RED WITNESS, _VENV_PY patched in-memory to
a nonexistent path:

  verify_py() -> C:\Python314\python.exe   == sys.executable   DEFECT BACK

Worse, three of the five tests in tests/test_verifier_pin.py began
`if not VENV_PY.exists(): print("skipped"); return`, and the runner counts a
non-raising test as PASS. So in exactly the state where the pin is unenforceable
the suite reports `0 failed`. That is a test that cannot fail when the thing it
guards is broken - 05-STAYING-OUT-OF-THE-MUD 1.6 - written by me while fixing a
defect of the same family, one round earlier. FIXED: verify_py() now RAISES with
the expected path and the SRLM_VERIFY_PY escape hatch; the skips are gone; an
absent venv is now a FAILURE because it means unpinned, not untested; and
test_verify_py_refuses_to_fall_back_to_the_launcher makes the red witness

ALSO CLOSED THIS PASS (§75 F2): THE FREEZE HAD NO GATE. `freeze` wrote
ruler_set_sha256 and nothing read it, so the freeze was a comment.
build_ruler.verify_frozen() now re-hashes every task from its stored screen
payload, compares the set hash, and ruler_noise obtains the ruler ONLY through it
- the parse-don't-validate shape dataset_gate.load_verified already used for

                            74560a4c..."

AND THE FROZEN ARTIFACT NO LONGER CARRIES SAMPLE-DEPENDENT FIGURES. It recorded a

time, which is why it said 3 tasks were out of band at n=10 and the truth at n=40
is 4. A frozen file should contain only what is frozen. Those fields are stripped;

TWO MORE COUNCIL FINDINGS VERIFIED AND ACCEPTED, not yet acted on:
  - pass@3 IS SATURATED BY CONSTRUCTION on this ruler. A [0.2,0.8] pass@1 band
    maps to [0.488, 0.992] at k=3, so 20 of 31 tasks sit outside [0.2,0.8] at
    k=3 regardless of the greedy draw (re-derived: exactly 20/31). KS=(1,3) is
    reporting a metric this instrument cannot move. Dropping 3 from KS is a
    one-line change and it is NOT made in this section, because changing the

  - THE GREEDY-FREE INSTRUMENT IS ALREADY MEASURED, at zero generation cost,

    added for a different reason and now paying for itself):
        greedy-free pass@1 over the same 40 replicates: mean 0.4905  sd 0.0476
        40 replicates/arm -> se_diff 0.01064  MDE@80% 2.98pp  (4,960 gens/arm)
        out-of-band under greedy-free pass@1: 3/31
    Council figures reproduced to 4 decimals. So a valid ~3pp instrument exists
    without regenerating anything, and the level shifts 2.45pp - which is why the
    metric change is a decision and not a cleanup.

78. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-30 - TRACK B BUG, reported by the proprietor: "when I
    sample the AI it won't talk back to me after training." Reproduced, diagnosed,
    fixed. The model was never broken; the GUI could not reach it.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FIRST, WHAT IT WAS NOT. The trained model is fine. Sampling the checkpoint the
GUI wrote at 15:33 today, from the CLI, outside the GUI:

  $ python generate.py --out out_gui --prompt "def solve(" --tokens 200
  def solve(a, tok):
      assert gcd(18, 1) == 1
      assert gcd(7, 8) == 1
      ...

Coherent, and heavily memorised - train_loss 0.088 against val_loss 2.774 on that
run - but it talks. So the defect was between the trained weights and the user,
not in the weights.

THE DEFECT, in studio.py. `self.model` was assigned in exactly ONE place, line
577, handling the "done" message a training run emits when it finishes. The
Sample button was created `state="disabled"` (line 413) and re-enabled only from
that same handler. And nothing anywhere loaded `<out>/ckpt.pt`. Three facts that
are individually reasonable and together mean: THE GUI CAN ONLY SAMPLE A MODEL IT
TRAINED IN THE CURRENT SESSION. Close the window, reopen it, and the model sitting
in out_gui/ is unreachable - button greyed, nothing to click.

And when it was reachable-but-unset, `_generate` opened with:

    def _generate(self):
        if self.model is None:
            return

A bare return. No dialog, no status line, no log entry. The button does nothing
and says nothing about why. That is the reported symptom exactly: it won't talk
back.

RED WITNESS, source-level, because tkinter needs an interactive window station
this shell does not have (recorded 2026-07-20; Win32 GUI calls fail silently
here):

  $ git show HEAD:localllm/studio.py | grep -A3 "def _generate"
        def _generate(self):
            if self.model is None:
                return
  $ git show HEAD:localllm/studio.py | grep -c "load_checkpoint\|_load_saved"
  0

Zero load paths. The green half is executable and is the capability that did not
exist: load a model trained in an earlier session and get text out of it.

THE FIX, and the shape of it matters more than the lines. generate.py already
knew how to load a checkpoint - four lines buried inside its `main()`, where no
other caller could reach them. The tempting fix was to copy those four lines into
studio.py. That is precisely the mistake venv_guard.py exists in this repo to end
(section 65/67: a helper copied into a second file was correct in the first and
wrong the moment it was reused, because it had a caller baked in). So:

  localllm/checkpoint.py   NEW. load_checkpoint() and sample(), one
                           implementation. generate.py and studio.py both import
                           it; neither has its own copy of the load or the
                           sampling loop any more.
  studio.py                _load_saved() brings a model back off disk; called on
                           startup so the Sample button is live if a checkpoint
                           exists, and called again from _generate as a last
                           resort. The bare `return` is replaced by a dialog
                           naming the directory it looked in and what to do.
  generate.py              now four lines shorter and routed through the same
                           helper, so the CLI and the GUI cannot drift apart.

TWO CHECKS ADDED AT LOAD, because both were cheap and both are miserable to debug
from the symptom:
  - a checkpoint is ckpt.pt AND tokenizer.json. Half a checkpoint cannot decode
    what it generates, so `checkpoint_exists` requires both.
  - if len(tokenizer.chars) != config.vocab_size, the tokenizer and the weights
    came from different runs. That produces index errors or silent garbage; it
    now raises at load naming both numbers.

GREEN - localllm/test_checkpoint.py, 9 checks, 0 failed, run under .venv-train:
  test_checkpoint_exists_needs_both_files          test_cli_still_works
  test_empty_prompt_still_produces_text            test_load_and_sample_round_trips
  test_missing_checkpoint_names_the_directory
  test_prompt_of_unknown_characters_does_not_crash
  test_studio_and_cli_share_one_sampler
  test_studio_can_load_from_disk
  test_studio_has_no_silent_return_on_missing_model
Two of those are regression guards rather than tests of the fix:
test_cli_still_works actually runs generate.py end to end (the refactor could
have broken the documented CLI), and test_studio_and_cli_share_one_sampler fails
if either file grows its own copy of the sampling loop again.

The unknown-character test is worth naming: the tokenizer is char-level over the
~97 characters in the corpus, so a prompt typed into the GUI containing an emoji
or CJK encodes to NOTHING, and generating from an empty tensor is an error rather
than an empty answer. Both callers previously handled that with their own
`or [0]`; now one does, and it is tested rather than assumed.

WHAT I COULD NOT TEST, stated because it is the weak part. I cannot drive the Tk
GUI from this shell, so no test clicks the Sample button. What is covered is the
logic the button delegates to, plus source-level assertions that the silent
return is gone and the loader is wired into __init__. `python -c "import studio"`
runs clean and Studio._load_saved exists, which catches import-time and wiring
errors but not layout. I also did NOT add a separate "Load model" button, on
purpose: I cannot verify a layout change I cannot render, and auto-load on
startup plus auto-load on Sample already covers the reported case.

NOT INVESTIGATED: whether the 15:33 run's val_loss of 2.774 against a train_loss
of 0.088 is worth acting on. That is a 16-layer model on a 549k-character corpus
and the gap is what you would expect; it is a separate question from "it won't
talk back" and the proprietor did not ask it.

80. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-30 - the typing-import confound MEASURED at 12.8% of
    failures; then the DPO public README audited the same way as the product one.
    12/12. Two real defects, and I committed one of them while fixing the other.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE CONFOUND IS REAL AND NOW SIZED. Section 77 recorded it as a cheap pre-check
that had not been run. It cost nothing to run after all, because the 310
completions banked for section 71's red witness are oss-task completions and were
still on disk. Replayed under the pinned verifier:

  total completions   310
  failures            156
  bare typing-generic NameError:  20   (12.8% of failures, 6.5% of all)
  which names: List x10, Optional x4, Tuple x4, Union x1, Any x1

So roughly one in eight `rejected` completions drawn from the 43-tid oss pool
would be rejected for a missing `from typing import ...` and nothing else. Train
DPO on that and the model learns import hygiene; the ruler, which verifies the
same way, then rewards it. TRUE POSITIVE ON THE INSTRUMENT, FALSE POSITIVE ON THE
THESIS - the pipeline would look like it taught the model to code when it taught
it to add one import line. This is a consequence of MY pin, not an argument
against it: under 3.11 that code genuinely does not run.

It is MEASURED, not fixed. Three options exist and they are not equivalent:
  (a) accept it - it is a real correctness failure, and teaching the import is
      teaching correct Python for every interpreter before 3.14;
  (b) neutralise it by prepending typing imports to the verifier harness, which
      would ALSO have prevented the whole section 71 divergence - but it makes
      the verifier more permissive, and that re-defines ground truth again and
      invalidates the receipt and the confirmed rates;
  (c) keep it and report the next run's effect twice, with and without
      typing-NameError-only pairs, so the claim can be decomposed.
This is a decision about what the reward MEANS and it goes to the proprietor.

THE DPO PUBLIC README, audited as section 79 said it should be. 12/12 now, via
verify_dpo_claims.py (ships as dpo/verify_claims.py). TWO REAL DEFECTS:

  1. IT NEVER DISCLOSED THE INTERPRETER PIN. The page claims "edit a dataset and
     the hash stops matching, so training halts" and "there is no bypass flag",
     but since section 71 what "verified" MEANS also depends on which Python ran
     the candidates, and the receipt now records that. A reader could not know
     re-verification is interpreter-specific. Fixed, and the fix ships the
     MECHANISM rather than my numbers:
         python -c "def f(x: List[int]) -> Tuple[int,int]: return (1,2)"
     RUN: 3.14 exits 0; 3.11 -> NameError: name 'List' is not defined. Ten
     seconds, any reader, no artifacts required.

  2. THE HEADLINE HONESTY FIGURES ARE NOT REPRODUCIBLE FROM THE PUBLISHED REPO.
     The 57-run saturation and sd 0.0199 numbers rest on data/eval_history.jsonl,
     which is not in the publish whitelist. Every other number on that page can
     be re-derived from a shipped artifact; those cannot, and nothing said so.
     They now carry an explicit block stating they are taken on trust and why
     they are reported anyway - omitting them would flatter the project.

AND I COMMITTED DEFECT 2 WHILE FIXING DEFECT 1. My first draft of the pin
disclosure put "172/310 on 3.14 against 154/310 on 3.11" in bold, sourced from a
local replay that is not published either. Caught on re-read, demoted to
illustration, and replaced as evidence by the two-line reproduction above. Writing
a rule and breaking it inside the same edit is worth the space it takes to record.

FOUR OF THE CHECKER'S OWN FAILURES WERE THE CHECKER, NOT THE README. Same shape
as the product-side pass, and one was worse than anything it found:
  - layout detection preferred HERE/README.md, which in this repo is a DIFFERENT
    project's readme. Every check ran against the wrong document and reported
    failures that meant only "that text is not in this file". Source layout is
    tried first now.
  - "is it published?" tested whether the file exists ON THIS DISK, which answers
    YES for every unpublished artifact in data/ - the exact inverse of the truth
    and precisely what that check exists to catch. It reads the publish whitelist
    now, falling back to the filesystem only in the published layout where the
    two agree.
  - the interpreter-disclosure check passed because the word "interpreter"
    appeared in an unrelated sentence about PYTHONOPTIMIZE. A green mark earned
    for the wrong reason is worse than a red one.
  - a line-wrap bug: markdown wraps, so "an 8-second\ntimeout" never matched a
    regex written against raw text, and a claim plainly on the page was reported
    missing. Prose checks use a flattened copy.

THE PATTERN ACROSS SECTIONS 79 AND 80 IS THE FINDING. Across two claim-checkers,
seven defects were in the CHECKERS and three were in the READMEs. Verification
machinery is not free and is not automatically trustworthy: a checker is code, it
has bugs at the same rate as other code, and its bugs are more dangerous because a
passing check reads as evidence. Every one of these was caught only by reading the
output rather than the exit code - "0 failed" was never wrong, but "[ok] no
torch.Generator found" was.

PUBLISHED: 37a4fde (product claims), b7fac75 (DPO claims + disclosures). Both
clean through the whitelist and forbidden-term scan.

standing by

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
81. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-30 - the goal was restated and it is much larger than
    what the code targets. Ingestion generalised (shipped). And a structural
    finding: the loop is already domain-general; the domain lives in ONE function.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE PROPRIETOR RESTATED THE GOAL, twice, and it reframes both tracks:

  "the goal for the project is to be able to be trained on ANYTHING. like
   commercial and industrial purposes but from scratch on whatever machine is
   used to train whats needed not just a chatbot"

  "its why ive been so heavy on the dpo pipeline because im thinking larger than
   human interaction"

That second sentence is the one that matters architecturally. The DPO track was
never about a chat assistant; it is about a model that improves against an
OBJECTIVE CHECK rather than against human preference. Unit tests are one such
check. They are not the only one.

SHIPPED FIRST, because it was the nearest thing standing in the way of "train on
anything". start_studio.py - the double-click path, the one for people who never
open a terminal - filtered on a hardcoded EXTS = {".txt", ".md", ".py"}.

RED WITNESS, five real files judged by the old rule:
    blob.bin      accepted=False
    events.jsonl  accepted=False   <- text, wrongly refused
    notes.txt     accepted=True
    run.log       accepted=False   <- text, wrongly refused
    sensor.csv    accepted=False   <- text, wrongly refused
One of four text files got in, and the binary was excluded for the wrong reason -
its name rather than its bytes. Drop a folder of machine logs or sensor exports in
and the tool reports there is nothing to train on: the whole industrial case
excluded by a set literal, silently.

GREEN. Acceptance is decided on CONTENT now (no NUL bytes, decodes as UTF-8, >=90%
printable) and lives in ONE place both callers import. csv/jsonl/log/tsv/yaml/sql
and extensionless files accepted; blob.bin, model.pt, photo.png and an unknown
.dat carrying a NUL all refused, each with a stated reason. Three consequences of
the broader default were handled rather than discovered later: self-ingestion
(`--src .` now reaches corpus.txt and would double every document), mojibake (the
old errors="ignore" turned undecodable bytes into PERMANENT vocabulary entries in
a character-level tokenizer), and vocabulary cost (>300 distinct characters now
warns, and reports how many appear fewer than 10 times). test_ingest.py, 7 checks.
Published as 9fe21d9.

THE STRUCTURAL FINDING, which is why this section exists. Reading forge.py against
the restated goal: THE LOOP IS ALREADY DOMAIN-GENERAL. Sample K candidates, score
each against an objective check, prefer the winner, emit a preference pair, send
unsolvable tasks to a curriculum - none of that shape is about code. What is
welded to Python is four places, and three of them are formatting:

    Task.entry / Task.tests   a Python function name; assert source defining
                              run_tests()
    extract_code()            strips ```python fences
    critique()                prompts about "solutions" and "defects"
    verify()                  builds a Python harness, runs it under VERIFY_PY
                              <- the only conceptually hard one

So everything this project has spent weeks hardening - the gate, the receipt, the
interpreter pin, the frozen ruler, the band screening, the noise floor - is
VERIFIER DISCIPLINE, and it transfers wholesale to any domain with a checkable
outcome. A PLC config that must pass a schema validator, SQL that must return the
right rows, a control policy that must hold a temperature bound in a simulator:
same loop, different verify().

METHOD NOTE ON THAT FINDING: I first tried to measure it by classifying forge.py's
top-level definitions automatically, and the classifier was WRONG - it counted the
SEED_TASKS test bodies as functions named run_tests and inflated the
domain-specific list to fourteen entries. The four-places result comes from
reading verify, Task, extract_code and critique directly. Recording it because a
crude measurement that happens to support the conclusion you already reached is
the easiest kind to publish by accident.

THE OPEN DECISION, and it is why the council is being convened rather than my
picking: do I extract the verifier as an interface NOW, or build a SECOND domain
first and let the seam be discovered? My own lean is the second - designing a
plugin architecture against one instance is speculative generality, and this
codebase has a documented history of helpers that were correct in their first file
and wrong the moment they were reused (venv_guard, section 65/67). But that lean
is an instinct, not evidence.

AND THE PROPRIETOR'S ACTUAL POINT: "theres probably a implemented way this switch
has been done before." That is Step 0 of the method and I have not done it. Before
designing a seam, find out whether the seam already has a standard shape. Pluggable
execution-feedback verifiers are not an exotic idea; if a framework has converged
on an interface, adopting it beats inventing one. DIRECTIVE for activation #18 is
set above.

standing by
