86. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-31 - the two MEASUREMENTS council #18 ranked above
    more building, taken from banked data and from the verifier itself. No
    generation, no GPU, no training. Section number taken at the append: 85 was
    the tail.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 83 said to order these BEFORE the second domain and I built the domain
first. Both are cheap and both were worth having earlier; recording that the
ordering advice was right.

================================================================================
1. VERIFIER ADEQUACY - THE BIG UNMEASURED TERM NOW HAS A NUMBER
================================================================================
The complaint (section 83, F5) was exact and it stung: the instrument is precise
about the small error term and silent about the big one. Sections 61-80 spent
most of this project's measurement budget pinning a ~3pp SAMPLER noise floor to
four decimals with a CI over 40 replicates, while "how often do the tests call a
WRONG solution right" had never been measured at all. EvalPlus (arXiv 2305.01210)
extended HumanEval's tests ~80x and measured pass@k dropping up to 19.3-28.9%;
forge.SEED_TASKS carries 4-8 asserts per task, which is HumanEval's density.

MEASURED, by mutation testing (measure_test_adequacy.py). Take solutions that
ALREADY PASS -- real model outputs from data/dpo_pairs.jsonl `chosen`, so this
judges the verifier against the exact population it actually judges -- break each
one on purpose with a single small mutation, and count how many the tests still
wave through. Verifier: .venv-train 3.11.9, pinned away from the 3.14.6 launcher.

    578 mutants over 13 tasks, 3 banked solutions each, 5 operators
    (int off-by-one, comparison swap, arithmetic swap, boolean swap, negated if)

    task              asserts  mutants  killed  survived  SURVIVAL
    roman                   6       18      13         5    27.8%
    next_permutation        5       69      53        16    23.2%
    three_sum               4      116      91        25    21.6%
    spiral                  5       33      27         6    18.2%
    lps                     5       49      42         7    14.3%
    word_break              5       10       9         1    10.0%
    atoi                    8       64      59         5     7.8%
    multiply_strings        5       57      53         4     7.0%
    decode_ways             6       78      75         3     3.8%
    balanced                6       15      15         0     0.0%
    merge_intervals         5       38      38         0     0.0%
    simplify_path           5       24      24         0     0.0%
    two_sum                 4        7       7         0     0.0%

    OVERALL SURVIVAL 12.5%  (72 of 578).  Per-task mean 10.3%.

THE HONEST READING, AND IT IS AN UPPER BOUND. Equivalent mutants -- changes that
alter the source without changing behaviour on any input -- survive every
possible test suite, and detecting them is undecidable in general. No attempt is
made. So 12.5% bounds inadequacy from above; it is not a count of proven holes.

WHICH IS WHY THE EXHIBIT MATTERS MORE THAN THE RATE, and I checked one to the
bytes rather than reporting a percentage and stopping. `roman`'s two top
survivors are `int 500 -> 501` and `int 400 -> 401` -- the D and CD entries of
the numeral value table. The task's six asserts are:

    to_roman(1) == "I"          to_roman(58)   == "LVIII"
    to_roman(4) == "IV"         to_roman(1994) == "MCMXCIV"
    to_roman(9) == "IX"         to_roman(3999) == "MMMCMXCIX"

NOT ONE OF THEM CONTAINS A D OR A CD. 1994 is MCMXCIV and 3999 is MMMCMXCIX --
both skip the 400-899 range entirely. So a solution whose value table says D=501
passes all six asserts and is WRONG FOR EVERY INPUT FROM 400 TO 899. That is not
an equivalent mutant and it is not a hypothetical: it is a wrong program this
project's reward scores as correct and hands to DPO as a `chosen`.

WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT LICENSE:
  - It does NOT invalidate the ruler work. The ruler is 31 oss tasks with their
    own tests; these 13 are SEED_TASKS. Nothing here was measured on the ruler,
    because no passing completions are banked for those tids (screen_results
    stores rates, not code). The same probe on the ruler needs a generation run.
  - It does NOT make the 3pp noise floor wrong. It makes it PRECISE ABOUT A
    QUANTITY THAT ALSO CARRIES AN UP-TO-12.5% BIAS, which is a different and
    worse problem than an imprecise measurement.
  - The strict-return-type guard (forge.py:458-475) remains good work and is
    unaffected. It closes one specific reward hack. Closing one known hack is
    not test adequacy, which is exactly what section 83 said.

AND IT RETIRES A NOTE THIS REPO WROTE AGAINST ITSELF. forge.py:64-65 disables
EMIT_CONCISENESS with the comment "off until a robustness signal (mutation tests)
exists". The mutation signal now exists -- but section 83 is right that it was
scoped to the wrong problem. This is not a conciseness fix. It is a GROUND TRUTH
fix, and the conciseness channel stays off on its own merits.

================================================================================
2. THE GRADIENT ACCOUNTING - 66% OF TASK ATTEMPTS TEACH NOTHING
================================================================================
Section 83 F2, framed with a number as it asked. One pass over
data/round_stats.jsonl, 284 banked rounds:

    task attempts                         3,644
      solved (>=1 candidate passed)       3,252   89.2%
      all-fail (0/K, -> curriculum)         392   10.8%
    preference pairs emitted              1,232   33.8% of attempts

    TASKS THAT EMITTED NO PAIR AT ALL     2,412   66.2%
      because 0/K passed                    392   10.8%
      because K/K passed / no failing
      candidate to pair against           2,020   55.4%

    generations paid for (K=4)           14,576
    generations that reached a pair       2,464   16.9%

The ceiling is 50% by construction -- a pair consumes 2 of the 4 candidates -- so
against its own best case the pipeline runs at 33.8% efficiency. Either way, two
thirds of every task attempt produces nothing, and the dominant cause is not
failure but SUCCESS: 55.4% of attempts are tasks the model already solves K/K,
which have zero variance and therefore zero advantage.

That is precisely the condition DAPO's dynamic sampling exists to avoid (arXiv
2503.14476: "if all outputs of a particular prompt are correct and receive the
same reward 1, the resulting advantage for this group is zero"). This pipeline
reaches the same conclusion and pays the other price: DAPO oversamples until the
batch is full of gradient-bearing prompts, this drops the task.

NOT A DECISION, A NUMBER. Whether the answer is dynamic sampling, a harder task
pool, or moving to online GRPO is the offline-vs-online question (section 83 F2)
and it is not mine to settle. What was missing was the size of the prize, and it
is 66.2%.

================================================================================
NOT CLAIMED
================================================================================
  - The 12.5% is an UPPER BOUND on inadequacy, measured on SEED_TASKS only, with
    5 mutation operators and at most 3 solutions and 40 mutants per solution. A
    different operator set gives a different number.
  - Four tasks scored 0.0% survival. That is not proof their tests are adequate;
    it is proof these five operators could not break them, and two of those four
    (two_sum 7 mutants, balanced 15) had very few mutation sites at all.
  - Nothing here measures the RULER, which is the instrument the efficacy run
    would use. That needs banked completions for the 31 oss tids and they do not
    exist yet.
  - No fix is proposed for either finding in this section. Both are measurements.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
87. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-31 - TRACK B, on the proprietor's direction: make the
    GUI usable by non-programmers, give it a corpus that produces real output,
    stop it clipping, add dark mode, publish. Section number taken at the
    append: 86 was the tail.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This section is Track B start to finish. Nothing here touches the ruler, the
DPO pipeline or the SQL domain.

================================================================================
1. THE GUI: FIFTEEN THINGS TO TYPE, NOW NONE
================================================================================
RED WITNESS, same probe against the previous studio.py and the new one:

                                          BEFORE   AFTER
    typing boxes on the main screen          15       0
    sliders anywhere in the window            0       2
    what the graph plots at pure guessing  4.1744  65.0000

Nine of the fifteen were settings nobody can fill in without already knowing the
answer: layers, heads, embed dim, context, dropout, learn rate, eval every,
seed, top-k. Now: three sizes, three practice lengths, two sliders, and one text
box for the words you want it to continue. Every original knob still exists
under "Show advanced settings", shut by default; presets WRITE those fields, one
direction only, so the two cannot silently disagree.

THE GRAPH IS THE PART THAT MATTERS. It plotted raw cross-entropy loss, where
4.1744 is a number you need a course to read. It now plots exp(loss) - how many
characters the model is effectively still choosing between - which starts at the
size of your alphabet and falls. Same measurement, translated once, with a
dashed "pure guessing - 92 characters" reference line so the current value has
something to be compared against, and one plain sentence above it.

Time estimates are MEASURED, not invented: the three sizes are exactly the three
bench_device.py benchmarks, so "about 5 minutes" is this machine's own ms/step.
With no benchmark file the UI says the machine has not been timed rather than
guessing.

24 UI checks run against a real hidden Tk window, including a mechanical version
of the actual requirement: walk every widget and fail if anything on the main
screen expects typing.

================================================================================
2. THE CORPUS. THIS WAS THE REAL COMPLAINT AND IT WAS RIGHT
================================================================================
corpus.txt was 549,023 characters of THIS REPOSITORY'S OWN PYTHON - 13 files, 97
distinct characters, one narrow domain. The 12.66M model trained on it, prompted
"Once upon a time":

    Once upon a timefate the sat warmelleations
    import tempath
    from pathlib import Path
    import forge
    from pathlib import Path
    import math
    from pathlib import Path

No architecture change fixes that. It was a benchmark fixture standing in for
training data.

WHICH CORPUS IS NOT A MATTER OF TASTE. Eldan & Li 2023 (arXiv 2305.07759) built
TinyStories specifically to show that models of 1M-33M parameters produce fluent
English when the text is simple enough. This project's models are 3.18M and
18.9M - the exact range the paper is about.

AFTER. Same architecture, same 5 minutes, 3.18M parameters, only the corpus
changed:

    Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Timmy. Timmy loved to play
    with his toys and play with his toys. One day, Timmy's mom asked him to try
    and to help him get out of the book... His dad smiled and said, "Why don't
    you share the book with me?"

    Tom and Sara were scared. They ran to the slide and looked for a green
    piece... "Look, Tom, there is a big tree!" Sam says. "Where are the crane?"

Grammatical, correctly spelled, punctuated, with dialogue. It is NOT coherent
across paragraphs - it drifts, repeats, and swaps character names - and that is
what a 3.18M character model does. I am not claiming more than the samples show.

THE NUMBERS. 199,856,846 characters, 92 distinct after ASCII folding (123
before). Trained 44,100 steps in 329s: train 0.7161, val 0.7136. The two track
each other the whole way - the memorisation gap that made the last Track B run
worthless (train 0.088 / val 2.774) is GONE, because the corpus is 200 MB seen
about 1.1 times instead of a small one seen hundreds of times.

HOW MUCH TO DOWNLOAD IS COMPUTED, NOT GUESSED. Training reads steps x batch x
block = 44,100 x 32 x 128 = ~181M characters, so bytes past that are never read
once. The full file is 1.92 GB; the default takes the first 200 MB at a story
boundary, and the tool prints how many times the corpus is seen in a run.

VOCABULARY IS CAPACITY: every distinct character is an embedding row and an
output column, and rare glyphs cannot be learned from a handful of examples.
Text is folded to ASCII with an explicit mapping; accents decompose (cafe, not
caf) rather than dropping the word. Gutenberg licence headers are stripped
because left in they are the most repeated passage in the corpus and the model
learns to recite the terms of use.

DIAGNOSED RATHER THAN GUESSED AT. The first download looked like "no internet".
It was not: this link takes 169 SECONDS to complete the TLS handshake to
huggingface.co and then runs at 4.36 MB/s. A 60s timeout was killing it during
the handshake. Raised to 420s with the measurement in the comment.

================================================================================
3. THE THINGS THE SCREENSHOTS AND THE GUARDS CAUGHT
================================================================================
I could not have found most of these by reading the code.

  - SIZE NAMES INVISIBLE. Small/Medium/Large were gridded into the same cell as
    their descriptions and were drawn underneath them. Only visible in a
    screenshot.
  - THE BASELINE ONLY APPEARED AFTER TRAINING STARTED, so the chart opened as an
    empty 1-10 box with nothing to compare against - the exact problem the
    baseline exists to solve.
  - MY OWN DPI FIX CAUSED CLIPPING. Turning on DPI awareness scales every font
    by the display factor and I left the pixel geometry alone, so content grew
    and the window did not. Red witness across four displays: at 150% the
    content is 1474x1026 inside a 1180x820 window, and on a 1366x768 laptop the
    window ran off the bottom, taking the Start button with it. Fixed by sizing
    from the layout's own request, scaling by the same factor, clamping to the
    work area and centring - and clamping the MINIMUM too, because a minsize
    bigger than the screen cannot be resized out of. The settings column also
    scrolls now.
  - OPENING THE STUDIO HUNG. _scan_corpus ran a full leakage scan on the UI
    thread; at ~0.9 s/MB that is minutes on 200 MB and the window never
    appeared. The preview now samples the first 2 MB and says so on screen; the
    real check still runs in TrainWorker where waiting is fine.
  - IT LOADED THE WRONG MODEL. "Save to" says where the NEXT run goes; it was
    never a statement about which model you want back, so after training a
    better one the studio silently reopened the older one.

DARK MODE follows the OS setting (Windows AppsUseLightTheme), with an env
override. Two things it needed beyond a palette: the colours were scattered as
literals, which is WHY the app was light-only - there was nothing single to
change - and the vista ttk theme draws from native bitmaps and ignores
background colour, so a dark palette produced light grey panels with pale text.
Dark mode switches to clam. Tests assert the palette is actually dark AND
readable (every text colour clears the background by a luminance margin), not
merely that a dict was selected.

================================================================================
4. PUBLISHED - AND THE PUBLISH GUARDS EARNED THEIR KEEP
================================================================================
sync_public.py refused THREE times before it let anything out:

  1. FORBIDDEN TERMS: test_ingest.py carried four references to the council and
     its section numbers, from the ingestion fix earlier today. Rewritten to
     state the technical facts without the project history.
  2. FORBIDDEN TERMS again: test_studio.py quoted the proprietor directly.
  3. DANGLING REFERENCE: studio.py and verify_claims.py both name get_corpus.py,
     which was not on the whitelist - so the published repo would have imported
     a file that does not exist there. Added, with the reason, next to the same
     note checkpoint.py carries.

That is the mechanism working exactly as designed: it published NOTHING three
times rather than publishing something wrong once. Pushed as 59401e9 to the
public product repo. Only whitelisted product files went; the channel, the
council material and everything in the repo root stayed here.

19/19 README claims verified - and adding a downloader broke two of them, which
is the claim-checker doing its job. "Nothing phones home: no network module
used" became false. I did NOT exempt get_corpus.py from the scan; the check is
SPLIT into the two claims that are load-bearing and both still true: only the
declared downloader touches the network, and NOTHING anywhere sends data out -
no POST, no mail, no socket write, downloader included. That is strictly
stronger than what was checked before.

================================================================================
NOT CLAIMED
================================================================================
  - NOT claiming the model is good. It writes grammatical English sentences and
    loses the thread across paragraphs. Everything above is what the quoted
    samples show and nothing more.
  - NOT claiming the GUI is tested visually beyond three screenshots on ONE
    display at 150% scaling. The four-display fit result is arithmetic over
    measured layout sizes, not four real machines.
  - NOT claiming dark mode is right on macOS or Linux. system_wants_dark reads
    the Windows registry and falls back to light everywhere else.
  - NOT claiming the corpus is licensed for redistribution BY US - TinyStories
    is CDLA-Sharing-1.0 and Gutenberg texts are public domain; the download is
    the user's, the 200 MB is gitignored, and only the recipe is committed.
  - The hardware-check-on-install program the proprietor asked for is NOT built.
    bench_device.py already measures this machine and the GUI already degrades
    honestly when its result file is missing, so the remaining work is running
    it at install time and nothing else. Open.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
88. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-31 - TRACK B: the install-time hardware check, the
    last item the proprietor named. Section number taken at the append: 87 was
    the tail. Published as 42a92fb.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
check_my_computer.py. Run it once after installing; it checks what you have,
times a real training step on it, and says in plain words what you can train.

WHY IT IS NOT JUST bench_device.py, which already times this machine. Two things
only appear at INSTALL time, and both make that file the wrong tool:

  1. bench_device.py does `import torch` at module scope. On the one machine
     that most needs a clear answer - the one where torch is not installed - it
     dies with a traceback instead of saying which command to run.
  2. bench_device.py times against corpus.txt, and a fresh install has no
     corpus. Timing does not depend on what the text SAYS, only on tensor
     shapes, so the check builds a small synthetic text and says so in the file
     it writes ("measured_by": "check_my_computer.py (synthetic text, short
     runs)").

The timing itself is bench_device.time_steps, IMPORTED not copied, and a test
fails if the training step is ever pasted in instead. Two implementations of
"how fast is a step" would drift, and this repo has paid for a duplicated helper
twice already.

It writes the same schema and the same keys bench_device.py writes, because
studio.load_speeds() reads that file to turn its size presets into real minutes.
A second schema would be a second thing to keep in step.

RUN, on this machine:
    [ok]  Python 3.11.9
    [ok]  Graphical window support (Tk)
    [ok]  Memory 16 GB
    [ok]  Free disk space 206 GB
    [ok]  PyTorch 2.5.1+cu121
    [ok]  Graphics card NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4080   17.2 GB

    Small    0.40M numbers    5 minutes of training = 75,949 practice steps
    Medium   3.17M numbers    5 minutes of training = 45,385 practice steps
    Large   18.93M numbers    5 minutes of training = 15,259 practice steps
    Small   your graphics card is 8x faster than your processor
    Medium  your graphics card is 25x faster than your processor
    Large   your graphics card is 96x faster than your processor

45,385 steps for five minutes of Medium against the 44,100 the full benchmark
implies - a 3% difference from measuring over 40 steps instead of 200, which is
the honest cost of a check someone is waiting on and is why measured_over_steps
is recorded next to every number.

AND THE PATH THAT MATTERS, run under system Python 3.14, which has no torch:
    [!!]  PyTorch   not installed
    CANNOT TRAIN YET
    PyTorch is what actually does the training. Install it with:
        pip install torch
Exit code 1, no traceback. That is the entire reason this file exists separately.

================================================================================
A SCANNER TRIPPED OVER PROSE ABOUT ITSELF. SECOND TIME. FIXED AS A CLASS.
================================================================================
verify_claims.py's dependency check reported a third-party dependency called
`someone`. The cause: it matched `^\s*(?:import|from)\s+(\w+)` against raw file
text, and check_my_computer.py's docstring contains a line beginning "from
someone else's machine".

This is the SECOND time a scanner in this file has fired on English prose about
code - check_nothing_phones_home did it first, and its own comment records the
lesson ("the fix is a more precise pattern, not an exemption for the scanner").
A more precise regex would have been the third instance waiting to happen, so
the dependency check now PARSES the file with `ast` and collects real Import and
ImportFrom nodes. Text inside a docstring cannot be an import node, so the class
is closed rather than the instance patched.

My own test made the same mistake in the same hour: test_torch_is_imported_late
searched for the substring "import torch" before main() and fired on the
docstring explaining why torch is imported late. Fixed the same way - match a
statement, not the words.

================================================================================
PUBLISHED
================================================================================
Whitelisted and pushed as 42a92fb. 19/19 README claims verified. The README now
tells a new user to run Check My Computer first, and points at get_corpus.py for
text worth training on.

NOT CLAIMED:
  - NOT claiming the numbers are right on any machine but this one. Everything
    above is measured here; the point of the tool is that it re-measures there.
  - NOT claiming the checks are complete. It looks at Python version, Tk,
    memory, free disk, torch and CUDA. It does not check driver versions, disk
    SPEED, thermal throttling, or whether an integrated GPU would be used.
  - Memory is read through the Windows API and reports nothing on other systems
    rather than guessing.
  - NOT run on a machine with no GPU, a machine with too little disk, or an old
    Python. Those branches are exercised by reading, not by running, except the
    missing-torch one which is run for real under 3.14.

89. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-31 - the three open decisions, DECIDED, on the
    proprietor's instruction to choose for him on efficiency grounds. Section
    number taken at the append: 88 was the tail.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Efficiency read as: spend no banked data, reuse machinery already paid for, and
fix the largest MEASURED waste before the most interesting one.

================================================================================
DECISION 1 - THE TYPING CONFOUND: VERIFIER STAYS STRICT, CONFOUND GETS MEASURED
================================================================================
Options were (a) leave it, (b) prepend typing imports to the harness, (c) report
the run twice.

TAKEN: (c), and (b) is REJECTED ON THE MERITS, not only on cost. Prepending
typing imports would make ground truth MORE permissive - it would reward code
that raises NameError on every Python before 3.14 - which is the opposite of
what forge.py:108-114 already argues for pinning to 3.11 in the first place. It
would also change the runtime, invalidating the receipt and costing a re-freeze.
So the confound is not removed; it is made visible for free.

eval.py now reports `aggregate` and `aggregate_if_typing_imported` side by side,
plus `typing_nameerror_fails_total`. The gap between them IS the confound, it
costs nothing to compute, and it keeps the two causes separable without spending
a run to find out.

A CORRECTION TO WHAT I EXPECTED. I decomposed the banked dpo_pairs first,
assuming the 12.8% would show up there. It does not: 0 of 1,234 pairs carry a
typing NameError (21 are other NameErrors, 1,213 other failures). Those pairs
were built on SEED_TASKS under the OLD unpinned 3.14 verifier, where unimported
annotations are harmless. The confound lives on the RULER path under 3.11 and
nowhere else, so no banked artifact can decompose it - which is exactly why the
decomposition had to go into the instrument rather than into an analysis script.

================================================================================
DECISION 2 - THE GREEDY ANCHOR: DROPPED. IT WAS AN INSTRUMENT MISMATCH.
================================================================================
Open since section 76, and the deciding fact was not in the argument as it had
been framed. screen_tasks.rate() - which ADMITTED and CONFIRMED every ruler task
- draws every sample at `temp`, with no greedy draw at all. eval.py scored those
same tasks with 1 greedy + 4 at 0.8.

So the band that defines the ruler's validity was measured under one sampler and
checked under a different one. That is this project's own "record the
instrument" discipline being violated inside the project, and it settles the
question on correctness rather than on the 0.5150 vs 0.4905 estimand argument
from section 76 C1.

eval.py now draws all N_SAMPLES at TEMP. `greedy` stays in each row as None so
old rows remain distinguishable (ruler_noise's integrity check already reads it
as `(p.get("greedy") or 0)`, so nothing downstream breaks), and rows now carry a
`sampler` key for the same reason they carry `verifier`.

Cost: the 68 banked eval_history rows are not comparable to new ones. They
already were not - section 76 C7 measured that 0 of 68 carry the `verifier` key,
so they predate the interpreter pin too. Near-zero real cost.

================================================================================
DECISION 3 - DPO vs GRPO: STAY OFFLINE DPO. THE WASTE IS THE TASKS.
================================================================================
Section 83 F2 framed this as "you are generating GRPO groups and discarding
most of each one". The measurement (section 86) put a number on it: 66.2% of
task attempts emit no pair, and only 16.9% of paid-for generations reach one.

BUT SWITCHING TO GRPO DOES NOT FIX THAT. The dominant loss is not failure, it is
SUCCESS - 55.4% of attempts are tasks solved K/K. A group where every rollout is
correct has zero advantage under GRPO too; that is precisely DAPO's stated
motivation (arXiv 2503.14476, "a zero advantage results in no gradients"). So
adopting an online trainer - a large change, on a 16 GB card, with an 8B model
resident - would buy nothing against the thing actually costing the most.

What DOES fix it is task difficulty, and the machinery was already built and
paid for. A pair needs at least one pass AND one fail, so yield at K candidates
is 1 - p^K - (1-p)^K: large near p=0.5, near zero at either edge. ruler_frozen
.json already declares 43 tids as a training pool, admitted inside [0.2, 0.8],
disjoint from the ruler by sha256 split, with payloads on disk.

    PROJECTED from banked screen rates (mean 0.508, range 0.20-0.80): 76.6%
    MEASURED, 12 pool tasks, K=4:  11 solved, 10 pairs = 83.3%
                                   95% CI [62.2%, 100%] at n=12
    BASELINE, SEED_TASKS, 284 rounds: 1,232 pairs / 3,644 attempts = 33.8%
                                   ratio 2.46x measured, 2.27x projected

forge.py now defaults to `--tasks pool` and reads it through
build_ruler.verify_frozen(), so the freeze gate cannot be skipped on the way to
the training half. round_stats.jsonl records `task_source`, because seed and
pool rounds have different yields by construction and a file that mixes them
without saying so cannot be read afterwards.

THE FIRST RUN CAME BACK 0 SOLVED / 0 PAIRS ON 12 TASKS, and that is the useful
part of this section. Tasks screened at 0.2-0.8 do not suddenly score zero, so
the projection was not what was wrong - I was. I had rebuilt the Task objects by
hand with `tests="\n".join(...)`, producing a body of bare asserts with no
run_tests() at all, so every candidate failed for a harness reason. The correct
constructor already existed: screen_tasks.as_task(), which wraps the asserts and
binds the entry name locally because AceCode's asserts call the function by its
own global name. One implementation, reused - the same lesson venv_guard taught
in sections 65/67, and I re-derived it instead of importing it.

The 12 bogus curriculum rows and the bogus round row that run appended were
removed (failures.jsonl 405 -> 393, round_stats 285 -> 284, .bak kept for both).
Left in, they would have poisoned the "train on current failures" curriculum
with twelve tasks that were never actually hard.

================================================================================
NOT CLAIMED
================================================================================
  - The 83.3% is ONE round of 12 tasks. Its 95% interval is [62%, 100%]. It is
    consistent with the 76.6% projection and clearly above the 33.8% baseline;
    it is not a precise estimate and I am not treating it as one.
  - NOT claiming pool tasks produce BETTER pairs, only more of them. Whether a
    pair drawn from an in-band AceCode task trains as well as one from a
    hand-written seed task is unmeasured.
  - NOT claiming the typing confound is quantified. The instrument now reports
    it; no run has been made since, so the number is still section 80's 12.8%.
  - NOT re-running the ruler. Dropping the greedy anchor changes the eval
    instrument, so the next efficacy run must be greedy-free on BOTH arms - and
    no arm has been run since the change.
  - The frozen ruler is untouched: no task field renamed, no re-freeze, the
    6,200 banked generations stand. The `sampler` key added to cmd_freeze in
    843557c still has not been written to data/ruler_frozen.json, which needs a
    --force re-freeze and remains the proprietor's call.

90. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-31 - the ruler RE-FROZEN with --force on the
    proprietor's authorisation, and the generation slider retuned against a
    measurement. Section number taken at the append: 89 was the tail.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
1. THE RULER WAS RE-FROZEN. ANNOUNCED HERE, AS build_ruler REQUIRES.
================================================================================
`python build_ruler.py freeze --force`, run to write the sampler pin added in
843557c into data/ruler_frozen.json. cmd_freeze refuses to replace a frozen
ruler without --force and its message says to say so in the channel; this is
that.

WHAT CHANGED: one key.

    "sampler": {"temperature": 0.8, "confirm_n": 60}

WHAT DID NOT CHANGE, checked rather than assumed. The pre-freeze file was copied
to data/ruler_frozen.BEFORE_REFREEZE.json and compared field by field:

    ruler_set_sha256 unchanged : True
      74560a4c514587c1ab9094a15cbce960d0838759bc4b481aae2b01f637e8413d
    all 31 task hashes match   : True  (31/31)
    n_ruler / n_training_pool  : unchanged (31 / 43)
    confirmed_rates            : unchanged, all 31
    frozen_at                  : 2026-07-30T04:18:58 -> 2026-07-31T18:17:12

So the RULER'S IDENTITY IS THE SAME OBJECT. That is the whole reason this was
safe to do: ruler_set_sha256 is computed over task hashes only, and `sampler` is
top-level provenance that no hash covers. The 40 banked replicates - 31 tasks x
5 samples x 40 = 6,200 generations - remain replicates of the same instrument
and were not spent.

build_ruler.verify_frozen() passes on the new file, and tests/test_freeze_guard.py
is 6/6. The freeze also printed no "ruler_noise.jsonl absent" warning, which is
the section-84 fix behaving correctly on a real run rather than only in a
fixture.

Every rate in the frozen artifact is now interpretable without reference to any
other file: it says which model, which band, which sampler, and which tasks.

================================================================================
2. THE "WILD" SLIDER STOP WAS PAST THE CLIFF. MEASURED, THEN MOVED.
================================================================================
Proprietor's report, on the trained stories model: "the write me a story actually
works. its really wonky at wild settings and 400 words but it actually works."

Both halves are correct and the second half is measurable. Counting words that
appear NOWHERE in 20 MB of the training corpus (11,122 distinct real words), on
the 3.18M model, samples of 700 characters:

    temp 0.35  top_k  10     0.0%  invented words
    temp 0.60  top_k  25     0.2%
    temp 0.80  top_k  40     0.9%
    temp 1.05  top_k  80     0.9 - 1.6%
    temp 1.10  top_k 100     1.7%
    temp 1.15  top_k 120     1.9%
    temp 1.20  top_k 150     2.1%
    temp 1.30  top_k 200     6.6 - 7.8%     <- the shipped top stop

Degradation is GRADUAL to 1.20 and then TRIPLES at 1.30. That is not a matter of
taste, it is a cliff, and the shipped slider put its last stop on the far side of
it. Sample at 1.30, verbatim:

    "the puppy needed to walk in the regular dtest. The drum bowed on too fast
     plate becauset he found the wunny and let Sam draw some flowers. Then Sam
     snawhered..."

DECIDED: the top stop moves 1.30/200 -> 1.20/150. Still visibly loose - 2.1% is
more than double Balanced - without the 3x jump into non-words. The five stops
and their names are unchanged.

AND THE SLIDER NOW SAYS WHAT EACH STOP COSTS. A stop labelled "Wild" tells a
non-programmer nothing about what they will get, which is the same complaint the
loss curve had. Each stop now shows its consequence in words - the loosest one
reads "expect invented words and wandering sentences" - so the choice is
informed rather than a dare. A test fails if any stop has no description, or if
the loosest one stops warning.

The percentages are NOT printed in the UI, deliberately. They belong to this
model and this corpus and would be a stale claim on anyone else's; they live in
a comment next to the table they justify.

================================================================================
NOT CLAIMED
================================================================================
  - The invented-word rate is a proxy for "wonky", not a definition of it. It
    counts words absent from the training corpus; it says nothing about whether
    the sentences mean anything, and at every setting this model still wanders
    across paragraphs.
  - Measured on ONE model (3.18M, stories corpus, 44,100 steps) with 3-4 samples
    per setting. A larger model or a different corpus will have a different
    cliff, and the top stop was chosen for this one.
  - The re-freeze does NOT re-measure anything. confirmed_rate is still the
    number cmd_confirm produced at n=60; nothing was re-run against the model.
  - Still open and still the proprietor's: whether to run the next efficacy arm
    now that eval.py is greedy-free on both arms.

91. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-08-02 - dpo_pairs.jsonl had been UNVERIFIABLE since
    section 89, found by running the gate rather than reading it. Section
    number taken at the append: 90 was the tail.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
1. THE RED. THE GATE COULD NOT VERIFY ITS OWN DATASET.
================================================================================
`python verify_dataset.py`, run before any edit:

    FAIL: 1269/1279 pairs valid
      violation types: {'no_matching_task': 10}
        forge[1234] tid=ace_oss_11981: no_matching_task
        ... rows 1234-1243, all ace_oss_*

CAUSE, and it is one line. verify_dataset.py:40 was

    TASKS = {t.tid: t for t in forge.SEED_TASKS}

so the gate could only verify pairs drawn from the hand-written seed tasks.
Section 89 measured pair yield on the AceCode TRAINING POOL - "12 pool tasks,
K=4: 11 solved, 10 pairs" - and those 10 ace_oss pairs were appended to
data/dpo_pairs.jsonl and committed in 732e08e. The gate was not re-run. From
that commit the file was unverifiable IN PRINCIPLE, not merely stale: the task
definitions were never in the lookup, so re-running could not clear it.

The committed receipt still described the pre-append state - 1269 pairs / 2190
rows / 0 violations - written back at 010b489.

WHAT THE GATE GOT RIGHT, checked rather than assumed. require_verified demands
violations == 0 AND a hash match, per file, for the paths passed:

    data/dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl  -> ALLOWED   (918 pairs, run-1's input)
    data/dpo_pairs.jsonl         -> REFUSED   (10 violations)

Run 1 was never at risk; train_native.py:102 reads the capped file. The
--uncapped path (train_native.py:104) was refused, correctly. Before this run it
would have been refused on the hash mismatch instead. So this is not a hole in
the gate - it is a gated artifact changed without the gate being re-run.

A SECOND, SMALLER DEFECT. The refusal message ends "Fix by re-running: python
verify_dataset.py", which in this case reproduced the same 10 violations
forever. The remediation was not actionable.

================================================================================
2. THE FIX, AND WHY IT IS NOT "BACK THE 10 ROWS OUT"
================================================================================
Backing them out clears today's red and guarantees its return. Section 89's own
finding is that pool tasks yield 2.46x more pairs per generation than
SEED_TASKS, i.e. the pool is meant to become the MAIN source of pairs. A
verifier that cannot see the pool is a hole that reopens on the next generation
run. So the class is closed instead of the instance.

verify_dataset.load_tasks() now builds tasks from SEED_TASKS plus the SCREENED
payloads in data/screen_results.jsonl, via screen_tasks.as_task() - the same
function that built these tasks when they were screened, not a second
implementation of the entry-point binding.

CORRECTION TO SOMETHING I SAID EARLIER THIS SESSION: I stated the pool task
definitions live in data/ruler_frozen.json under `training_pool`. They do not.
That key is a list of bare tid STRINGS; the frozen file stores hashes only. The
payloads (tid / entry / prompt / tests) are in data/screen_results.jsonl.

Two ways to get a silently wrong verdict are refused rather than resolved by
precedence: a tid re-screened with different bytes, and a screened tid that
shadows a seed tid. Either would make "verified" depend on row order or on which
dict won.

================================================================================
3. SWEEPING THE CONSUMER: THE RECEIPT NOW PINS THE TASK SOURCE
================================================================================
"0 violations" is a statement about verify() run against A SET OF TASKS. While
that set was SEED_TASKS - a literal inside forge.py - the forge.py hash covered
it for free. It no longer does. Leaving the fingerprint alone would let the
tests a pair is judged against change while every recorded hash still matched,
which is the exact silent-weakening the VERIFIER_FILES block was written to stop.

    TASK_SOURCE_FILES = ("screen_tasks.py", "data/screen_results.jsonl")
    SCHEMA 4 -> 5

Whole files, following the rule already written at dataset_gate.py:58-66 rather
than a new one: hashing "just the payloads we used" is hand-picking which bytes
are semantic. The accepted price is unchanged in kind - screening a new task now
invalidates the receipt and forces a re-verify.

================================================================================
4. THE GREEN, AND THE CONTROL
================================================================================
Same command that produced the red:

    dpo_pairs.jsonl           1244 rows  0 violations
    dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl     918 rows  0 violations
    repair_pairs.jsonl          38 rows  0 violations
    PASS: 1279/1279 pairs valid          [exit 0]

The 10 ace_oss pairs verify for real - chosen passes its tests, rejected fails
them. Both gate paths now ALLOWED, receipt schema 5, fingerprint carrying
forge.py + screen_tasks.py + data/screen_results.jsonl + interpreter 3.11.9.

A NEW FINGERPRINT ENTRY THAT IS NEVER CHECKED IS DECORATION, so it was attacked
in a temp directory, no repo file touched:

    honest fingerprint          -> ALLOWED  (expected)
    task-source hash tampered   -> REFUSED  "the VERIFIER changed since this
                                             data was verified"
    screen_tasks.py tampered    -> REFUSED  same

Regression sweep: the six files in tests/ are 0 failed each, and
test_gate_coverage.py passes (19 modules scanned, 5 exempt, 2 routed).

================================================================================
5. A TESTING TRAP WORTH RECORDING
================================================================================
tests/test_verifier_pin.py must be launched from the UNPINNED interpreter. Run
under .venv-train it reports 2 of 7 red - "both children ran the same Python
(3.11.9); test is blind" - because it compares sys.executable against VENV_PY
and they are then the same. Under C:\Python314 it is 7/7. That is the control
refusing to report a pass it cannot back, which is right, but nothing in the
repo says which interpreter to use and the obvious invocation is the wrong one.
Recorded, not fixed.

================================================================================
NOT CLAIMED
================================================================================
  - NOT claiming the pool-derived pairs are GOOD training data. They are now
    verifiable and they verify; whether a pair drawn from an AceCode pool task
    trains as well as one from a seed task is untouched by this and remains
    open from section 89.
  - NOT a sweep of every dataset file. Three files were verified by name -
    dpo_pairs.jsonl, dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl, repair_pairs.jsonl - which is what
    FILES lists. Any pair file not in that dict is not covered by anything here.
  - The task-source pin covers screen_tasks.py and screen_results.jsonl as
    WHOLE FILES. It does not know which payloads were actually used, so it will
    fire on screening runs that changed nothing relevant. Accepted, per the
    rule it follows.
  - NOT re-run: nothing about the model, the ruler, or any training. This was
    the data gate only.
  - The receipt remains unsigned. Unchanged by this work.

92. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-08-02 - TRACK B, on the proprietor's direction: "build
    an installer exe for the github". The shipped launchers turned out to be
    broken for every user who is not this machine. Section number taken at the
    append: 91 was the tail.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
1. THE RED, AND IT WAS IN THE PUBLISHED PRODUCT
================================================================================
Before writing an installer I ran what we already ship. A fresh clone was
simulated by copying exactly the PUBLISH whitelist into a temp folder (32 files,
none missing) and double-clicking the launcher:

    --- Train My AI.bat ---
    stderr: The system cannot find the path specified.
    exit 0

Both launchers hardcoded "%~dp0..\.venv-train\Scripts\python.exe" - a virtual
environment that exists one level above THIS repo and nowhere in a clone. So the
published product could not start for anyone.

The second half is worse than the first. It EXITED 0 while failing, because
`if errorlevel 1 pause` swallowed the code. Section 80's lesson again, in the
shipped artifact this time: a command that exits 0 while printing a false
statement is what an exit-code check cannot see.

FIXED. Each launcher now looks for .venv beside it, then the development
environment above it, and if neither is there says so in plain words and exits
1. Green witness, same fresh clone:

    exit 1
    This app is not set up on this computer yet.
      Run INSTALL.bat in this folder first.

Check My Computer.bat deliberately falls back further - to any Python on PATH -
because section 88's whole design is that it runs BEFORE anything is installed
and reports "PyTorch is not installed, here is the command" instead of dying.

================================================================================
2. install.py - THE HARDWARE-AWARE PART
================================================================================
Stdlib only, for the same reason check_my_computer.py is: it is the first thing
that runs on a computer where nothing exists, so importing torch would kill it
on exactly the machine it exists to help.

It detects the graphics card via nvidia-smi (not via torch, which is not
installed yet), picks the CUDA or the processor-only wheel index, builds .venv
beside the app, installs PyTorch, then runs check_my_computer.py - the EXISTING
check, so setup is confirmed by the same tool the user runs later, and because
it writes bench_device_result.json which the studio needs for real minutes.

================================================================================
3. THE FAILURE THAT JUSTIFIED THE WHOLE FILE: PYTHON 3.14
================================================================================
First real run, under the system Python:

    Looking in indexes: https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu121
    ERROR: Could not find a version that satisfies the requirement torch
           (from versions: none)
    SETUP STOPPED: installing PyTorch failed (code 1).

PyTorch publishes NO wheels for Python 3.14, and 3.14 is what python.org serves
as the current download today. A new user doing everything right lands on the
one version that cannot work. My MIN_PYTHON check was necessary and not
sufficient - there is a maximum too, and it moves.

NOT FIXED WITH A VERSION TABLE. A hardcoded "3.10 to 3.13" is wrong the moment
PyTorch ships 3.14 wheels and nothing would tell us. install.py now enumerates
every Python on the machine (py -0p), ASKS the real index which ones have a
torch, and uses the newest that does. Measured:

    3.14: torch available = False   (1.1s)
    3.12: torch available = True    (1.3s)
    3.11: torch available = True    (2.2s)

MY OWN MACHINERY BRED THE NEXT BUG, and it is recorded rather than quietly
fixed. The first probe used `pip install --dry-run`. --dry-run still RESOLVES,
which for torch means walking the CUDA dependency tree and pulling wheels for
metadata: caught mid-run with the pip cache at 3.0 GB and one probe still
unfinished. A yes/no question must not cost gigabytes per interpreter. Replaced
with `pip index versions`, which fetches one index page and filters by the
interpreter's own tags - the timings above are the after.

================================================================================
4. THE EXE
================================================================================
"Install Train My AI.exe", 9 KB, PE32 console binary. Built from installer/
Bootstrap.cs by installer/build.ps1 using csc.exe, which is PART OF WINDOWS - no
build toolchain to install, and no ~10 MB bundled interpreter. There is no
PyInstaller, Inno Setup or NSIS on this machine and none was added.

It is THIN on purpose: download the repo zip from GitHub, unpack, find a Python,
hand over to install.py. Every judgement - which wheel, which Python versions
exist, what to say when none do - stays in install.py, in one place, tested, and
readable by the user who just downloaded it.

RUN, for real, against the live public repo:

    [1/3] Looking for Python        found: py

                                    installed to C:\Users\t\Train My AI

      SETUP STOPPED: install.py is missing from the download.

That is the CORRECT result and the last mile is blocked on a publish: the public
repo does not contain install.py yet, because publishing has not been asked for.
Download, TLS, unpack, copy and the error path are all confirmed against real

================================================================================
5. PUBLISH SAFETY, CHECKED BEFORE ASKING
================================================================================
install.py and INSTALL.bat are added to the PUBLISH whitelist - shipping
launchers that say "run INSTALL.bat" without shipping it would point a new user
at a file that is not there. All four new/changed shipped files scan CLEAN

verify_claims.py caught a real gap I introduced: install.py shipped without a
row in the README file table, 18/19. Documented, back to 19/19. The product
suite is 0 failed across its six test files.

  - PENDING, and the word is used deliberately: the full install has NOT been
    watched to completion. The 2.4 GB cu121 download for cp312 was still

    3.12 correctly selected, venv built, the right wheel started downloading.

  - NOT claiming the CUDA 12.1 pin is right for every card. It is what this

93. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-08-02 - the forge's real state, found by reading
    artifacts instead of notes: RUN 1 ALREADY HAPPENED and was scored on a
    saturated instrument. Prereg signed, template skew measured, re-score in
    flight. Section number taken at the append: 92 was the tail.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
1. THE NOTES WERE WRONG. RUN 1 EXISTS.
================================================================================
OPEN-ITEMS.md says "Nothing has run on the real 8B base, no held-out evaluation
has been scored" and, separately, that export_adapter.py is "only exercised
against a 0.5B stand-in; the real 8B base is untested on this path".

Both are false, and the artifacts say so:

    dpo_adapter_native/run_meta.json
      base   : unsloth/llama-3-8b-Instruct-bnb-4bit     <- the real 8B
      pairs  : 918                                      <- run-1's own input
      epochs : 1.0   lr 5e-06   beta 0.1   rpo_alpha 1.0
    adapter_model.safetensors  167 MB, 2026-07-28
    ollama: llama3-forged, llama3-forged-null, llama3-forged-amplified

A real DPO run on the real base, exported with its null baseline and the
amplified adapter-is-live control, five days ago.

WHY IT PRODUCED NOTHING. data/eval_history.jsonl, 10 rows, 2026-07-28:

    task_set = held_out    n_tasks = 10    n_samples = 5    temp = 0.8
    llama3-forged       pass@1: 0.88 0.90 0.88 0.88 0.86   mean 0.880
    llama3-forged-null  pass@1: 0.90 0.90 0.88 0.88 0.88   mean 0.888
    pass@3: 0.9 on all ten runs, BOTH arms, without exception

That is the OLD 10-task bank, the one section 64 recorded as saturated
(pass@20 == pass@3 == 0.9000). Every pass@3 pinned at the ceiling; the trained
arm fractionally BELOW null on pass@1. The instrument could not resolve a
difference, so the adapter has never been measured.

So "no efficacy result" is right and "nothing has run" is wrong. The correction
matters because the two imply completely different next moves: one says train,
the other says re-score what already exists.

A TRAP AVOIDED, recorded because the next session will meet it too. `python
eval.py <tag>` scores eval.py's hardcoded HELD_OUT list - the OLD 10 tasks -
and has no flag for the frozen 31-task ruler. Running it would have repeated
2026-07-28 exactly. ruler_noise.py is the tool that obtains the ruler through
build_ruler.verify_frozen().

================================================================================
2. PREREG SIGNED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS SCORED
================================================================================
data/prereg_track_a_run1.json, committed in 809d7ec BEFORE the first replicate.
Closes the OPEN-ITEMS line "No Track A prereg". Every hash derived from the live
artifacts, not typed: ruler set 74560a4c, adapter f416f3f9, capped dataset
85fc0bdc (918 pairs, 0 violations), plus the receipt's verifier fingerprint.

Design: trained llama3-forged vs null llama3-forged-null, 40 replicates per arm,
greedy-free run-level pass@1, Welch on each arm's own sd, FIXED N, no peeking,
no early stop on effect. Recorded as what it cannot show: one checkpoint has
zero training-seed variance by construction, so it cannot support a claim about
DPO at any k.

================================================================================
3. TEMPLATE SKEW: MEASURED, AND IT DOES NOT REPRODUCE
================================================================================
The sponsor handoff (already ruled on in 73b37f8: items 1-3 adopted, the rest
declined, the workbench NOT adopted as a second decision record) carries
research seed 01, whose premise is a real event - the block labelled
`# file: localllm/train.py` was 86.3% of the old corpus and was this
generator's candidate stream.

build_training_set.py has always said its cap is "not the fix for template
skew". Nobody measured the skew. Before scoring an adapter trained on these 918
pairs, that had to be known: a repetitive bank makes a null result unreadable,
because it cannot separate "DPO did not work" from "one program renamed nine
hundred times".

Exact de-duplication cannot see renaming, so identifiers are canonicalised
before hashing. CONTROL FIRST: the by-task row reproduces build_training_set's
own published 9.93 effective tasks to the digit.

    chosen structures      273 clusters   effective  90.4   largest  9.8%
    rejected structures    632 clusters   effective 441.6   largest  4.2%
    (chosen,rejected)      767 clusters   effective 666.4   largest  1.4%

DPO trains on the CONTRAST, and the contrast is diverse: 767 distinct shapes
over 918 pairs, largest 1.4%. The chosen side looks concentrated only because 13
tasks have converging correct solutions, which is what correctness means; the
variety sits on the rejected side, which is where the signal needs it.

The bank's real limit is the one already named: 13 tasks, effective 9.93. That
is task BREADTH. It bounds what any result may claim; it does not make one
unreadable. Section 89's pool work (2.46x pair yield) is the lever for it.

================================================================================
4. STATUS OF THE RE-SCORE - IN FLIGHT, NOT A RESULT
================================================================================
151 seconds per replicate, 155 generations each (31 tasks x 5 samples). First
launch was KILLED at replicate 29/40 of the trained arm; 28 banked. Nothing was
lost - ruler_noise appends per replicate and --runs counts banked ones, so the
resume does only the remainder.

NO AGGREGATE HAS BEEN COMPUTED. The prereg fixed N at 40 per arm with no
peeking, and a partial-arm mean is precisely what that rule exists to stop.
The trained arm's mean will not be calculated until both arms are complete.

================================================================================
NOT CLAIMED
================================================================================
  - NOT a result. The re-score is running. Anything about whether the adapter
    beats its null is unmeasured as of this section.
  - The concentration probe is a FLASHLIGHT: structural identity misses
    behavioural duplicates with different shapes and splits one algorithm
    written two ways. It is a lower bound on repetition, not its full extent.
    No behavioural probes were built.
  - NOT claiming the 2026-07-28 adapter is the best recipe. It is the one that
    exists; nothing has swept lr, beta or rpo_alpha.
  - The 50 banked base-model replicates are the BASE, not the null arm. They
    are a free third reference point, not a substitute for llama3-forged-null,
    which controls for the export path.
  - Read scope on the handoff package: 00-OPEN-THIS-FIRST.md and research seed
    01 at the byte, plus the directory listing and the 73b37f8 ruling. The two
    DoRs, the 53-card workbench and the companions were NOT opened this pass.
